## 2026-11-11 -- Avoid a restricted Cursor Chat workspace flag

Cursor Chat no longer requests a workspace-context exclusion that the provider can reject for an account, team or model. Its workspace remains the private turn folder containing the permission configuration and current message image copies. Cursor backend refusals now appear as one plain sentence naming Cursor rather than exposing retry messages and a raw exception label. Agent tabs and read-only reviewers retain their existing behavior.

`server/src/providers/cursor/chatSandbox.ts` removes `--exclude-workspace-context` from both Chat arguments and the parser capability probe. The empty `--allowed-tools` list, explicit Read, Write, Shell, WebFetch and MCP denies, disabled indexing and disabled codebase reference remain. Documents still arrive as extracted text and fitted images still use explicit `--image` inputs. `server/src/providers/cursor/print.ts` recognizes structured account, team and model refusal messages on failed Chat exits and sends their reason as a short Cursor sentence. Other failures retain the existing error handling.

The first regression found the privileged flag in the generated parser arguments. The second reproduced the raw retry dump shown for a backend refusal. Thirty-four focused tests passed on Linux, covering both corrections alongside attachments, cleanup, cancellation, ACP streaming and reviewer isolation. Build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit passed. The preceding installed run failed before any model answer because the backend disallowed workspace exclusion; parser acceptance alone did not establish permission to use it.

Remaining: one approved installed real Chat turn must check relative, full-path and symlink refusals, an attached document and an attached image. Any further backend control refusal stops acceptance without removing that control or submitting another turn. Operator acceptance and real macOS and Windows checks remain pending.

## 2026-11-11 -- Expose configured tool servers on every Agent provider

Agent tabs can now manage configured tool servers from CONFIG regardless of the selected provider. Cursor, Ollama, llama.cpp, LM Studio, Strata and DeepSeek tabs show the same MCP SERVERS section already available on Claude and Codex. Chat tabs and isolated cluster members hide the section because configured servers are not attached there. Standard and BLIND cluster members retain it wherever that provider supports cluster membership.

`client/src/components/RightPanel/ConfigTab.tsx` replaces the Claude/Codex visibility gate with the existing attachment boundary: a non-Chat tab outside isolated membership, with MCP senders available. Registry definitions remain machine-global, while server enablement and dangerous-tool choices remain per tab. No server transport or tool permissions change. `docs/PROVIDERS.md`, the configuration manual and `rightpanel.config.mcp` contextual help explain the provider coverage and shared-list boundary.

Checked on Linux with ten focused component tests passing, none skipped. The tests render all eight providers, both Claude and Codex transports, supported access-mode visibility and Chat exclusion. The new assertions failed against the original gate. Build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit passed. The manual paragraph check initially rejected an overlong paragraph; splitting the explanation resolved it. Component rendering requires development-mode React; clearing the inherited production setting made the DOM test harness usable.

In the built program with a temporary home, an Ollama tab added a synthetic server through CONFIG. The entry was saved in the registry and listed again after reload. No model turn, probe or model load was requested.

Remaining: Director review and operator acceptance in a try build. No macOS or Windows hardware acceptance is claimed.

## 2026-11-11 -- Confine Cursor Chat to message attachments

Cursor Chat can receive extracted document text and fitted image inputs without granting its agent access to computer files. Each turn uses a fresh private folder under the selected Arco home. Completion, startup failure and cancellation remove that folder. Server startup removes abandoned turn folders without following symlinks; cleanup errors warn once and allow startup. Cursor Chat already shows NO WEB and offers no web toggle. Agent ACP sessions and read-only reviewers retain their existing project access.

`server/src/providers/cursor/chatSandbox.ts` creates the project permission configuration and prepared image copies. Read, Write, Shell, WebFetch and MCP permissions are explicitly denied. An empty `--allowed-tools` list requests no built-in tools, including directory listing, browser and subagents. Workspace context, indexing and codebase references are disabled. Images use `--image`; no attachment path becomes a Read instruction. Unsupported controls refuse Chat, and unsupported image inputs receive a plain refusal. Internal flags are checked with a deliberate parser error before any model session.

The regression first demonstrated that Chat started inside its existing conversation folder. Thirty-three focused provider tests passed on Linux, including attachment preparation, isolation, simultaneous folders, refusal, cancellation, ACP streaming and reviewer plugins. Build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit passed. Installed parser checks caught an invalid project configuration key, which was removed. An initial help-based capability probe accepted unknown flags; requiring the exact sentinel error fixed that false positive. Cancellation during probing initially left a folder pending a second probe; abort propagation resolved it. Installed permission checks blocked relative, absolute and symlink reads with read-control gating both enabled and disabled.

Remaining: Director review, the approved installed Cursor Chat turn and operator acceptance. Image delivery through the internal input flag and complete Chat behavior still require that real turn. Real macOS and Windows acceptance remains pending.

## 2026-11-11 -- Keep the source of a handoff available in the workbench

Successful Handoff and Spawn operations now retain a source link beside the seed and in HANDOFFS. Only the latest seed shows the current source. A link-write failure leaves the handoff usable without a source control. The From control opens or reopens the earlier conversation in HISTORY at its recorded event boundary. Show full history removes that display filter. Closing the source tab preserves access; deletion or archival makes its retained link unavailable. A chain carries references to earlier sources even when an intermediate conversation disappears. In-place rotation records the earlier portion of the same workflow.

`server/src/handoffHistory.ts` captures server-owned references before reflection and saves them after successful export. `WorkflowMeta.handoffHistory` remains separate from Task parentage. Historical Spawn inherits only references already granted at its cutoff. The authenticated `/api/handoff-sources` route resolves opaque source keys, checks the caller and source again, and refuses symlinked state components. Chat, incognito, BLIND and ISOLATED conversations receive no links. The provider guide, architecture reference, Handoff manual and source contextual help describe these controls.

Focused history, metadata, store, routing and projection tests passed on Linux: 35 passed, none skipped. Build, typecheck and documentation checks also passed. The initial regression failed because the source-reference implementation was absent. An existing suite then refused the default test home; explicit isolated HOME and ARCO_HOME resolved that setup failure. Typechecking found a missing saved-source directory argument and an unavailable manual markup helper; the directory argument and standard emphasis markup corrected both.

Remaining: dedicated agent retrieval, persistent provider announcements and approved real-agent acceptance follow in separate changes. This change does not announce an unattached tool. Operator try-build acceptance and real macOS and Windows checks remain pending.

## 2026-11-11 -- Load Cursor reviewer plugins from nested temporary folders

Cursor read-only reviewers can now register their generated file and plan plugin when the operating system supplies a shallow temporary folder. Each run has a private parent and a nested plugin directory. Ending one run removes its own parent while leaving other active runs intact. The plugin still exposes only the existing read-only file tools and, with a workflow identity, the three plan readers.

`server/src/providers/cursor/filePlugin.ts` creates the nested directory before writing the manifest and MCP configuration. The installed Cursor registration method rejects canonical plugin paths with fewer than three nonempty components. A generated folder immediately under the Linux temporary root fails that check; its nested child passes. No approval flag or project configuration is added. ACP uses its existing in-process MCP transport, and Chat omits this project plugin. The provider guide, Cursor manual and Review contextual help describe the temporary plugin lifetime.

The regression first failed on canonical path depth. Focused tests cover generated manifests, workflow identity, read-only plan calls, simultaneous runs and removal of the complete private parent. An initial layout test lacked the fresh clone's built output; building before repeating that test supplies its required fixture. Fourteen focused tests passed on Linux, with none skipped. Build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit passed. The documentation paragraph limit initially failed after adding lifetime guidance; splitting the paragraph resolved it. A no-model check against the installed registration method accepted the actual generated plugin and parsed both server entries.

Remaining: Director diff review, an installed Cursor print review with observed plan API calls, and operator acceptance. Real macOS and Windows checks remain pending; no portability acceptance is inferred from temporary-path conventions.

## 2026-11-11 -- Wrap top-bar controls before they overlap

The top bar now wraps its controls before provider status dots can cover the layout switch. The wordmark, layout choice, provider indicators, feature menus, voice controls, notification badge, version chip and power action retain their existing order and behavior. Wrapping responds to the space those controls need rather than waiting for one window-width threshold. Fixed layout choices still permit the header to wrap.

`SystemHeader.tsx` allows both flex groups to wrap at every width. The controls group retains its natural width until it must move onto another row, with its maximum width bounded by the bar. This prevents the group from shrinking beneath its children and spilling into the wordmark group. Repository client documentation, the top-header manual section and layout-switch contextual help describe the wrapping behavior. The earlier resize-grip correction affects separate dialog controls and is unrelated.

Checked on Linux with the top-bar browser suite: four layout modes, VOICE visible and hidden, three interface scales, and 66 viewport widths from 2000 through 700 pixels, totaling 1584 geometry checks. All four provider dots, a notification badge and a release-candidate version chip are present. Existing menu and provider-popover checks remain. Build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit passed. The new browser assertion first failed because a provider dot intersected the layout switch at 900 pixels; the flex correction resolved it. An incomplete version-response fixture was corrected to require the full chip text.

Remaining: operator acceptance in a try build. No macOS or Windows hardware acceptance is claimed.

Cursor Chat can now start a reply on Cursor versions that reject partial-output streaming with plain-text output. The Chat adapter keeps the existing text decoder and transcript replay. The new argument assertion failed on the actual spawned Chat argument list before the change. Agents can use Arco's built-in tool servers when a compiled server starts without `NODE_ENV=production`, including the documented manual and reverse-proxy launch commands. Source development continues to run the TypeScript tool servers. Cursor's file and plan readers follow the same rule, so starting the compiled server with a development environment does not point those readers at missing source files.

`server/src/mcp/mcpConfigWriter.ts` chooses the cluster, workflow plan and voice server entries from the running module's file extension. `server/src/providers/cursor/filePlugin.ts` does the same for its file and plan reader entries. `docs/SERVICE_MODE.md` explains the behavior beside both manual launch examples. Packaged and service launch commands continue to work as before, and no setting or control was added.

A real Claude CLI turn on the compiled server with `NODE_ENV` unset called `read_file` and `plan_list` and returned the expected synthetic document counts and plan title. Regression tests first selected nonexistent TypeScript files from the built output; using the running module's extension corrected the selection. An initial focused run also refused the default Arco home; a temporary test home resolved that setup failure.

## 2026-11-11 -- Keep model image fitting within source dimensions

JPEG uploads that already fit the model image limits no longer acquire an enlarged model-copy sibling when an optional image converter is installed. Model fitting preserves the source pixel bounds, including when automatic orientation swaps the image axes. Upload storage and its existing replacement rules are unchanged.

`server/src/fileContent/externalImage.ts` bounds the ImageMagick JPEG decoder hint by each source dimension before applying the shrink-only resize. It validates probed dimensions and rejects converted output whose longer or shorter edge exceeds the corresponding source edge. The optional `sips` target is also capped at the source long edge. Rejected acceleration falls back to the bundled decoder. The provider guide, Chat manual and attachment contextual help explain the model-copy limits.

Checked on Linux with 31 focused attachment, accelerator, portable-codec, upload and HTTP router tests passing, none skipped, plus build, typecheck, public tree audit and documentation checks. New regressions first reproduced an unnecessary sibling and a 1600x1200 JPEG enlarged to 2000x1500. A square decoder hint still enlarged the shorter dimension; bounding both hint dimensions resolved it. A synthetic converter test confirms rejection of enlarged output while allowing rotation and downscaling. The accelerator defect predates upload shrinking.

Remaining: repeat installed-provider acceptance and operator testing before the final release gate. Real macOS and Windows acceptance remains pending; no provider inference or credential access was used for these checks.

## 2026-11-11 -- Keep browser fixture requests inside their test

Browser smoke results now fail when the fixture Vite server reports a refused connection to the default backend. The file-selection browser fixture handles session creation inside its own intercepted API routes, so its checks do not depend on a separate running server. A successful browser assertion can no longer conceal that particular backend connection failure.

`scripts/smoke/run-browser-suites.mjs` collects each Vite process output in a temporary file while its associated Python suite runs. This avoids a full pipe blocking the server, preserves output for diagnostics, and checks the complete output after shutdown, including errors split across writes. `browser-suite-retry.mjs` identifies the default loopback backend refusal without matching other ports. `file-selection-browser.py` blocks service workers, registers its API route on the browser context, and asserts session requests reached the fixture. The contributor guide describes the runner behavior.

On Linux, the focused browser runner tests passed, including a split-output refusal and an otherwise successful suite, and the file-selection browser smoke passed with its session assertion. The initial regression test failed because the refusal detector was missing; adding the detector and connecting it to the suite result resolved that failure. Build, typecheck, the public tree audit and documentation checks were also run for this change.

Remaining: Director review and an operator try build. The check addresses the default backend refusal; other fixture errors remain subject to their existing suite assertions.

## 2026-11-10 -- Combine Native media-retirement notices per turn

Claude Native now shows one media-retirement notice for a turn instead of repeating the same notice as individual images make room for newer input. The notice totals retired images and PDF documents, uses singular wording for one item and omits categories whose count is zero. A single retired image therefore reads Replaced 1 old image with a text receipt. The recovery advice still says to attach or request a file again.

`mediaRetirementNotice.ts` formats the message. `ClaudeNativeWorker` accumulates replacement counts during attachment and tool-result admission, then flushes the totals before its terminal result or error event. This ordering matters because the provider adapter removes its listeners on those events. The turn-finally path also clears pending counts without duplicating an already delivered notice. The next turn starts with an empty tally. Media selection, request caps and signed-thinking retention are unchanged.

The failing-first worker regression reproduced three notices where one was expected. Focused checks cover aggregation, singular and plural wording, mixed categories, empty totals, listener settlement and subsequent turns, alongside existing media and compaction cases. Checked on Linux: 98 focused tests passed with none skipped, along with build, typecheck, documentation checks (six link tests) and the public tree audit. No paid provider call is needed for these synthetic regressions.

Remaining: Director review and an operator try build. The real 200k extended-thinking retirement check remains unavailable in the currently exposed model combinations; no model-window behavior is changed here.

## 2026-11-10 -- Use the supported Claude model context windows

Sonnet 4.5 now uses its supported 200,000-token context window throughout Claude Native. Its normal context meter reads 50 percent at 100,000 retained prompt tokens, automatic compaction and handoff use the corrected denominator, and visual admission allows 100 images and 100 PDF pages. Dated IDs behave the same as aliases. Sonnet 4 and older Opus 4, 4.1 and 4.5 variants also use 200K. Haiku 5.5 now uses its published 1M window, while Haiku 4.5 retains 200K.

`claude-native/modelParams.ts` corrects the worker lookup used by context reports, media admission and automatic compaction. `contextEstimator.ts` applies the same bounded model matches to normal context usage. The Sonnet 4.5 exact entries change together; supported older variants are recognized before the broad family fallbacks. Existing Sonnet 4.6 and newer 1M model behavior remains intact. The provider guide, provider manual and Context fill help describe the corrected limits. Runtime catalog lookup remains unchanged.

Checked on Linux with 79 focused context-window, estimator, media-limit, budget, worker-parity and Fable tests passing, none skipped. Six initial Sonnet 4.5 cases failed before correction; the wider comparison exposed twelve additional alias or dated-ID failures. Official model documentation establishes the supported windows and retirement of the older Sonnet 1M beta. Build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit passed.

## 2026-11-10 -- Replace oversized managed image uploads safely

Uploaded JPEG and PNG images can now occupy less disk space while keeping the same saved path. Images over 1 MiB or 2,000 pixels on the long edge are candidates for replacement. After a smaller candidate is fully written, flushed and decoded with the expected dimensions, it atomically replaces the larger upload. The larger uploaded copy is deleted; no full-size backup is retained. Existing message, event, file-context and replay references keep resolving.

`server/src/uploadImages.ts` confines replacement to regular files beneath `UPLOADS_DIR`, rejecting links, escaping realpaths and changed file identities. The upload route waits before returning the path. JPEG stays JPEG at quality 90 with EXIF orientation retained. PNG stays PNG and loses pixels only above 2,000. Neither format is upscaled; animated and 16-bit PNGs stay unchanged. Non-images, other image formats, files below both thresholds and results without a size saving remain untouched. Project Read and file references never replace or delete project files. Legacy model-copy creation now enforces the same uploads boundary.

Checked on Linux with 27 focused upload, attachment and HTTP-route tests, build, typecheck, public tree audit and six documentation tests. A live built server accepted a synthetic upload and served its smaller image through the unchanged preview path. Regression cases first failed on the absent replacement operation, external model-copy writes and dropped JPEG orientation. Managed-path checks, verified replacement and EXIF preservation resolved them. Review tests reproduced lost PNG animation and precision; chunk inspection now refuses both. Hard-link refusal is covered. Failure tests cover writes, flushes, verification, rename and a target changed into a link.

Remaining: Director review and operator try-build acceptance. macOS and Windows require real-host checks. Other image formats retain existing model fitting without destructive conversion.

Resizable Arco dialogs now reserve a right-side gutter for the bottom-right grip. Footer actions such as SPAWN, SEND and the folder picker's confirmation remain clear of the grip at default and minimum sizes. Dragging and keyboard resizing, remembered dimensions, maximize and reset retain their existing behavior.

`WorkSurfaceControls.tsx` applies the existing 26-pixel padding reservation to every resizable surface instead of only surfaces whose outer frame scrolls. This covers `DialogShell` consumers and custom picker and viewer frames through their shared resize controls. Cleanup restores the previous inline padding. ## 2026-11-10 -- Keep streamed replies together across tool status updates

Assistant replies now stay in one bubble when a running tool completes or fails between text deltas. The existing tool card updates where it originally appeared instead of becoming a new text boundary. This applies during live delivery, cold event replay and reconnect after a partially received reply. New tool calls, operator steering and Cursor reasoning continue to separate reply segments.

`client/src/ws/handleServerMessage.ts` checks the reported item or tool-use identity before closing the current segment. `appendActivity` preserves the card identity and first event sequence while applying its latest status and output. The existing event-transcript reconciliation therefore retains the starting tool anchor when a session overlay arrives. Codex app-server already inserts paragraph breaks only between distinct agent-message identities; that server behavior needs no change. The provider guide, workspace manual and Agent reply contextual help describe the ordering.

Before implementation, eight initial cases reproduced split replies during live delivery and cold replay. Tests cover completion, failure, progress updates, item-only and tool-use-only identities, session overlays, reload, steering, new tools and earlier late-text handling. An explicit Cursor text, tool-start, tool-completion, text sequence retains two bubbles without joining words. The Cursor fixture previously expected a completion to split text; its expectation now retains the intended reasoning and new-tool boundaries. Build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit passed.

Remaining: operator acceptance in the next try build, installed-provider acceptance where required, the final release gate and real macOS and Windows hardware checks. No provider inference or credential access was used.

## 2026-11-10 -- Text-first Claude PDF reads

Claude Native and Claude CLI now receive extracted PDF text by default. Scanned pages are fitted as images, and a receipt explains how to request specific visual pages when layout, charts or figures matter. Text pages do not consume the image or PDF-page budget. Previously saved native PDF blocks remain readable in existing conversations.

Native `Read` and `read_url` accept `pdf_mode: "visual"` with a one-based `pages` range. The portable `read_file` and `fetch_file` tools accept the same options and return fitted images of the selected pages for visual inspection. Operator attachments use the same text-first decoder. `docs/PROVIDERS.md`, the provider manual, attachment and tool help, and `CHANGELOG.md` describe the behavior and limits.

A review test then exposed a false PDF receipt for plain text mentioning a media type; checking the decoded file kind resolved it. Build, typecheck, public tree audit and documentation checks were run for the change.

## 2026-11-10 -- Make room for new Native media within model limits

Claude Native now makes room for new files by replacing the oldest retained media with text receipts between API requests. A visible notice reports replaced image and PDF-document counts. Conversations below their limits keep the same history objects and bytes. Tool pairing remains intact; older reasoning is omitted and prefix bookkeeping restarts. The in-progress assistant/tool-result round stays raw, retaining its signed thinking as in-turn compaction does.

`mediaLimits.ts` derives independent image and PDF-page caps from the selected model context window: 100 images at 200k and 600 above, with 100 PDF pages below 1M and 600 at or above it. Unknown windows use 100 each. Encoded media uses 128 MiB on the first-party endpoint and a conservative 20 MiB for other endpoints because upstream platform limits vary. Admission and pre-request PDF repair use the same policy.

`mediaRetention.ts` constructs replacement histories without editing accepted request objects. Fetched base64 PDFs count actual pages; fetched text and media-shaped tool input JSON do not. New media that alone exceeds a guard receives counts and instructions to use COMPACT or HANDOFF before attaching or requesting again.

Focused suites cover limits, retirement, tool pairing, thinking, notices, prefix reset, compaction and files. Splitting a manual paragraph resolved documentation validation. Missing helper, fetched-PDF counting, PDF-repair and tool-input regressions failed before their corrections. The mid-loop Read regression initially stripped the active signature; protecting the current round corrected it. An additional pending-result regression required retiring unsent older media without editing active thinking. All 91 focused tests passed with no skips on Linux. Build, typecheck, documentation checks (six links) and the public tree audit passed.

Remaining: operator-approved isolated Native sign-in and real media acceptance, followed by the final release gate and real-host checks.

## 2026-11-10 -- Drain active local shell trees when tabs close

Closing or handing off a tab now ends its active local shell trees and waits internally for tree cleanup and pipe closure, capped at fifteen seconds. Cancellation retains its existing process-tree termination behavior, and the shell result now waits for platform tree cleanup to finish. This prevents asynchronous cleanup from being forgotten when the provider's main turn has already stopped.

`ollamaTools.ts` retains the tree-signaling promise until the command's pipes close. `localShellLifecycle.ts` keeps active cleanup reachable by workflow identity until the result settles. `disposeProviderResources` invokes that shared drain, covering Ollama, llama.cpp, LM Studio, Strata and DeepSeek through their common dispatcher. The provider guide, local tool manual and Cancel turn contextual help describe the cleanup cap and active-command boundary.

Checked on Linux with four focused tests passing and one PowerShell fixture skipped because that shell is unavailable. Before implementation, the disposal regression returned while the shell result was still pending. Checks exercise actual tab-disposal routing, descendant termination, existing timeout behavior, and asynchronous tree cleanup plus pipe closure without touching another workflow. A nonexistent-shell spawn returns the expected error and leaves no tracked shell for later disposal. Build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit passed.

## 2026-11-10 -- Preserve MCP media after encoded text resources

MCP tool responses now retain images that follow encoded text resources. Encoded resources that decode into ordinary text no longer consume the 20-block media allowance. Actual decoded image and PDF document blocks continue to consume that allowance; PDF page limits remain the responsibility of the provider request guard rather than this response-normalization counter.

`server/src/mcp/fileContent.ts` applies the media counter after the shared decoder identifies each block. Text blocks retain the existing bounded text handling. The source-byte limit and the 100-entry response traversal limit remain in place, so separating text from media does not remove those decoding bounds. The provider guide, in-app provider overview and composer attachment help explain the distinction between response media blocks and request PDF pages.

Two focused regression tests exercise 20 encoded text resources followed by an image, and 21 actual images. Before the correction, the first case dropped its image because all encoded resources incremented the counter. After the correction, the text case retains the image while the actual-image case still retains exactly 20 images and emits a decoding-budget note for the last. The focused MCP suites passed 14 tests with no skips on Linux, using synthetic payloads and no provider credentials. Build, typecheck, documentation checks (six link tests) and the public tree audit passed. An initial build overlapped a release rebase and saw a newly imported shared export before its generated bundle was rebuilt; a clean complete build on the settled tree resolved it.

Remaining: the surrounding Native request-limit and oldest-media retention work is separate. Director review and installed-provider acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-10 -- Keep Native images available after web fetches

Claude Native now accepts pasted and Read images after server web fetches without falsely filling its image and document budget. Text-source documents and web-fetch result envelopes contribute neither PDF pages nor encoded media bytes. Actual PDF documents retain their measured pages; a PDF without internal page metadata is measured from its bytes rather than assumed to contain 100 pages.

`admitImageResult` in `claude-native/imageRead.ts` checks document source type and media type, and reports the specific guard limit alongside image, PDF-page and encoded-size totals. Native attachment preparation supplies attachment-specific recovery advice: compact or start a new conversation, then attach the file again. This avoids suggesting Read access to managed uploads outside the workflow. Existing history remains unchanged, including signed reasoning and prompt-cache bindings. Wire projection still removes internal page metadata without modifying stored messages; saved replay and compaction receipts omit media bytes entirely.

Checked the Native file, image, append-only prefix and tool-result eviction suites on Linux: 49 tests passed, none skipped. The new web-fetch and pasted-notice regressions failed before the correction. A direct image test invocation initially lacked an isolated test home; rerunning through a temporary Arco home resolved that setup error. Build, typecheck, documentation checks (six link tests) and the public tree audit passed.

Remaining: oldest-media replacement and model-specific request limits require separate review. Real Claude Native web-fetch and pasted-image acceptance follows the approved push. No provider calls or credentials were used for the fixture checks.

Codex Agent tabs now receive an explicit live web-search setting on both exec and app-server. Repository provider documentation, the provider manual and transport contextual help describe the matching behavior.

Before the change, all four Agent cases failed because exec supplied no explicit setting, while all four Chat cases already passed. Build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit passed. The shared helper resolved the mismatch without changing model selection, sandboxing, approval policy or MCP attachments.

Remaining: approved real provider search calls must confirm behavior through each transport. These fixture checks pin the policy sent by Arco, not the agent's decision to use web search or the quality of a provider response.

## 2026-11-10 -- Apply registry tool restrictions on supported Codex

Codex tabs on version 0.167 and later now receive the registry's per-tool restrictions. Explicitly disabled tools and cached dangerous tools that were not ticked are passed to both exec and app-server. Older or unknown Codex versions keep their existing server-level connection and an explanatory CONFIG warning. Supported versions no longer show the blanket claim that Codex cannot restrict tools.

`buildCodexMcpOverrides` combines each registry entry's disable list with that server's derived `mcpDisallowedTools`, then emits bare names in `mcp_servers.<key>.disabled_tools`. The shared version predicate also controls the warning and labels in `McpRegistrySection`. ## 2026-11-10 -- Unit gate reports temporary folders

The unit and browser test gates now fail when a test leaves a new Arco temporary folder in the gate's dedicated temporary directory. The failure lists the remaining folder names and preserves the sandbox for inspection, including when the test run already failed. Existing folders and unrelated names do not count as new leftovers.

`scripts/test-unit.mjs` snapshots the dedicated temporary directory before running tests and checks it in the final cleanup path. `scripts/smoke/run-browser-suites.mjs` uses `scripts/temp-folder-runner.mjs` to give browser suites their own temporary root. `scripts/temp-folder-gate.mjs` compares directory names rather than counts alone, so one removed folder cannot conceal a newly leaked one. `CONTRIBUTING.md` documents the failure and retained evidence. The focused tests use temporary synthetic directories and check equal counts, successful cleanup and a failing child with retained evidence.

A combined unit run reported zero temporary-folder leftovers; one unrelated Codex output-style assertion failed and remains assigned for correction. Remaining: the final release gate follows operator testing. macOS and Windows behavior has not been checked on real hardware.

## 2026-11-10 -- Select the supported Codex image setting

`codexImageTool.ts` inspects the offline `codex features list` output and caches the result for one minute. A listed `view_image` feature selects `features.view_image`; a CLI without that feature keeps `tools.view_image`. The probe has a five-second timeout and bounded output. `codex.ts` applies the selected dotted override, while `codex-appserver/worker.ts` applies the equivalent nested thread override. No minimum Codex version is newly imposed. Repository provider documentation, the provider manual and attachment contextual help explain the compatibility behavior.

Checked on Linux with the installed Codex 0.167.0 configuration listing: `view_image` is stable and enabled by default. The focused Codex configuration and attachment tests passed 23 cases with none skipped. Build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit passed. The initial regression test failed before the selector existed. An initial focused run was refused by the temporary-home guard; rerunning with a separate Arco home resolved the test setup failure.

Remaining: a real Codex image turn on both transports requires operator approval. Older-version selection has synthetic coverage; a real older CLI turn has not been run.

Repeated local-model reads now reuse saved image and document content instead of adding another identical file to Conversation files. Archive, restore and delete continue to include the entire conversation file-context folder.

`server/src/providers/localFileMedia.ts` names each bounded text or decoded image snapshot with its SHA-256 content digest and format extension. Existing files pass the checked-file reader before reuse. `server/src/fileContextReceipts.ts` accepts both digest names and the previous random names. Incognito retention remains in bounded memory. The provider, persistence and storage guides describe reuse, alongside the in-app provider manual and the STORAGE project help entry.

A retention fixture also assumed different image names meant different content; distinct generated PNG pixels preserve its original two-image replay check. An unavailable image-library import failed during that fixture update and was replaced with the existing PNG dependency. Build, typecheck, documentation checks (six link tests) and the public tree audit passed.

## 2026-11-10 -- Numeric patterns in public audits

Public audits now distinguish a standalone numeric private pattern from the same digits embedded in a longer number. This avoids reporting unrelated numeric tables while continuing to detect the configured number at digit boundaries. A later standalone occurrence is still detected even when an earlier occurrence is embedded in a longer number. Text patterns retain their existing substring behavior.

The behavior is implemented in `scripts/public-audit.mjs` for configured private patterns, including package and tree scans. The existing reviewed shader bundle exception remains limited to its specific path and token. `scripts/packaging/public-audit.test.mjs` exercises leading and trailing digits, standalone occurrences, subsequent occurrences, and text patterns with synthetic fixture values. No user control or setting changes.

Checked on Linux with the focused package audit tests, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `node scripts/public-audit.mjs --tree`, and `npm run docs:check`. The new boundary test initially failed because the audit used an unrestricted substring search; checking adjacent digits and continuing the search resolved it. The existing shader test was updated to reflect the boundary rule while preserving its path checks.

Remaining: the final release gate and operator try build follow the release pipeline. macOS and Windows have not been checked on real hardware for this change.

`scripts/public-audit.mjs` identifies the shader chunk by its anchored path and hash-shaped filename. `scripts/packaging/public-audit.test.mjs` exercises a new bundle name and verifies that a different number and an address remain visible. It also checks a different directory, another file, prefix and suffix variants, an empty hash and punctuation outside the permitted hash characters. The test constructs the local-pattern fixture from fragments and keeps its pattern file outside the scanned package.

The focused package audit suite passed all three tests on Linux. Build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit passed. Before the change, the new hashed filename failed the focused test because the audit accepted only two earlier filenames. The anchored filename check resolved the failure without broadening the token exception to other files or changing its digest.

Chat web-access documentation now describes the shared public-URL reader's text and media limits. The first review found that the contextual Chat help still described all local fetches as text-only and capped them at 128 KiB; that statement did not account for image and document decoding through the shared reader. The updated wording separates text downloads from media source limits and retains the public-host and redirect protections. No build or browser suite was needed because this change modifies documentation only.

Remaining: the full release gate and operator acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-10 -- Retain local file context across turns

Local-provider conversations now retain named file receipts and recent media across operator turns and reload. Ollama, llama.cpp, LM Studio, Strata and DeepSeek restore up to two fitted images and 8 KiB of decoded file text. Current attachments take priority, while older receipts identify saved inputs or the original tool and arguments. Missing files produce a notice instead of silently disappearing.

`file_context` events carry references without encoded media. Immutable snapshots live in each conversation's `file-context/` folder and count as Conversation files. Operator archive, restore and purge include them; automatic preparation never deletes older snapshots. Exact-file Read access supports retained files in isolated workers without granting access to their parent folders. Incognito uses bounded owner-scoped memory, cleared on close or reset. Delivery tracking excludes pending inputs, and completed handoffs start a fresh file window. Native replay and derived summaries preserve readable receipts.

Regression tests first demonstrated lost attachments on all five adapters. Additional fixtures caught a dropped queued-worker metadata event, duplicate current attachments, missing fetched-document capture and duplicate Native receipts. Correcting those paths preserved the existing delivery boundaries. All 78 focused tests passed, covering two turns, disk replay, MCP images and PDF text, primary dispatch, private memory, missing files, path checks, retention bounds, storage accounting and archive/restore. Build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit passed on Linux. An initial build found two missing test parameter types; explicit declarations resolved them.

Repository provider, persistence and storage guides, the providers manual, Composer help and Tool call help describe the behavior. Remaining: the final release gate and operator acceptance on real hosts.

## 2026-11-10 -- Estimate image context from pixel dimensions

Ollama no longer treats encoded image bytes as text tokens when deciding whether to compact before a request. Images with the same dimensions now have the same visual estimate, even when compression produces very different file sizes. A large uncompressed representation therefore does not trigger compaction merely because its encoding is longer.

`server/src/imageTokens.ts` holds a pure image-header reader and bounded pixel estimate shared with Native context management. Native retains its existing `imagePixelSize` export through a re-export. `providers/ollamaTokens.ts` uses the shared cost for each image while retaining its text, reasoning, tool-argument and schema accounting. Header inspection is bounded, and unknown headers use the conservative maximum image cost. Compaction counts only images admitted by the serving model and request budget. Ollama's later `prompt_eval_count` remains the measured context figure used for the context display.

Regression fixtures first failed for two encodings of the same PNG, different pixel areas and unknown headers of different encoded lengths. The corrected estimator matches Native's pixel cost and keeps the same-image estimates below the same compaction threshold. Existing Native image, receipt and eviction tests also exercise the shared helper. Cached metadata fixtures also exposed an absent capability list; unknown vision is now handled safely. Checked with 47 focused image, compaction, execution-history and Native media tests, plus build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit on Linux.

Repository token-accounting documentation, the token-tracking manual and both worker and cluster Auto-compaction help entries explain the estimate and later measurement.

Remaining: provider and real-host acceptance follow the release checklist.

Later ranges remain available through `read_file` with `pages`. PDFs outside the byte budget provide extracted text, while scanned pages retain fitted-image fallback.

`prepareProviderAttachments` adds opt-in document preparation for `claude.ts`; other providers keep their existing text fallback. Installed Codex and Claude discovered both file tools without a model turn or credentials.

Claude Native can read public image and document links with `read_url`. Local WebFetch and the CLI file servers can now deliver fetched images and document ranges through one Arco reader. The read-only `fetch_file` tool exposes this path to Claude CLI, Codex and Cursor. ## 2026-11-10 -- Read legacy text with line ranges

File reads and attachments now decode suitable non-UTF-8 text as Windows-1252 instead of returning an invalid-text receipt. A short decoding notice precedes the selected text. Offset and limit still select the file's own lines, rather than counting the notice as a line. Files containing NUL bytes or too many unexpected control bytes remain binary receipts.

`readFileContent` and the bytes worker use the shared `decodeLegacyText` helper already used by Grep. Its threshold is checked per bounded chunk, with the allowed whitespace and escape characters excluded from the control count. `readRegularRange` accepts an optional fallback decoder so a non-UTF-8 character beyond the header probe is decoded without rereading the whole file. Existing callers retain their original behavior unless they request that fallback. `legacyTextResult` applies the notice after line selection and retains output caps. Native and local Read, the portable reader, attachments and file-reference expansion inherit the shared behavior.

Synthetic fixtures first reproduced rejection of a Windows-1252 accented word. Focused checks now cover a selected later line, a character beyond the header probe, file and byte inputs, and random NUL-free bytes containing excessive controls. The bounded-read observer remains within its existing I/O budget. The byte reader's existing range-truncation notice is preserved. Build, typecheck, documentation and public checks passed. Full units passed 2,932 tests with 5 skipped both normally and with symlinked temporary directories. The upload-attach and help-docs browser suites passed.

Repository provider guidance, the provider manual and contextual tool help describe the fallback and binary threshold. Remaining: portable Native URL media is a separate correction, and real macOS and Windows acceptance remains on the operator checklist.

## 2026-11-10 -- Grep skips control-heavy binary content

Grep continues to search Windows-1252 text while leaving control-heavy binary data out of search results. Files with common Unicode encodings retain their existing handling. This prevents a NUL-free binary file from appearing as readable text merely because its bytes can be decoded as Windows-1252.

`fileContent/legacyText.ts` supplies the shared legacy-text check and decoder. It rejects NUL bytes and chunks where disallowed control characters reach one percent. Tabs, line breaks, form feeds and escape characters remain allowed. The bounded JavaScript search validates each chunk before accepting legacy text. Native ripgrep uses the same check on its initial prefix and validates matching files before returning lines. The provider guide, provider manual and composer help describe the binary-search boundary.

Synthetic fixtures first reproduced binary output from local and Native Grep, including the ripgrep path. The corrected checks retain a Windows-1252 accented search at its original line number, while rejecting control-heavy content. Tests also cover the exact threshold, a control-heavy later chunk and allowed whitespace. Build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit passed. Normal and symlinked unit runs each passed 2,924 tests with 5 skipped. The help-docs and help-coverage browser suites passed on Linux. No provider calls or credentials were used. Real Windows and macOS acceptance remains separate.

## 2026-11-10 -- Portable codec fallback verification

Bundled HEIC, standalone BMP and TIFF decoding now has executable coverage that proves installed image converters are not used. The checks verify image dimensions and decoded color values from synthetic fixtures. A controlled accelerator returns an intentionally wrong image when enabled, making an accidental external-program path observable rather than relying on the test's name or the machine's installed programs.

`FileContentOptions.externalPrograms` is an internal decoder option for verification. Setting it to false bypasses `fileContent/externalImage.ts` inside the disposable worker before any process can start. Full units passed 2,921 tests with 5 skipped both normally and with symlinked temporary directories. The image-editor-lifecycle browser suite and 17 focused checks passed.

## 2026-11-10 -- Native tool media receipts survive reload

Claude Native now retains a readable receipt after reload for images and documents returned by registry MCP tools. Encoded media remains in the live provider request, not in this receipt. Repository provider guidance, the provider manual and tool contextual help explain how to request media again after reload.

The cursor-segments and turn-visibility browser suites passed.

Documents and notebooks expand as readable text, and unsupported binary files receive a description instead of raw bytes. A review of a preview capture can see the captured image when the reviewer supports images. Native and local Grep search UTF-8, UTF-16 and Windows-1252 text while omitting binary content.

`contextRefs.ts` uses the shared file decoder, retains the context budget, and passes media through `turnEngine.ts` for immediate, queued and steered turns. `searchText.ts` checks file signatures and text encoding for JavaScript Grep; Native ripgrep checks returned content without changing filename-only discovery. `reviewContent.ts` makes a private screenshot copy for the reviewer and removes it after dispatch. The message composer help, workspace and provider manuals, preview review guide and provider guide describe these behaviors.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, public tree audit, documentation checks, the context-reference and preview smoke suites, and the help-docs and help-coverage browser suites. The complete unit suite passed 2,893 tests with 5 skipped, both normally and with symlinked `TMPDIR`. Focused fixtures covered office content, UTF-16, binary search, image delivery and preview report symlinks. Symlinked temporary-directory checks passed 31 file and search tests and 77 file-content and storage tests.

An early Grep implementation skipped legacy encodings, and a context reference failed on an incomplete UTF-8 sequence. Encoding-aware search and the shared decoder corrected both. The initial UTF-16 fixture exposed a byte-order mark classified as audio; the shared decoder corrected the signature order. A deadline fixture initially used a symlinked temporary path and now resolves the canonical file before opening it. Remaining: provider-specific media adapters and real macOS and Windows acceptance are separate release work.

## 2026-11-10 -- Decode files on local and CLI provider transports

Local and CLI provider turns receive file contents as fitted images, document text or notebook cells instead of binary bytes or a filename alone. Local models without confirmed vision receive an image note. Initial attachments and local or Codex steering messages use the same preparation. Cancelling during Cursor or Codex attachment decoding prevents the provider request from starting.

The local `Read` and `WebFetch` tools use `fileContent.ts`. WebFetch classifies accumulated header bytes with the shared reader, preserving large TIFF images whose signature crosses network chunks. The read-only `read_file` MCP tool covers project files for Claude CLI, Codex and Cursor. Codex enables `view_image` on both transports. Cursor print uses a temporary read-only plugin while retaining ask mode and the workflow folder. LM Studio obtains vision metadata from its native model endpoint. Local MCP calls retain images and extract PDF text while enforcing configured tool restrictions.

Checked with build, typecheck, documentation checks, the public tree audit and full units with normal and symlinked temporary directories: 2,897 passed and five skipped in each run. The upload-attach, deepseek, cursor-segments, help-docs and help-coverage browser suites passed on Linux. Synthetic transport fixtures cover all five local providers, CLI payloads, MCP images and PDFs, document ranges, binary notes, cancellation and bounded text I/O.

Regression tests first reproduced discarded images, binary text, cancellation races and truncated TIFF pixels. Canonical attachment paths fixed symlinked-directory failures. Local dependency copies fixed browser fixtures that rejected module imports outside their root; the TIFF generator's argument type was corrected.

Remaining: real macOS and Windows acceptance. No provider calls or credentials were used.

## 2026-11-10 -- Portable image format coverage

File reads and attachments now decode AVIF images and ICO icons and show SVG source alongside a fitted image. ICO chooses the largest directory entry, supports PNG and bitmap payloads, and preserves alpha and transparency masks. SVG source remains available when a preview cannot be rendered or when a model accepts only text.

The shared decoder uses `fileContent/icons.ts` and `fileContent/svg.ts`. SVG rendering runs in the existing disposable worker through unmodified resvg WebAssembly, without script execution, external resource resolution or system-font access. Preview dimensions and encoded bytes use the same limits as other images. The dependency and its full MPL license are recorded in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES`; native module versions and lock records remain unchanged.

Synthetic regressions first failed for both icon payload types, SVG source-plus-image delivery, AVIF brand classification and exact package exceptions. Focused tests now pass, including selected icon size, transparency, bounded SVG text, resource refusal and compiled-worker rendering with an empty executable search path. Build, typecheck, documentation checks, public audits and the image-editor-lifecycle and help-docs browser suites passed on Linux. Full units passed 2,884 tests with 5 skipped; all 29 file and attachment checks passed with a symlinked temporary directory. A symlinked temporary-directory run exposed a noncanonical test path; resolving the fixture path restored the decoder contract.

The package keeps SheetJS runtime entry points, its imported codepage table and license unchanged. Unused command and distribution files are omitted. Reviewed package-only exceptions match exact paths and exact vendor tokens; other tokens, paths and a real IP in the same file still fail. The assembled package audit passes.

Repository attachment guides also replace the former converter-only description.

Remaining: SVG text requiring a font may be absent from the raster, with a visible note and retained source. Real macOS and Windows acceptance remains separate.

## 2026-11-10 -- Native documents, attachments and tool-server media

Claude Native now reads PDFs, office documents and notebook cells through Arco's portable decoder, and fits images without requiring an installed converter. Initial, queued, follow-up and steered attachments use the same preparation. Task helpers share Read. MCP image and embedded document results remain visible to the model, while cards and saved events show readable file receipts instead of encoded media.

Read's `pages` argument selects PDF pages, sheets, slides, sections or cells. `providers/claude-native/fileContent.ts` converts shared blocks into Native content, and `mcp/fileContent.ts` normalizes tool-server results without fetching linked resources. Private page-count metadata is removed before provider requests. Media budgets cover combined images and PDF pages; token estimates use visual dimensions or page counts rather than treating base64 as prose. Actual context measurements still use `count_tokens`.

Later focused checks passed for MCP response bounds and UTF-16 classification. Three browser suites passed on Linux.

The initial portable-image regression still required an external converter, and the MCP regression discarded image blocks. Shared decoding and typed results resolved both. Existing tests were updated for normalized PNG copies, including large GIFs. Queued cancellation and UTF-16 classification failed before correction. Decode slots are abortable and acquired before large-file reads; byte-order marks take precedence over audio signatures.

Remaining: other-provider adapters are separate changes. Utility jobs remain bounded text-only tasks without file tools. Word sections are not printed-layout pages; legacy images without section mappings require a whole-document read. Real macOS and Windows acceptance remains separate.

## 2026-11-10 -- Portable file-content decoding primitives

File-reading adapters now share bounded decoding for images, PDFs, office documents and notebooks. Results separate readable text from image and document blocks. Unsupported binaries and invalid text encodings receive descriptions instead of decoded bytes. Existing provider controls change only when their adapters adopt this infrastructure.

`server/src/fileContent.ts` exports `readFileContent`, `fileContentFromBytes` and `fileHeaderKind`. Disposable workers bound time, memory and concurrency. JavaScript and bundled WebAssembly codecs fit images without requiring installed converters; available ImageMagick or sips can accelerate the work. PNG uses scanline decoding up to 512 megapixels; other raster decoders allow 250 megapixels. Sources can reach 512 MB. PDFium extracts text and renders scanned pages; pdf-lib selects pages. Office and notebook readers preserve indexed text, tables and outputs. Ordinary text retains bounded line reads. Archive parts and spreadsheet sheets load by selection where supported.

`npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run docs:check`, and public audits passed. Normal units passed 2,865 tests with 5 skipped; two added transparency and signature-detection checks passed separately. The subsequent complete symlinked-`TMPDIR` run passed 2,867 with 5 skipped. Fixtures covered offline bundle installation, capped text I/O, a 70 MB PNG and a 20,000 by 20,000 PNG below 768 MB peak memory. The image-editor-lifecycle browser check passed on Linux.

A temporary packed installation decoded PDF, DOCX, XLSX, tall PNG and WebP with no converter or tsx. HEIC WebAssembly loaded and rejected a malformed synthetic container. SheetJS is bundled, avoiding an install-time CDN dependency. The native lockfile entries remain identical to the previous candidate. A vulnerable codec candidate was replaced; the dependency audit reports no findings.

Remaining: provider, attachment and MCP adoption, plus real macOS and Windows acceptance.

## 2026-11-10 -- Keep the opening request whole through in-turn compaction

Follow-up to the context measurement change. An in-turn compaction never cuts the request that started the turn: when that request (text plus attachment references) exceeds `KEPT_REQUEST_MAX_BYTES` (128,000 UTF-8 bytes), the compaction is deferred for that turn and one notice names the size and suggests Fold or Handoff. The previous behavior kept the first 64 KB with a cut marker, which the operator decision rules out. `inTurnCompact` now resets at every turn start so the notice can repeat for a later oversized turn. `keptRequestText` returns the text with its byte size; `keptRequestFits` is the one place the cap is read.

`count_tokens` leaves out the server-tool definitions (web search, web fetch, code execution). The provider guide records that they amount to a few hundred tokens, under 0.5% of any window, so the in-turn threshold is unaffected. The display side (worker badges, ACTIVITY session line) landed separately in the client display correction; the provider guide now states both rules together.

Tests: the cap test asserts deferral with one notice, no summary request and an untouched history; `keptRequestFits` and the byte size are covered; the attachment-reference test stays. Checked with build, typecheck, both unit runs (normal and with a symlinked temporary directory), the public tree audit, the documentation check and the browser suites compaction-threshold, composer-status, help-docs and help-coverage.

Documentation: provider guide, manual (in-turn compaction), contextual help (auto-compaction policy, cluster member policy), changelog. Remaining: spend-total omissions and the remote budget fraction mismatch stay on the board for 0.1.6; a live research turn on the release candidate confirms which measurement rule each server-tool round took.

## 2026-11-10 -- Measure Claude Native context from the real prompt size

A Claude Native turn that ran web search, web fetch or code execution no longer compacts early. The in-turn check, the CTX meter fallback, the between-turn auto-compaction and auto-handoff readings and the context budget read the real prompt size of the last request, never the summed usage of a request the API sampled several times. In-turn compaction keeps the request that started the turn word for word, with attachment references, before the automatic note.

Cause, confirmed in the worker: `maybeCompactInTurn` and the end-of-turn `usagePromptTokens` fallback summed the round's top-level usage, which for a server-tool round is the sum over passes, so eleven passes of about 90k read as 97% of a 1M window. The kept tail collapses consecutive user messages, so a turn without narration lost its opening request to a later steer or mail message.

New `usageContext.ts`: last message pass of `usage.iterations`, else top-level usage for a single-pass round only, else no figure; the in-turn check then runs `count_tokens` and skips the decision when that fails. Billing sums a `compaction` pass. `native_compacted` persists `keptRequest`; replay composes the same text. The Claude CLI session reading applies the same rule, and a Claude tab without a server reading shows no CTX figure instead of the turn's summed usage.

Fourteen worker and rule tests (thirteen failing first) plus CLI and meter tests. Checked with build, typecheck, both unit runs, the public tree audit, the documentation check and the compaction browser suites. Whether the API sends `usage.iterations` for plain server-tool rounds is undocumented; the console records which rule measured a round.

Documentation: provider guide and architecture notes, manual (configuration, context window), contextual help (auto-compaction, cluster policy, context fill), changelog. Remaining: a live research turn on the release candidate confirms the path taken.

## 2026-11-09 -- Return healthy changed files when another project is unavailable

CHANGED FILES now returns available files when another registered project cannot finish validation. Root preparation counts against the same request deadline as discovery. A root that times out is omitted, the page is marked incomplete, and the healthy project rows remain usable. Files still require the existing lexical, canonical and protected-target checks.

`explorerChanged.ts` starts its 650-millisecond deadline before preparing the access context or roots. Preparation has an early cutoff that reserves time for healthy scans. `knownRoots` and `projectRoots` can publish independent validated roots without waiting for unrelated project display metadata. The ordinary explorer root collection remains available for other callers. Canonical duplicates and registered descendants retain the existing scan rules. Settings canonicalization omits timed-out locations rather than treating an unresolved home alias as a grant. Late preparation results cannot add roots or mutate the returned page, and directory traversal retains its late-handle cleanup.

The regression fixture first held only the nondefault registered root's stat and observed that the request failed to settle before release. Checks now require the healthy default file and an incomplete result before that held stat is released. Additional fixtures cover configured locations and nested checkouts, delayed history registry reads, canonical-grant parity with the ordinary explorer, unresolved home aliases, and late-result immutability. Existing changed-file coverage, budget, ranking and confinement assertions remain in place. ## 2026-11-09 -- Keep changed files visible across registered projects

CHANGED FILES now includes authorized projects beyond the default projects folder, including registered projects outside the home folder. A project-specific scan uses the same grants and stays inside its selected folder. Unregistered folders, protected files and links escaping a root do not become readable. Project options and file rows can therefore include these additional projects in the existing list.

`explorerChanged.ts` builds one request-local access context and collects the roots already used by the explorer. Canonical duplicates share one scan. Registered descendants are pruned from ancestor walks and scanned separately, retaining their project labels even when an ancestor excludes nested checkouts. Each root receives an equal share of the total 20,000-entry and 650-millisecond traversal budgets. A large or stalled root cannot spend another root's share. The newest 500 matches remain eligible across the collected results, and any exhausted traversal or result limit sets `partial`.

Failing-first fixtures reproduced omitted projects, an unauthorized explicit scan and entry-budget starvation. Focused tests cover registry removal, outside-home registration, protected files, escaping links, nested-root deduplication and stalled-directory fairness. Existing ranking and discovery fixtures now register their synthetic roots explicitly. Browser coverage checks project selection, exact viewer paths and reveal actions. Repository guides, the manual and contextual help describe cross-project discovery and shared limits; release notes retain one CHANGED FILES entry.

A browser fixture initially checked reveal before its asynchronous request; waiting for the request corrected it.

Remaining: final release acceptance and real-platform checks are separate. Existing reveal outside TREE's root is deferred.

## 2026-11-09 -- Return safe directory aliases from Glob

## 2026-11-09 -- Honor discovery deadlines during pending directory I/O

Project-document discovery now returns partial results when its deadline expires even if a directory operation is still pending. A slow folder cannot keep ARTIFACTS waiting beyond the scan budget. Late directory handles are closed, late reads release their directory, and late failures are handled without unhandled promise rejections. Existing path boundaries, search exclusions and result limits remain in force.

`scanBeforeDeadline` bounds asynchronous waits while preserving cleanup ownership for operations that cannot be cancelled. `projectScan.ts` uses it for directory opens, reads, closure, nested-checkout probes and Git ignore queries. Document root resolution, known-project loading and candidate metadata reads share the document scan's deadline. `projectIgnore.ts` rounds remaining milliseconds before passing a timeout to Git, avoiding invalid fractional subprocess timeouts. The client guide, ARTIFACTS manual and contextual help explain that slow folders can produce an incomplete list while available results remain usable.

Five failing-first Linux cases cover a held open, held read, late open rejection, late read rejection and a fractional remaining Git deadline. The four delayed operations reproduce the old over-budget response; their corrected checks return partial results promptly and verify late handle closure. The deadline cases and existing document, Native/local search and TUNE MD regressions pass together with twenty-two checks. Verification uses synthetic projects and isolated homes without provider calls.

## 2026-11-09 -- Preserve artifact copy errors and descriptor ownership

MOVE TO PROJECT now preserves the underlying copy error when a write fails, instead of replacing it with a bad-file-descriptor error during cleanup. The original artifact remains available. Successful copies retain their existing review, hashing, destination checks and synchronization behavior. Output transfers and downloads use the same ownership rule, including interrupted streams.

`artifactCopy.ts`, `outputs.ts` and `http/outputRoutes.ts` create streams through the open FileHandle rather than passing its numeric descriptor to a separate stream owner. Reads retain explicit cleanup; output transfers use awaited handle writes before synchronization and closure. Destruction on failure now informs the handle that its descriptor was closed, making subsequent cleanup safe. A server-wide search found no remaining streams constructed from raw FileHandle descriptors.

The regression injects write failures during both staging and publishing, and delays handle cleanup so stream destruction finishes first. Before the fix, cleanup masked the injected failure with EBADF. The existing 72 MB random-binary test remains and is repeated ten times with ordinary temporary storage and ten times through a directory symlink. Build, typecheck, both unit-suite modes (2,706 passed and one skipped each), documentation checks, the public tree audit, and storage and outputs browser suites cover the change on Linux.

The initial large-file run exposed exhausted temporary storage after the ownership fix revealed ENOSPC. Verification moved to separate disk-backed synthetic temporary storage. The linked-directory fixture needed a canonical comparison. A FileHandle write-stream cleanup hang was resolved by using awaited handle writes. Long temporary paths also exposed fixture socket-length and ancestor-discovery assumptions; the full gate was rerun with short paths. No control, setting, label or documented copy policy changes, so repository guides, the in-app manual and contextual help need no edits. Real Windows and macOS acceptance remains separate.

Builders and the test-writing preset keep it, as do ordinary standalone Native Claude tabs.

`TerminalInput` reads the workflow's membership and role from the cluster store on every surface. The role helper recognizes the non-coding presets and explicit restrictions. A hidden button clears any previously armed state, while the existing one-turn send behavior remains unchanged for roles that can use it. README, `docs/CLIENT.md`, the architecture guide, glossary, CHANGELOG, manual and contextual help describe the visibility rule. The README fixture selects a director, whose button was already hidden, so its screenshot does not change.

Failing-first tests reproduced the researcher preset and a director opened in `WorkflowView` seeing the button. Focused checks now cover each preset, coordinator and custom no-code roles, a builder, and a standalone tab. Build, typecheck, documentation checks, the public tree audit and seven composer, cluster and help browser suites passed on Linux. The unit run passed 2,697 tests and skipped four, but the large-binary artifact-copy test failed with an invalid file descriptor during close. The same test failed on the unchanged release base. Two browser fixtures needed the scoped saved-cluster response; adding synthetic replies resolved them. No provider account or paid model is used.

Remaining: the existing artifact-copy failure is tracked separately. The final release gate and real-platform acceptance remain open.

## 2026-11-08 -- Separate live previews from workbench controls

Live PREVIEW now opens a separate address for each workflow. Its app cannot use Arco controls or another preview, and operator cookies and Authorization stay out of app requests. Ports are taken only while views are open. Occupied ports are skipped; exhaustion names the configured range and the setting to change. Multiple views share one listener without one view closing the others.

PREVIEW PORTS in Settings > Advanced accepts a fixed range, normally 4300-4309. ARCO_PREVIEW_PORTS overrides it for services. New previews use the current range and Arco's bind address; existing views keep their ports. Protected lease endpoints issue narrow workflow access, renew active views and release closed or expired views. Separate HTTP and WebSocket listeners keep assets and live reload working. Service-worker registration is refused to prevent workers installed through PREVIEW surviving port reuse.

Failing-first tests reproduced the original credential leak and a shared-cookie invalidation case. Listener-scoped capabilities and observer-specific leases resolve both. EDIT on a pending message now returns its selected skills alongside text, kind and attachments. Existing draft text and skill choices are kept. Restored choices merge by scope and slug, so resending applies each selected skill once. Choices stay with their owning tab across navigation, including an edit reply received while another tab is open.

The composer keeps one-shot choices in its draft store, reads them at send time and clears them after an accepted send. Removing the tab drops its choices; pinning still supersedes a one-shot choice. ## 2026-11-08 -- Protect HTTPS session cookies in transit

HTTPS workbench sessions now mark their browser cookie Secure. HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, Path=/ and session-only lifetime remain unchanged. Plain HTTP remains supported.

The mint route passes its request to sessionCookieHeader. Direct TLS supplies the transport proof. A reverse proxy must be a trusted peer, declare one HTTPS forwarding value and carry an approved HTTPS browser Origin. Forwarding headers alone, mismatched origins and unrelated peers cannot assert HTTPS. Loopback proxy peers are trusted; ARCO_TRUSTED_PROXIES adds exact peer IPs for another proxy. Packaged services retain this configuration.

Four failing regression cases first demonstrated the missing Secure flag, including the real mint response. Focused checks passed 29 cases. A real Chromium fixture uses a temporary certificate and inert TLS proxy to check the accepted cookie, then verifies HTTP with a protocol-only spoof. The full browser set passed all 90 suites after repairing a stale layout skill-catalog fixture. No provider turn, terminal attach or saved credential was used.

The integration gate passed 2,634 unit cases with four skipped, plus build, typecheck, documentation and the public tree audit. Seven affected browser suites passed after rebasing. Earlier gates exposed a linked-path Native warning failure and five upload-root startup failures, all reproduced without this change. Incoming fixes resolved both families; six startup checks passed under a symlinked temporary directory. The shared browser fixture repair supplies catalog data for the layout fixture's synthetic project.

The transport check is separate from CORS served-address metadata. Repository API, operations and remote guides, the manual and contextual System help describe the same proxy requirements and installed-service environment updates.

The guide corrects cluster HISTORY and PREVIEW selection: focused member, director, then first member. Manual and help name the development frontend port exception. Remaining: pending-message skill restoration, preview isolation, and real-host and release acceptance.

A link that leaves its project or worktree is refused, including a link to another registered tree. Unrelated folders and protected application or credential directories remain unavailable.

Git operations and diffs retain `requireProjectBinding` and still require an exact admitted working tree. The HTTP API guide documents the search parameter and its refusal response. ## 2026-11-08 -- Retry transient locks in checked document saves

Plan, plan-reference and knowledge saves regain their bounded Windows rename retries. An ordinary temporary file lock can clear without failing the save immediately. Each attempt still refuses a destination or parent whose identity changed, including a new destination created by another writer while an initial save was waiting. Failed saves retain the current destination and remove their temporary files when the original parent remains available.

`atomicCheckedWriteFile` and `replaceCheckedFile` use `renameWithRetry` with a validation callback before every rename attempt. Validation errors leave the retry loop immediately; only the existing transient Windows rename error codes receive backoff. Both the destination and its parent are compared with their original identities. The existing save controls and document formats are unchanged, so the manual and contextual help need no wording change. The changelog records the restored behavior.

Sixteen synthetic retry cases passed alongside the existing atomic-write and checked-file cases: 42 focused tests passed normally and with a symlinked TMPDIR. The fixture exercises EACCES, EPERM and EBUSY, changed identities, initial creation, permanent refusal, the retry deadline and cleanup. Initial saves failed on attempt one before the fix. Callback extraction also exposed snapshot scope and type errors; explicit snapshots and the Stats type corrected them. Build, typecheck, the full unit suite, browser suites, documentation and public tree checks cover the combined branch.

Remaining: real Windows acceptance remains open. The platform fixture demonstrates the retry branch on Linux and does not count as Windows hardware acceptance.
