## 2026-11-08 -- Restore archived directory permissions in legacy checkpoints

Legacy tar checkpoint restores now retain archived directory permission bits on POSIX systems, alongside the file permissions already restored. A private directory no longer returns with permissions derived from the process defaults. New destination directories stay private while their contents are populated, and final modes are applied from the deepest directories upward so restrictive parent permissions cannot interrupt extraction.

`restoreLegacyTar` keeps its complete destination preflight before the first project write. Its final mode pass opens directory handles without following links, checks their recorded identities and current canonical locations, then changes permissions through those handles. The existing project root and application state remain outside the archived directory entries. Windows does not implement POSIX directory modes. The checkpoint guide, manual and REVERT TO HERE help describe the legacy behavior and its additive restore limitation.

Four new synthetic regressions check nested 0700 and 0750 directories, private creation, restrictive final modes and a redirected directory. The focused suite passed all 42 cases, including existing extraction and checked-file tests, both normally and with a symlinked TMPDIR. The initial permissive-umask fixture reproduced the lost modes; delaying mode application resolved it. The combined local gate covers build, typecheck, all unit and browser suites, documentation and the public tree audit.

Remaining: real macOS archive acceptance and the existing real-host release checks.

## 2026-11-08 -- Stop cancelled multipart summaries before later requests

Stopping Native compaction now cancels the entire operation, including the gap after one summary part succeeds and before another begins. Later summary calls are not started, and cancelled manual or in-turn compaction leaves the previous conversation history intact. A later explicit COMPACT starts a fresh operation rather than inheriting permanent cancellation.

The worker records a compaction cancellation generation before asynchronous preparation begins. Each summary part checks that generation before and after its request, including the final part. `generateCompactSummary` checks it again before creating an SDK request, while the existing controller still aborts a request already in flight. `cancelCompaction` advances the generation even when no request controller is active. Operation ownership clears after settlement. The provider guide, configuration and recovery manual sections, and COMPACT and worker policy help describe cancellation across parts.

Checked with synthetic summarizer functions and recording SDK stubs. New regressions cancel manual and in-turn compaction after one successful part, cancel manual preparation before the first request, and refuse entry to a stopped summary generation. They verify one request at most, no successful marker, unchanged history and a successful later explicit retry. Initial tests started subsequent calls despite cancellation. The operation generation and boundary checks resolve those failures. All 22 focused cancellation, ownership and in-turn checks pass without provider calls.

## 2026-11-08 -- Keep every summary request within its input budget

Native compaction now splits projected conversation text before any part exceeds its estimated input budget. A user and assistant pair can split between roles when necessary. Long individual messages are sliced without adding unbudgeted annotation text, so no conversation text is discarded. This applies to both manual and automatic compaction.

`splitSummaryParts` enforces the supplied budget for every result instead of allowing an assistant continuation to exceed it. `summarizeInParts` always sends those results, including the single-part case. Each request reserves room for the actual carried summary and supplies a leading user context note when a part starts with assistant narration. If the carried summary itself cannot fit, compaction reports failure before another request and preserves the existing conversation. The provider guide, compaction manual and worker policy help describe the bounded requests.

Checked with synthetic projected messages and a stubbed summarizer. Regressions cover the reported boundary-crossing pair, long messages, small budgets, assistant-leading parts, carried-summary overhead and a carried summary too large to send. Initial tests reproduced parts above their budget and the oversized original-prefix fallback. Strict splitting and per-request headroom resolve those failures. All 19 focused budget and in-turn compaction checks pass without provider calls.

The Linux gate passed build, typecheck, 2,579 unit tests (four skipped), docs and the public tree audit. The full browser run covered 89 suites; only the known layout-ranges fixture failed. ## 2026-11-08 -- Clarify browser file validation guidance

The getting-started guide now identifies browser file operations and Media Studio as the scope of the Windows path spelling checks. It no longer describes these checks as a rule for every file path used by Arco. Ordinary drive-letter paths and network shares remain supported by the browser policy, while ambiguous component spellings are refused there.

The opening paragraph of `docs/GETTING_STARTED.md` names the affected interfaces explicitly. The HTTP guide, file-editing manual, and file, media, save and download help also specify that changed-file validation happens when opening a file. They no longer imply that every later replacement is monitored throughout descriptor-based I/O. Agent file tools continue to use their separate project path resolver. This documentation correction changes no runtime checks, controls, saved settings or defaults, and adds no release-note claim about new behavior.

Checked against the Windows spelling checks in `fileAccessPolicy.ts` and the separate `resolveToolPath` implementation. The documentation check and public tree audit passed. The documentation review identified an overly broad sentence in the guide; naming the browser operations and Media Studio resolves that mismatch without claiming wider enforcement. Runtime suites were not repeated for this prose-only correction. Real Windows filesystem acceptance remains open, including the existing checklist for alternate streams, trailing characters, drive paths and network shares.

## 2026-11-08 -- Match browser origins by their complete address

Browser HTTP requests and WebSocket upgrades now require an exact approved scheme, host and port. Another localhost port or unrelated tailnet site cannot use an existing browser session. Same-site browser subresources without Origin are refused for APIs and uploads; navigation to the app shell and non-browser callers retain their existing behavior.

Access through Arco's own served address remains available after Host validation, including remote access without new upgrade configuration. The Vite proxy preserves that public Host. Reverse proxies preserve Host and state their public scheme through X-Forwarded-Proto. This CORS authority is separate from any assertion about encrypted transport.

The development frontend remains approved while development mode is enabled. Host wildcard rules do not approve other browser origins. ARCO_ALLOWED_ORIGINS adds complete HTTP or HTTPS origins for extra cross-origin callers, including custom ports. Exact ARCO_ALLOWED_HOSTS names keep their standard origins. Packaged services retain the new origin configuration on installation.

Failing tests first showed an unrelated localhost origin accepted by the pure guard and a preflight reaching HTTP204. Focused checks now cover scheme and port mismatches, configured remote addresses, production versus development, same-origin remote compatibility and package-service persistence. A real Chromium scenario uses an existing session, verifies configured remote access and the real Vite proxy, and refuses unrelated-port POST, preflight, control and terminal handshakes. No terminal is attached and no provider turn runs. The management-hub smoke passed ten steps and preview verification passed seventeen checks. The full gate passed build, typecheck, 2,504 unit tests with four skipped, all 89 browser suites, documentation checks and the public tree audit.

The browser fixture needed context-level response assertions because CORS hides page response events. Repository guides, manual and contextual help describe the same boundary. Remaining: HTTPS cookie flags, preview-origin isolation and real-host acceptance.

## 2026-11-08 -- Bind saved Native login tokens to an explicit destination

Claude Native workflows and the Native evaluator now send saved login tokens to Anthropic unless the global settings file explicitly sets a custom endpoint. An inherited SDK endpoint variable alone cannot redirect saved credentials. The settings-file-only `claudeNative.baseUrl` override preserves deliberate custom use without adding a new UI control. A configured custom destination receives the saved token.

`apiEndpoint.ts` reads the operator-owned global setting, never project settings. Native worker and evaluator SDK constructors pass that resolved destination explicitly. An existing worker rebuilds its client when the destination changes, even if the credential timestamp is unchanged. Settings validation accepts HTTP or HTTPS URLs without embedded credentials, queries or fragments. Unrelated settings saves preserve the override, and explicit null clears it. API-key-only SDK endpoint behavior is unchanged; this change does not add another Native sign-in mode.

Checked with synthetic saved tokens, an isolated home and recording SDK fetches. Regressions cover both saved-login paths ignoring an ambient override, a configured endpoint serving both paths, worker rebuilding, project override refusal, settings persistence and clearing, invalid endpoint values and unchanged environment API-key SDK setup. Initial regressions directed saved-token clients to the ambient endpoint and dropped the explicit setting. Explicit destination binding and settings sanitization resolve them. The provider guide, settings reference, manual and provider/authentication help describe the source and trust requirement.

The Linux gate passed build, typecheck, 2,507 unit tests (four skipped), all 88 browser suites, documentation checks and the public tree audit. All 16 focused endpoint and evaluator tests also passed with a symlinked TMPDIR. Remaining: real provider and real macOS and Windows acceptance; no real sign-in or provider call was attempted.

## 2026-11-08 -- Separate Native sign-in state from its private verifier

Claude Native sign-in now keeps the PKCE verifier on the server rather than including it as OAuth state in a browser URL. Each sign-in generates two independent random values. The public state identifies the pending flow; the private verifier supplies the S256 proof during token exchange. Local callbacks and manual URL paste retain their existing completion behavior.

`oauth.ts` sends the actual state and verifier as separate token-exchange fields. The authorization URL still carries the matching S256 challenge and challenge method. Flow identifiers, expiry, cancellation, credential-binding checks and superseded-flow handling are unchanged. The provider guide, in-app sign-in manual and authentication help describe the separation without exposing authorization material.

Checked with synthetic sign-in grants and isolated credential storage. The new regression compares the exchange fields, recalculates the challenge, checks authorization and callback URLs and public flow results for verifier disclosure, and confirms one exchange. Existing callback, manual fallback, cancellation, expiry, duplicate completion, supersession, malformed grants and refresh tests remain in the focused set. The initial regression failed because state and verifier were identical. Independent generation and separate exchange arguments resolve it. A test edit also touched an embedded JavaScript fixture; removing its accidental type annotation restored the fixture.

The Linux gate passed build, typecheck, 2,501 unit tests (four skipped), all 88 browser suites, documentation checks and the public tree audit. All 18 focused tests also passed with a symlinked TMPDIR. Remaining: real provider sign-in and real macOS and Windows acceptance; no real sign-in was attempted.

## 2026-11-08 -- Limit plan tool admission to supported methods

Agent plan authoring continues to create, list and attach plans and to read, write and list their reference documents. Other HTTP plan methods now require the operator browser session. A cookie-free caller cannot replace the checklist, mark the plan complete, delete it or remove its reference documents. The ordinary plan controls use the existing browser session automatically.

`wsAuth.ts` now evaluates the request method together with the exact plan path. The six declared method/path pairs match the workflow MCP plan catalog. Query parameters do not widen admission, and nearby paths receive no inherited exception. Cluster peer tools and the loopback voice relay retain their existing admission rules. The HTTP guide and request-boundary notes, the plan manual and contextual edit, completion, delete and reference help describe the distinction.

Checked with a method matrix covering every plan-exempt path, missing and valid cookies, invalid cookies and query variants. Synthetic requests through the real router exercise all six registered MCP tools, reject operator-only changes before request-body reads, preserve the saved plan and reference, and retain authenticated operator actions. Initial regressions admitted PATCH and DELETE without a cookie. The exact-method check resolves those failures. The Linux gate passed build, typecheck, 2,538 unit tests (four skipped), all 88 browser suites, documentation checks and the public tree audit. All 44 focused checks also passed with a symlinked TMPDIR.

Remaining: real macOS and Windows acceptance.

## 2026-11-08 -- Apply file access rules to content search

Content search in the command palette now omits files protected from the file browser. Ordinary project files still appear, and protected matches do not use the result limit. Git review keeps showing tracked changes, including paths that the file browser would protect.

The content search engine checks each matching file against the shared browser-file access policy before returning its path or matching text. The check includes the file name, protected parent folders, resolved path and the project root. The search still honors the existing ignored folders and Git ignore rules. The command palette help, in-app manual, client guide and changelog describe the filter. No setting or command changes.

A synthetic regression fixture placed matching text in ordinary and protected file names. Before the fix, the search returned both protected names and their matching lines. The shared access check removes those hits, and a one-result limit still returns the ordinary file. The test does not use provider credentials or an external service. Build, typecheck, the public audit, documentation checks and 2,484 unit tests passed on Linux; four unit tests were skipped. Sixteen search and file-policy tests passed with a symlinked temporary directory. The full browser gate remains for the committed change.

Remaining: the final release gate and the real macOS and Windows checks are still required.

## 2026-11-08 -- Stage and validate legacy checkpoint archives

Legacy tar checkpoints are now read into a temporary staging tree before any project file is changed. Archive entries and existing destination paths are checked first. Links, parent escapes, special entries and linked destination folders refuse the restore as a whole. Ordinary archived files still replace or restore project files, while newer files remain. New Git-tree checkpoints keep their existing restore path.

`legacyTarRestore.ts` reads bounded regular files and directories, including GNU long names and POSIX path metadata. Archives are limited to 512 MB and 10,000 materialized entries. Staging is cleaned after success or failure. The checkpoint guide, HISTORY manual and REVERT TO HERE help describe the legacy behavior and limits.

Checked on Linux with failing-first link and traversal archives that contained an earlier valid replacement, confirming the project stayed unchanged on refusal. Positive tests cover ordinary files, newer-file preservation, linked destinations, GNU and POSIX names, and a real system-tar archive. The combined gate passed build, typecheck, 2,524 unit tests with one skipped, docs and public audit; 79 focused checks passed under symlinked TMPDIR. All 88 browser suites ran, with the single theme startup timeout cleared by the six-suite follow-up, and affected server-backed checks passed. The old direct extraction changed an earlier file before reporting an unsafe later entry; full staging removes that failure. Real macOS and Windows archive acceptance remains open.

## 2026-11-08 -- Reject ambiguous Windows file path components

Windows browser file paths now reject components ending in dots or spaces and paths using alternate data stream separators. These spellings can identify a different file from the name shown in the interface. Ordinary drive-letter paths, network shares and case-insensitive project containment remain supported.

`assertSpellingSafe` checks Windows components after separating the drive or UNC root. The drive separator remains valid; colons in file components are refused. UNC server and share components receive the same trailing-character check. The getting-started guide, file-editing manual and file and media destination help explain using ordinary names when a path is refused.

Checked on Linux with failing-first `path.win32` cases for trailing dots, trailing spaces and alternate streams, plus positive drive, UNC and case cases. These tests simulate path syntax and are not real Windows acceptance. The combined gate passed build, typecheck, 2,524 unit tests with one skipped, docs and public tree audit. All 79 focused checks passed under symlinked TMPDIR. The full 88-suite browser run had one theme fixture startup timeout, cleared in the six-suite follow-up; final affected browser checks passed. The initial tests showed that component-name matching alone accepted ambiguous spellings. Remaining: real Windows checks for short names, streams, trailing dots, network shares and case, and macOS filesystem acceptance.

## 2026-11-08 -- Verify file handles before reads and writes

File previews, downloads and file saves now verify the opened regular file before exposing or changing its contents. The checks compare file identity and recheck the parent folder. A replaced parent or changed target is refused. Writes do not truncate an existing file before these checks pass. These portable checks do not turn the application into an operating-system sandbox.

`checkedFile.ts` supplies checked reads, writes and atomic document replacement. Explorer, raw media, output downloads and copies, text and image saves, bounded tool reads and local writes use the checked handles. Plan and knowledge reads and atomic writes also use them. Output copies retain their owner-only permissions. The HTTP guide, file-editing manual and save, download and media help describe retrying from a fresh selection after a refusal.

Checked on Linux with deterministic parent swaps during read and write, stable ordinary files, existing output-copy permission tests and plan concurrency tests. The combined gate passed build, typecheck, 2,524 unit tests with one skipped, docs and public audit. All 79 focused checks passed with symlinked TMPDIR. All 88 browser suites were exercised; the theme startup timeout cleared on the six-suite follow-up and affected server-backed checks passed. Testing caught an inline-copy mode regression, corrected by retaining explicit owner-only creation. Concurrency fixtures were moved to the handle-read seam. Concurrent local filesystem writers remain outside any claim of complete sandboxing; real-host checks remain open.

## 2026-11-08 -- Bind file views and project actions to known folders

Search and Git views now use the workflow folder recorded by the server. A caller-supplied folder cannot redirect an operation belonging to another workflow. Project-only document actions require a folder already known to Arco. Ordinary project selection and read-only folder navigation remain available, and the selected project can be opened or registered before using its document actions.

`requireProjectBinding` applies the existing binding policy to search, diff, worktree, comparison and Git mutation routes. Plan, skill, knowledge and handoff routes validate their project context, including a plan move destination. Public-project directory validation checks canonical aliases. The HTTP guide, right-panel manual and contextual Git, plan, knowledge and settings help explain the selected-folder behavior.

Checked on Linux with a made-up workflow and outside fixture folder, a valid workflow with a forged caller folder, and known-project operations without a workflow. Existing HTTP revision, reference-create and refused-move tests now register their synthetic project contexts before testing conflicts. Build, typecheck, 2,524 unit tests with one skipped, docs and public audit passed in the combined gate. All 79 focused checks passed under symlinked TMPDIR. All 88 browser suites ran; a theme startup timeout cleared on the six-suite follow-up, and affected server-backed and Git checks passed. Real-host acceptance remains open.

## 2026-11-08 -- Check skill import sources after resolving links

Folder skill imports and source updates now reject protected folders even when a normal-looking alias points to them. Protected filenames within an external source folder are refused as well. Importing an ordinary skill folder still provides the same reviewed file list, size limits and security findings before anything is installed.

`collectFromFolder` checks the source root through `safeDirectory` before walking it. The internal collector for already-installed skills remains separate, because managed skill storage is an intentional application document location. Child links remain forbidden, and files are opened without following the final link. The feature guide, Skills manual and IMPORT and UPDATE help explain which source folders can be selected.

Checked on Linux with a synthetic protected source alias, an ordinary source and a protected child file. Existing folder, zip, GitHub fixture, update, move, duplicate and sharing tests remain green. The combined gate passed build, typecheck, 2,524 unit tests with one skipped, documentation checks and tree audit. The 79 focused symlinked-TMPDIR checks passed. The full 88-suite browser run had one theme fixture startup timeout, cleared by the six-suite follow-up; final skill and plan server checks passed. The initial regression showed that rejecting child links did not protect an aliased root. The root check closes that case. Real-host acceptance remains open.

## 2026-11-08 -- Refuse links in managed document storage

Plans, plan reference documents, knowledge articles, skills and handoff prompt overrides now refuse linked entries inside their managed storage. A reference cannot read an outside file through a link or write into an outside folder through a linked reference directory. Plan moves inspect the source reference tree before creating the destination copy. The project folder itself can still be reached through a filesystem alias.

`managedPath.ts` checks storage descendants, and `projectDocumentPath` applies the project-root policy before joining state paths. The plan store, knowledge store, skill paths and handoff prompt paths use these checks. Project ignore creation also refuses a linked state folder. The cluster guide, plan-reference manual and contextual plan, knowledge, skill and handoff help describe the refusal and recovery.

The combined gate passed build, typecheck, 2,524 unit tests with one skipped, documentation checks and public audit. All 79 focused checks passed under symlinked TMPDIR. All 88 browser suites were exercised; the theme fixture timeout cleared in the six-suite follow-up, and affected server-backed checks passed. Existing concurrency tests needed their pause moved from path reads to checked handle reads as the subsequent race protection was integrated. Real-host acceptance remains open.

## 2026-11-08 -- Confine local file tools to resolved project paths

Native and local-model file tools now refuse paths that leave the selected workflow folder through a file or directory link. Read, Write, Edit and explicit search folders resolve to the same physical project. Ordinary inward links, new nested files and project folders reached through an alias remain usable. Glob also refuses outside patterns before enumeration and validates returned paths.

The shared `toolPath.ts` resolver supplies this boundary to the Native and local tool registries. The existing lexical helper remains available for callers that perform their own physical checks. Bounded file reads remain in place after integrating the provider read-limit change. The provider guide, provider manual and TRANSPORT help explain the folder boundary without changing the separate shell approval policy.

Checked on Linux with failing-first synthetic file and directory links, dangling links, outside Glob patterns and positive alias cases. The combined local gate passed build, typecheck, 2,524 unit tests with one skipped, documentation checks and the public tree audit; 79 focused checks passed with a symlinked TMPDIR. The full browser run exercised all 88 suites. One theme fixture startup timeout cleared on a six-suite follow-up. Final affected server-backed and Git browser checks passed. An integration mismatch initially left local Grep using the lexical default folder; canonicalizing that default fixed its symlinked temporary-directory regression. Real Windows and macOS acceptance remains open.

## 2026-11-08 -- Bound file and web reads before allocation

File context and built-in file tools now read in bounded chunks instead of allocating whole files before truncation. The existing output limits remain: 64 KiB for file references, 256 KiB for Native Read and 32 KiB for local Read. Line offsets still reach later parts of a file. Special files are refused before opening, so a pipe without a writer cannot stall context preparation or a tool read.

`boundedRead.ts` supplies regular-file handle checks, prefix reads and a bounded line scanner. Native and local JavaScript Grep retain later matching lines while reporting lines whose searchable prefix was shortened. Local fast WebFetch reads at most 128 KiB before text extraction and cancels the remaining response. Provider-hosted Native web tools are unchanged. The provider guide, in-app provider and workspace manual sections, and composer context-reference and posture help describe these limits.

Checked on Linux with nine new regression cases and 47 focused checks under a symlinked TMPDIR. The full gate passed build, typecheck, 2,486 unit tests (four skipped), all 88 browser suites, documentation checks and the public tree audit. Initial regressions exposed whole-file allocation and complete response consumption. A byte-count assertion also exposed a scanner reading an entire long line before returning; Read now stops within one fixed chunk of its output budget. Two existing preflight tests paused the obsolete readFile boundary; moving those pauses to open preserved their concurrency assertions.

## 2026-11-08 -- Keep diagnostics and interactive views consistent

Connections can restore a local model address to the runtime default. Saving an empty Model server address clears the saved endpoint while retaining unrelated Ollama options. Activity reports Codex prompt tokens without counting cached input twice; its fresh count excludes cache hits. Session totals normalize each turn before adding it.

Terminal reconnects replace the old display with the server's bounded scrollback replay while keeping the live shell. CREATE WITH FRESH sends the provider shown in its picker from Ollama and llama.cpp anchors. Diagnostics uses the existing Windows executable resolver; Model Health honors ARCO_LLAMACPP_BIN through the runtime binary resolver.

Computer key actions apply native text deletion, horizontal navigation, space insertion, button activation and form submission. Unsupported actions report their limit. Cancelled key events, readonly fields, disabled controls and destructive-action confirmation retain their guards. Cursor movement and deletion preserve complete Unicode characters.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, 2,482 unit tests passing and four skipped under a symlinked temporary directory, all 87 browser suites passing with no skips, documentation checks and the public tree audit. Eight new unit cases cover the fixes; browser scenarios check endpoint reset, native key effects and compact SKILLS visibility. Windows lookup uses synthetic PATH and PATHEXT fixtures.

Initial tests exposed all six defects. Additional keyboard checks caught a Shift+Enter confirmation bypass and split emoji; both were corrected before restarting the gate. Fixtures needed development React and a supported Ollama option. Review corrected the wording for an invalid binary override. SKILLS already sits outside the overflowing toolbar, so that finding does not reproduce and needs no product change.

Repository guides, manual and contextual help agree. Remaining: real Windows and macOS acceptance and the final release gate.

## 2026-11-08 -- Preserve provider CLIs when platform builds are unavailable

Provider CLI updates now stop before installation when the registry confirms that the advertised version lacks its required platform build and no usable fallback can be verified. The current executable and its reported capabilities remain unchanged. The Providers panel reports a deferred update with the reason, so retrying later does not repeatedly install a known incomplete wrapper.

`npmPlatformResolve.ts` distinguishes an unknown registry result from a confirmed missing build. An unreadable version list, an empty list and a list without a usable older platform build return an explicit blocked resolution without an install target. `runCliUpdate` handles that result before invoking npm or probing replacement capabilities. A verified older build still works as the existing fallback. Initial network failures retain the existing unknown-registry behavior. The provider update guide, manual and version-chip help describe the refusal and retry path.

Checked with synthetic registry responses and an injected command runner, covering all three refused fallback states, an available older build, an unknown registry result and preservation of current capabilities. Initial regression tests returned the unavailable latest version and reached the updater. An initial command mock missed a cached custom promisifier and reached npm; the nonexistent fixture version was refused with ETARGET. No installation completed. The corrected test injects a runner and blocks both real execFile and its custom promisifier, failing loudly if either command path is reached. The full Linux gate passed build, typecheck, 2,483 unit tests (four skipped), all 88 browser suites, documentation checks and the public tree audit. All 18 focused provider-update and capability tests passed.

Remaining: real macOS and Windows acceptance.

## 2026-11-08 -- Show the page in cluster preview

A bound page now appears in the cluster PREVIEW panel when the panel sits in a bounded layout. The page fits the available panel space as the window and interface text size change. The member in view still determines which preview and capture actions are used. The single-tab preview retains its existing layout and behavior.

The cluster right panel supplies a flexible, height-limited content area when PREVIEW is selected. The existing preview component can then measure that area and fit its frame. Other cluster panel tabs retain their existing content layout. No control, setting, command, saved format, or contextual help entry changes. The client guide and in-app manual already describe the same preview behavior, and the changelog records the correction.

The cluster-member-panels browser fixture mounts the actual cluster panel in a bounded host and binds a synthetic preview. The new assertion first failed because no frame appeared despite visible controls. With the layout correction, it passed across five window sizes at 100% and 150% interface scale. Existing capture and member-selection checks also passed. The first fixture launch failed to load its modules in the fresh worktree; building the packages resolved that setup issue.

Remaining: the final release branch requires its full local gate and real macOS and Windows acceptance.

## 2026-11-08 -- Keep file selections tied to their displayed location

The ARTIFACTS file tree remains usable when the configured working folder is missing. TREE starts at the first readable location: the configured folder, Projects in the home folder, then the home folder. Directory listings and file previews share that root. This read-only fallback does not register a project or grant write access.

LIVE WORKTREE now renders the index patch against HEAD for STAGED rows. Later unstaged edits cannot replace the staged text or shift its line numbers. Staged deletions remain inspectable without reading a missing working-tree file. CHANGING and NEW FILES keep their current-file previews. The shared DirectoryBrowser permits SAVE HERE and filename Enter only after the displayed destination has loaded, with no listing error or uncommitted path edit. Filename Enter also prevents an enclosing form submission.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, the full unit suite (2,477 passed, four skipped), documentation checks and the public tree audit. All 88 browser suites passed with zero skipped; three Explorer tests also passed with a symlinked TMPDIR. Browser checks cover edited paths, delayed and failed listings, keyboard and mouse agreement, staged patch line numbers and unstaged previews. Initial regressions exposed all three failures. A test fixture required the settings patch API, and its line-number assertion was corrected to match the existing diff renderer.

Remaining: the bounded provider reads and unavailable platform-update findings, plus real macOS and Windows acceptance.

## 2026-11-08 -- Edit pending messages before delivery

Pending messages can return to the message box for editing without replacing text already being written. PENDING offers EDIT beside CANCEL and a PREVIEW view with the same actions. Successful edits append after a blank line and restore the original steer, follow-up or interrupt kind and attached files. The behavior applies to standalone workflows and cluster members.

The server confirms removal from its live queue before the client changes the draft. Delivery and editing use the same synchronous queue boundary: if delivery takes the message first, the draft stays unchanged and the notice says Already sent, nothing to edit. Stable request identities, bounded server replies and client reply matching prevent repeated removal or duplicate restoration during reconnect. Other observers never adopt the editing client's draft.

The composer shows EDITING with the restored kind. SEND or Enter preserves that kind while busy; explicit follow-up and interrupt shortcuts can override it. An idle worker starts an ordinary turn. Sending or clearing the draft removes the marker. Existing staged attachments remain, and restored paths are added without duplicates.

Checked on Linux with eight focused unit cases and browser scenarios covering all three kinds in standalone and cluster tabs, append, change-and-resend, attachments and the delivery race. The full gate passed build, typecheck, 2,446 unit tests (four skipped), all 87 browser suites, documentation checks and the public tree audit. Initial failures demonstrated the missing server response and client edit contract. Browser startup required rebuilding the updated shared package; a filename assertion needed the attachment control. A fixture used a connection-state label absent from the runtime type and was corrected.

Remaining: real macOS and Windows acceptance and the assigned screen findings.

## 2026-11-08 -- Use the managed model endpoint consistently

The llama.cpp port fix now changes the endpoint used throughout the managed server lifecycle. Spawn arguments, listener checks, health requests, loaded state and inference agree on the selected host and port. The final endpoint flag wins, including equals-style flags; malformed ports are rejected before launch. IPv6 hosts receive the required URL brackets.

`llamacppManager.ts` resolves the endpoint from launch arguments and exposes the shared `llamacppBaseUrl` formatter. Both inference and the local pickup evaluator use it. The provider guide, llama.cpp manual, diagnostic-fix help and model-registry help describe endpoint consistency. The existing catalog and diagnostic actions remain the controls for changing launch arguments.

All 28 focused checks, including the Plan/Board/Both matrix, passed under a symlinked temporary directory.

The port-fix fixture first launched with one port while checking another. Shared endpoint resolution made launch, health and consumer assertions agree, including invalid-port refusal and repeated override precedence.

Remaining: real macOS and Windows acceptance and the release gate on the final branch tip. Synthetic fixtures used no paid provider calls.

## 2026-11-08 -- Clear readiness when the managed model process exits

The llama.cpp manager no longer reports a dead managed server as ready. Exit and error events clear the matching loaded state, retain an explanation, and let the next request load a replacement. A late event from an older child cannot invalidate the replacement, and a child that exits during health polling cannot be published as ready.

`llamacppManager.ts` retains child listeners for the entire process lifetime and aborts the losing readiness wait. A failed child remains owned until cleanup when necessary. The provider guide, llama.cpp manual, model-health help and registry help explain readiness recovery. No external process is adopted or stopped by the synthetic checks.

Checked on Linux with the integrated build, typecheck, full unit suite (2,469 passed, 1 skipped), documentation checks and public audit. The transferred full browser gate passed all 87 suites; the integration ran five affected suites. All 28 focused checks, including the Plan/Board/Both matrix, passed under a symlinked temporary directory.

The fake-child fixture first retained ready state after an exit. Lifetime listeners made exit, process-error, replacement ownership and readiness-race cases pass.

Remaining: real macOS and Windows acceptance and the release gate on the final branch tip. Synthetic fixtures used no paid provider calls.

## 2026-11-07 -- Remind busy members about new peer mail

AUTO-PICKUP offers Also remind busy members under Rule-based and Smart: Automatic. The checkbox is off by default and saved for each cluster. When enabled, new actionable unread mail sends the existing busy NUDGE through the member's normal steering queue. The same unread batch sends once, with at least five minutes between reminders for a member. Mail arriving during that gap is checked again by the periodic sweep.

The option travels through `cluster_auto_pickup_set`, cluster metadata and the `autoPickup.remindBusy` state echo. `clusterBusyReminder.ts` applies the acknowledgement filter and tracks unread message identities. Off, Observe-only and expired Smart sessions send no reminders. The turn engine drops a reminder if its original busy turn ends, is replaced or the option is turned off during preparation. Idle automatic pickup keeps its existing rules.

The workflow and cluster creator offer + NEW FOLDER beside Browse another folder. A folder can be created inside a project location and selected immediately for the new session. An existing folder is refused and remains unchanged. Creating a folder does not bookmark it.

The new form asks for a project location and a folder name. It starts with the selected location or the first configured location, and reports invalid names, unavailable locations, and creation failures. The `/api/mkdir` request uses exclusive creation so an existing name cannot be silently reused. The ordinary folder browser keeps its existing behavior. Ollama keeps a successful context-window increase for later tool requests on the same backend. ## 2026-11-07 -- Preserve proposal effects and current task eligibility

Saved orchestration proposals now preserve concurrent creation and rejection decisions. Applying a selection resolves parent tasks first, including parents accepted earlier. After an acceptance-save failure, retrying reuses recorded effects instead of duplicating tasks or comments or repeating an update over a later operator edit. Missing or cyclic proposal dependencies refuse the selection before task creation.

The proposal handlers use one decision lock. Optional task-kernel receipts record each applied item's task id beside its effects; mutation events restore those receipts during recovery. Existing snapshots remain readable. DISPATCH ONCE and DISPATCHER recheck assignment, dependencies, membership, board selection and worker availability before claiming work. Daemon claims also check current arming and capacity limits. Project-board edits from LEDGER refresh all workflows viewing that board, once per shared connection.

New messages are refused before acknowledgement until the folder is ready; STOP cannot interrupt the storage move. Reservations release on failure and success. A message already preparing context also prevents migration.

Plan checkbox saves now compare the displayed body with the saved version and report a conflict instead of replacing intervening edits. Workflow, cluster and library views use the same checked save. Architecture actions append against the current plan or reference, and text-matched member ticks resolve inside the canonical plan mutation. Reference lists follow the selected plan; opening a link checks its saved body, and creating a reference never replaces an existing document.

Plan and reference fields wait while their submitted save is pending. Completion belongs to its original view. Recall saves the submitted distillation draft while keeping later edits open and unsaved. Skill UPDATE fingerprints every installed file, including hidden files and metadata, and checks again before replacement. Skill editor saves and folder operations share a mutation lock. Model catalog changes require the reviewed revision and unique aliases; a refused diagnosis fix does not reload the model.

Cloud snapshots publish complete checksum-named encrypted blobs before switching metadata. Failed publication preserves the active pair, including an ambiguous rename result. Legacy hub blob pairs remain readable. New checksum-named blobs require a compatible hub after a downgrade; local snapshot bundles, plan files and catalog JSON retain their formats.

## 2026-11-07 -- Restrict source launcher stops to owned processes

The Linux source launcher no longer terminates development servers through global process-name patterns. Shutdown requests and signals are limited to processes matching the checkout and Arco home. Recorded process groups also require a matching start identity, so a reused PID is not accepted as the original launcher process.

The launcher records process id, start identity, canonical checkout and home together. Listener cleanup samples process identity around its ownership check before signalling. Recorded groups must still be led by the recorded process. Checkout canonicalization handles directory aliases and trailing separators, and status reads only the PID field from the expanded record. Shell fixtures replace process inspection, signals and HTTP shutdown with synthetic functions.

## 2026-11-07 -- Preserve replay boundaries and finish provider operations

Earlier HISTORY cutoffs now retain the selected conversation outside the recent tail. Cancelled handoffs preserve Native context, while completed handoffs give stateless local providers the new brief and subsequent exchanges without the archived prefix. Literal USER and AGENT lines in messages retain their content and roles. Stopping fresh-summarizer preparation starts no worker, and cancellation cannot publish a successful handoff.

`resumeSummary.ts` streams history through an explicit cutoff and rebuilds older derived caches under the corrected cancellation rule. `exportContext.ts` shares the completed-handoff projection between transcript and tool summaries, checks cancellation before spawn and artifact publication, and uses tagged JSON strings for otherwise ambiguous transcripts. The local parser accepts that representation and legacy text. Codex app-server tracks turn ownership and pending image saves, passes registry definitions at launch and hashes them into its restart signature. Cursor uses streaming UTF-8 decoders, settles compression through the shared completion signal and ties cancellation timers to their operation.

Moving an output now retains both files and reports a conflict when the source is edited or replaced during copying. Archiving checks activity and captured file contents again before removal. Reopening a conversation, queuing an event or writing metadata leaves the live files intact and permits another archive attempt after the activity ends.

`fileVersion.ts` hashes regular files through a bounded buffer and compares identity, size and timestamps. `conversationActivity.ts`, `workflowStore.ts` and `workflowMeta.ts` expose write activity to the archive check. ## 2026-11-07 -- Keep selected skills in mid-turn steering

One-shot skills selected for a busy message now accompany mid-turn steering. The same explicit skill instructions survive whether the provider consumes the message during its current turn or the queue delivers it afterward. A second drain cannot deliver the selected instructions again.

`handleUserMessage` resolves selected skill bodies before admitting a busy send and stores the resulting text with its expanded context. The synchronous `drainSteerForWorker` therefore receives the same complete input as the later queue path. Resolved picks are cleared from the pending metadata to prevent a second expansion. Built-in skill discovery resolves its trust context independently of later turn setup, preserving enabled and replaced-skill checks.

## 2026-11-07 -- Cancel sends interrupted during preflight

STOP cancels ordinary messages that are still preparing prompt or file context. A cancelled send cannot claim a new busy token, start a provider turn or enter a pending queue after preparation finishes. An unchanged send still proceeds normally when no cancellation occurs.

`handleUserMessage` applies its captured cancellation generation to every pending send before queue or dispatch admission, instead of limiting the check to interrupt messages. The guarded-claim callback applies the same check when admission itself waits. The regression holds a synthetic file-context read, revokes the busy generation, and then allows preparation to finish. A matching uncancelled fixture verifies normal delivery.

The provider reference, provider manual, Cancel turn help and changelog describe cancellation during preparation.

## 2026-11-07 -- Keep automatic calibration behind idle admission

Automatic Claude CLI context calibration cannot start alongside a newer primary turn. A session that stops being idle while its context estimate is loading loses calibration admission. Cancellation during probe setup also prevents the calibration subprocess from being created.

`maybeCalibrate` captures session, model, busy-claim and cancellation generations before awaiting transport or context information. It rechecks them before registering the cancellation handle and starting the probe, and rejects stale results. The cancellation handle is installed before asynchronous probe setup. `runCalibration` checks settlement immediately after registration, before spawning the CLI process. Tests intercept subprocess creation so no provider command can run during reproduction.

The provider reference, provider manual, context-fill help and changelog describe the idle-only boundary.

## 2026-11-07 -- Keep session watchers on the current generation

A delayed Claude CLI session load no longer replaces a newer session watcher after reconnect or handoff. Old rows cannot be replayed into the current conversation by a superseded startup. The current session remains the source of live session entries and earlier-history paging state.

## 2026-11-07 -- Preserve newest exchange in deterministic folds

Deterministic Fold preserves the newest complete question and answer together, even when their combined size exceeds the configured tail target. A reply that exceeds the target by itself cannot cause the entire exchange to disappear. The same guarantee applies when that exchange is the whole saved conversation.

`projectKeptTailByTokens` admits the question paired with the newest answer before applying the older-history budget cutoff. `foldInPlace` retains the resulting tail when constructing the summary seed, including the all-history case. The tests cover a short reply, an oversized reply and the persisted Fold event, so the retention claim is checked at both the helper and worker boundaries.

The provider reference, context manual, FOLD help and changelog state the complete-exchange guarantee.

## 2026-11-07 -- Reconnect stale Native MCP sessions

Claude Native retries a failed or closed MCP connection at the next turn. Changes to a registered server's command, arguments or environment also take effect at that boundary. Healthy connections whose launch settings are unchanged remain open across turns.

`McpSession.isClosed` exposes the connection lifecycle without probing a tool. `reconcileMcpSessions` removes unavailable sessions and compares a hash of the launch definition before reuse. Changed definitions close the prior connection before replacement. Session disposal clears both caches. The synthetic MCP child supplies a named tool so repair, reuse, closure and changed arguments can be observed through the actual connection protocol.

The provider reference, provider manual, MCP server help and changelog describe next-turn recovery and settings refresh.

## 2026-11-07 -- Preserve peer attachments until delivery succeeds

Peer messages with attachments now stay pending until every attachment in the read batch has been written successfully. A temporary disk or filesystem error leaves all message payloads available for retry. Files with matching basenames, or names that become identical after sanitization, remain separate. Another delivery preserves files already received or edited locally.

`clusterMailbox.ts` prepares attachment files before consuming mailbox records. Each delivery reserves a private directory and gives each attachment its own numbered folder. Exclusive file creation prevents replacement. Failed preparation removes only that attempt's copies. Successful preparation reuses the returned paths when `peer_read` or pre-turn delivery records the message. Malformed attachment metadata follows the existing bad-record quarantine policy, preserving the original bytes while valid messages continue.

Unsent text remains available when a cluster broadcast cannot leave the browser, when a file save finishes after more editing, and when the phone console refreshes its instance cards. ## 2026-11-07 -- Complete provider responses without losing text or waiting forever

Local generation requests stop waiting for response headers within their deadline, and completed streams finish at the provider's done marker even when the connection stays open. WebSearch and model discovery keep their deadlines active through JSON reading. Automatic preview discovery also bounds stalled candidate requests. CLI snapshots retain later blocks and shorter final answers, prompt arguments remain literal during template expansion, Windows recognizes the installed Demucs executable, and Codex goal budgets count cached input once. Cluster requests refuse existing Cursor sessions that started without peer tools, while fresh Cursor members remain supported.

`requestDeadline.ts` supplies the shared request boundary used by HTTP generation, discovery, WebSearch and preview probing. `StreamParser.ts` tracks snapshot text by message and block. `prompts.ts` substitutes original template tokens in one pass. `demucs.ts` uses the Windows command resolver, while `goalRun.ts` receives provider semantics from `seedWorker.ts`. Provider, primitive, preview, media and goal references were updated with the manual and the affected composer, preview, goal and media help entries. Stream and timeout fixes add no new controls.

## 2026-11-07 -- Claude Native compacts inside a long turn

Arco compacted Claude Native only between turns (`maybeAutoCompactPolicy` returns while `wf.busy`), so one turn with hundreds of tool calls could fill the window and stop by itself. Arco runs the Native request loop, so the worker now checks after each tool round (`maybeCompactInTurn` in `server/src/providers/claude-native/worker.ts`): when that round's request had a prompt at or above the threshold, it compacts before the next request and the same turn continues. The threshold comes from `resolveNativeInTurnCompactPercent` (`server/src/ws/turnSettings.ts`): the tab's or cluster's AUTO-COMPACTION threshold, 70% under Provider, nothing when Off, with Claude API compaction on, or on Chat turns.

The history before the round is summarized with COMPACT's projection and keep-last tail; the round itself stays raw after a user note that the compaction was automatic (`inTurnCompactedHistory`), so every tool_use keeps its result. A summary request too large for the window is made in parts, oldest first. One compaction per crossing; still above, one notice. PreCompact hooks run with trigger auto. A failed or blocked summary changes nothing; CANCEL ends the turn as usual; a steer is delivered after the compacted history. Events carry `trigger`, `percent` and `thresholdPercent`; the client shows notices, no takeover or CONTINUE, and refreshes CTX. Replay rebuilds the note. The manual COMPACT button no longer auto-continues.

Checked with `inTurnCompaction.test.ts` (fake API whose prompt grows past the threshold: compaction between requests, the turn completes, pairs intact, no loop, failure, cancel, Off and beta, steer, replay, parts), `turnSettings.test.ts`, `compactManualContinue.test.ts` (failed before) and `client/src/ws/inTurnCompaction.test.ts`.

Docs: PROVIDERS, PROTOCOL, GOTCHAS, the auto-compaction, CONTINUE and member help, the CONFIG text and the manual.

Remaining: not run against the live API.

`clusterPresets.ts` holds `withFileLock` across preset list mutations. `knowledge/store.ts` shares a project index lock across saves, removals and complete article patches. `clusterMailbox.ts` reads the complete batch before consuming messages and reports malformed records. `clusterStore.ts` copies pending mail before changing membership. `http/imageWriteRoutes.ts` exclusively creates unconfirmed destinations. `media/ffmpeg.ts` encodes in a private staging directory, then publishes without replacing existing files. ## 2026-11-07 -- An open Cursor tab no longer joins a cluster from the tab menu

The previous change made the tab right-click menu use the server's member list, which includes Cursor, so CREATE WITH EXISTING and ADD TO EXISTING accepted an open standalone Cursor tab. That tab would have been a mute member. The Cursor worker passes the cluster peer-tools server in `mcpServers` only when its ACP session starts (`resolveMcpServers` in `server/src/providers/cursor/worker.ts`), and a cached worker is reused as long as it lives, so a tab whose session started outside a cluster cannot call `peer_send` or `peer_read`. The cluster panel's ADD EXISTING (`AddWorkerSection.tsx`) already leaves Cursor out for this reason; fresh Cursor members get the peer tools at their first start.

`TabContextMenu.tsx` now accepts the server's list less Cursor, for the anchor and for the candidates. A Cursor anchor gets its own refusal: an open Cursor tab cannot join because its session started without the peer tools, and Cursor members are added with + ADD FRESH in the cluster panel. The refusal for other providers names Claude, Codex, Ollama and llama.cpp. Respawning the Cursor worker on join was not chosen: it would interrupt a running Cursor turn and has not been checked against a live Cursor session.

Checked with `TabContextMenu.test.tsx`: every accepted provider still seeds a cluster; a Cursor anchor is refused with the new text and a Cursor tab is not offered as a candidate (this case failed with the previous code). Typecheck and `npm run docs:check` pass.

Docs: the manual's cluster section, the cluster-actions and spawn help entries, the SPAWN tooltip, `docs/CLIENT.md`, `docs/GOTCHAS.md` and the CHANGELOG bullet from the previous change.

Remaining: the server still accepts an existing Cursor workflow in `cluster_create` and add-existing requests; only the UI keeps it out.

## 2026-11-07 -- Image tests skip on an ImageMagick that cannot write images

Four image tests in `server/src/providers/attachments.test.ts` (the `deriveModelImage` and `queueModelImage` cases) failed in some local gate runs and passed in others. The cause was the test's ImageMagick probe, not the code under test. The probe accepted the first `magick` that answered `-version`. An ImageMagick install that lacks its coder modules answers `-version` but cannot draw anything ("no decode delegate for this image format `xc:'"), so the fixture generation inside those four tests threw. Which `magick` came first depended on the PATH the unit run inherited: a run whose PATH reached a working ImageMagick passed 7 of 7, a run whose PATH reached only the incomplete one failed 4 of 7. The isolated network namespace made no difference; both results reproduce with and without it.

The probe now draws a 2x2 PNG to standard output and treats a binary that cannot as absent, trying the next candidate. With no working ImageMagick the four tests are skipped with the reason "no ImageMagick on this box that can write images", like on a machine without ImageMagick; with a working one they run and pass.

Checked: with only the incomplete ImageMagick on PATH, 3 pass and 4 skip (before: 4 failed); with a working one first on PATH, 7 of 7 pass, inside and outside the network namespace. Unit-test aggregate and gate as usual.

Remaining: none. Hosted CI does not install ImageMagick; the runner image's own copy, if any, is probed the same way.

## 2026-11-07 -- Browser suites keep private ports and one browser lookup

Several workers can run the full browser set on one machine. `node scripts/smoke/run-browser-suites.mjs` re-executes inside an empty network namespace before any suite starts, so each run's fixed Vite ports stay on that run's loopback. `ARCO_TEST_REQUIRE_NETNS=1` makes a missing namespace a hard stop instead of a shared-port run. `ARCO_TEST_NETNS=0` still opts out and prints that the host network is in use.

Chromium is chosen once. `ARCO_TEST_CHROME` wins. Otherwise `chrome_executable()` in `scripts/smoke/host_isolation.py` reads `CHROMIUM_PATH`, the login account's Playwright cache, or a browser on `PATH`. It does not look under the process home, which suites replace with a temporary directory. Every browser suite launches through that function. When the binary sits in a Playwright cache, the runner also sets `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` to that cache so a launch without an explicit binary does not search the temporary home.

The gate command is `env ARCO_TEST_REQUIRE_NETNS=1 node scripts/smoke/run-browser-suites.mjs`.

Checked with `python3 scripts/smoke/host_isolation_test.py` (3 passed) and `node --test scripts/smoke/browser-suite-isolation.test.mjs` (a fake `unshare` that exits nonzero fails the required-namespace check before any suite). Two full sets were started together from two checkouts. Both ran in an isolated namespace. Neither reported a port already in use, and neither failed to find Chromium. Each finished 83 of 87 suites. The same four failed in both: `add-worker-identity` and `member-settings` recorded `/api/public-project/choice` as a blocked call, `home-start-page` timed out waiting for `#home-title`, and `artifact-copy` did not see `publicRepository` true. An early second checkout failed its dependency scan until the shared package was built.

Remaining: a host that cannot create a user namespace must set `ARCO_TEST_NETNS=0`, and two full sets on that host can still collide on fixed ports. The four suite failures above are product assertions, not port or browser lookup failures.

## 2026-11-07 -- Five fixes from the documentation sweep

The documentation sweep listed five suspected code bugs. Four were real; the fifth was a wrong comment.

Remote lifecycle on macOS (`scripts/agent/allowlist.ts`): the templates used the label `com.arco.app`, while `arco service install` writes the LaunchAgent `arco-workbench`. The agent passed no user id, so commands addressed `gui/0`, and stop used `launchctl bootout`, which unloads the job so a later `kickstart` fails. The label now defaults to `arco-workbench`, the agent passes its own uid, stop is `launchctl kill SIGTERM` like `arco service stop`, and without a uid macOS commands render nothing. New `scripts/agent/allowlist.test.ts` compares the target with the label in the generated plist; three of its four cases failed before.

GENESIS tab: DISARM sat inside the established-codebase warning, which small projects never get and which is not stored, so a reload hid it. The armed box now always shows DISARM. `GenesisTab.test.tsx` failed before.

Tab right-click menu: a local provider list left out Cursor, so Cursor tabs were refused with text saying only Claude or Codex work. It now uses the shared `isClusterMemberProvider`. `TabContextMenu.test.tsx` renders every accepted provider; both cases failed before. The SPAWN tooltip now states the real limit.

Backup script: usage said `arco backup`, which does not exist. It now says `npx tsx scripts/arco-backup.ts`; BACKUP.md and GETTING_STARTED.md say the package does not ship it. `scripts/backup/cli.test.ts` failed before.

The `mediaUnconfined` comment in `server/src/settings.ts` now says undefined means confined, as the routes behave.

Docs: REMOTE_MANAGEMENT, GOTCHAS, GENESIS, CLIENT, BACKUP, GETTING_STARTED, the manual and help entries for Creative mode, cluster actions and spawning into a cluster.

## 2026-11-07 -- Non-canonical request paths are refused before the session check

The session gate (`checkHttpAuth` in `server/src/http/wsAuth.ts`) decides from the raw request path whether a route needs the cookie. Several route modules, such as the storage routes, read `req.url` through `new URL()`, which resolves `..` segments (also written `%2e%2e`), backslashes, a leading `//` (read as a host) and absolute-form URLs. A cookie-free `GET /x/../api/storage/policy` therefore looked like a page path to the gate and reached the protected storage handler, which answered 200.

`handleHttpRequest` in `server/src/http/router.ts` now refuses such a path with 400 before the origin and session checks (`isCanonicalRequestPath`). Browsers never send these forms, so pages, the Vite proxy and the preview proxy are unaffected. Every later check and route reads the same path.

Checked with a new case in `server/src/http/routerAuth.test.ts` that sends eight non-canonical forms through the real router without a cookie: `/x/../api/storage/policy` answered 200 before the fix; all now answer 400, `/api/storage/policy` still answers 401 and `/healthz` 200. The session, origin and host isolation tests pass unchanged.

Docs: `docs/HTTP_API.md` and the CHANGELOG. The manual and help did not change; no control changed.

Remaining: none.

## 2026-11-07 -- Notification and utility routes behind the session; ntfy token bound to its server

`handleHttpRequest` in `server/src/http/router.ts` dispatched four route modules before the session-cookie gate: smart pickup, tidy, the utility agent and notification channels. A local client without the cookie could read or change Settings > Notifications, start utility-agent calls on the configured providers, or run a notification TEST. The TEST merged the caller's address over the saved settings and kept the saved ntfy token, so the token went to a server of the caller's choice.

The four modules now run after the gate, like every other `/api` route. `/api/app/version` stays before it with the health probes, because a page whose session ended with a restart uses it to notice the new server. `sanitizeNotificationChannels` in `shared/src/protocol/notifications.ts` keeps a saved token only when the new address has the same scheme, host and port as the saved one. A TEST or a save with another server does not carry the token, and saving another server without entering a token clears it.

Checked with `server/src/http/routerAuth.test.ts`, which sends cookie-free requests through the real router with a recording notification stub. Six protected routes answered 200 before the fix and the stub received the token for the other server; now they answer 401, nothing is sent, and the health and version probes still answer 200. A second test pins the token rule. Both failed before and pass after; the session, origin, attention and notification-channel tests pass.

Docs: `docs/HTTP_API.md`, `docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md`, the CHANGELOG, the manual (Settings > Notifications steps) and the contextual help for the Notifications settings.

Remaining: none.

## 2026-11-07 -- Plan MOVE keeps an edit saved while it runs

MOVE in ARTIFACTS > PLANS (`POST /api/plans/move`, `movePlan` in `server/src/planStore.ts`) read the source plan, wrote that copy to the target project, copied the reference documents, then removed the source. The source lock was taken only for the removal. An edit saved in between (a body update from the Plan tab or an agent, or a reference document written through the API) was acknowledged, then deleted with the source, while the target kept the older text.

The removal now happens only while the source still matches what the target holds: text, title, done state and the reference documents, compared under the source's index lock. `writePlanRef` and `deletePlanRef` now take that lock too, as `updatePlan` already did. When an edit landed after the copy, the target is brought up to date and the check runs again. After three changing rounds the move stops with an error; the source keeps everything and the target copy is removed if it is still exactly what MOVE wrote. The two locks are still never held together, so opposite moves cannot deadlock.

Checked with `planStore.moveRace.test.ts`, which holds MOVE at its reference copy and saves an edit meanwhile: a body edit, a reference document written after the copy, and a plain move. The first two failed before the fix (the target held `OLD`, the edit was gone) and pass after; the plan and reference tests pass unchanged.

Docs: `docs/PERSISTENCE.md`, `docs/HTTP_API.md` and the CHANGELOG. The manual and help did not change; no control changed.

Remaining: reference files written with plain file tools do not take the lock.

---

## 2026-11-07 -- The first event after a torn log record is no longer lost

A crash or a failed append can leave a conversation's `events.jsonl` ending in an incomplete record without its newline. `initWorkflowStore` in `server/src/workflowStore.ts` skipped that line when it looked for the last sequence number, but nothing fixed the end of the file. The next event was appended right after the torn bytes, so it shared their line, failed to parse and was skipped on every replay: the first message after the restart was saved but never shown.

Before the first append, `repairLiveTail` now checks the last byte of the live file. A last record that parses and only lacks its newline gets the newline. An incomplete one is written to `events.jsonl.partial-<time>` beside the log and flushed, then cut from the live file, which then ends on a record boundary. A log written by a newer release is left untouched, as that release's log is not written to. Removing the torn bytes from the live file also keeps a later seal from putting an unparseable line into a segment, which the segment check would reject.

Checked with `workflowStore.partialTail.test.ts`: a torn record followed by a restart and one saved message, a complete record without its newline, and a log holding only a torn record. All three failed before the fix (the new message missing from replay) and pass after; the kept file holds exactly the torn bytes.

Docs: `docs/PERSISTENCE.md` (crash semantics) and the CHANGELOG. The manual and help did not change.

Remaining: none.

---

## 2026-11-07 -- Older logs keep records with reused numbers through sealing and unseal

Logs written before 0.1.5 can hold live records whose numbers repeat or go back: a checkpoint record carried its checkpoint's number, and REVERT TO HERE or a restart right after it reused numbers. Two places decided by number alone what was already sealed. `arco storage unseal` (`unsealLog` in `server/src/eventLog.ts` and `scripts/packaging/storage.mjs`) dropped every live record not numbered above all earlier ones, wrote the shorter history and deleted the segments. Readers and the seal skipped every leading live record at or below the newest sealed number, so records reused right after a seal were hidden, and the next seal removed them from the live log without sealing them.

Now a live record counts as already sealed only when it is a copy: the same line as a record in a sound segment, each copy used once, among the leading live records (`SealedCopies`). That still covers the copied newest record and a seal that stopped before replacing the live file. Every reader, the seal and both unseal exports use the rule. An older record that would start a segment sorting before an existing one stops the seal; the log stays plain and nothing changes. A damaged segment keeps its old handling.

Checked with `eventLog.legacySeqs.test.ts`: the two reproductions from the bug sweep through six readers, the seal and both exports, the stopped seal, and an interrupted seal. Three of the four tests failed before the fix; the existing event-log, damage and storage tests pass unchanged.

Docs: `docs/PERSISTENCE.md` and the CHANGELOG. The manual and help did not change.

Remaining: a log that stops sealing this way grows as a plain file; no repair is offered.

---

## 2026-11-07 -- A segment that vanishes during a read no longer stops the server

A full-history reader (`readLogLines` in `server/src/eventLog.ts`, used by replay, search, usage and outputs) lists the sealed segments of a conversation, then opens them one by one. When a segment was removed or replaced in between, for example by ARCHIVE or DELETE in the STORAGE view, the file's open error was emitted on a stream that nobody listened to. Node treats that as an unhandled error and the server process exited. The reader's own handling for unreadable segments, which reports the segment and skips it, never saw the error.

The read stream's error now goes to the decompressor, so it reaches the reader's loop and becomes the usual `SegmentReadError`: reported once, the segment skipped, the rest of the history read. `arco storage unseal` (`scripts/packaging/storage.mjs`) had the same pattern; it now stops for that conversation with a message, keeps its segments and continues with the others, as for any other damaged segment.

Checked with `eventLog.segmentVanish.test.ts`: two segments, the second deleted after the first record was read, and an unseal where one segment name cannot be opened. Both tests ended the run with the unhandled `ENOENT` and `EISDIR` errors before the fix and pass after. The existing event-log tests pass unchanged.

Docs: `docs/PERSISTENCE.md` and the CHANGELOG. The manual and help did not change; no control changed.

Remaining: none.

## 2026-11-07 -- New cluster members no longer show as their own tabs

Creating a cluster showed every new member as its own top-level tab, one after another, each taking focus, until the cluster tab opened and folded them in. ADD WORKERS and REPLACE did the same for the new member. The cause is the message order, unchanged since 0.1.4: the server announces each member with `workflow_created` while it creates them, and sends `cluster_created` (or `cluster_state` for an existing cluster) only after the last one. The client treated each announcement as a new standalone tab and switched to it. How long the tabs stay visible depends on how long the server takes per member.

The four creation paths in `server/src/ws/clusterHandlers.ts` now mark these announcements with `clusterMemberPending`. The client stores it as `JobState.pendingClusterMember`, keeps the current focus, and leaves the job out of the tab bar (`standaloneTabJobs` in `client/src/utils/homeModel.ts`) and out of Home. The cluster state that lists the member clears the flag. A `cluster_*` error, from a create, add or replace that failed after members were made, shows them as standalone tabs as before.

Checked with a new browser suite, `cluster-create-tabs`: the real TabBar receives a 7-member create, ADD WORKERS and REPLACE in the server's order and checks every frame and every tab bar change. Before the fix it found 103 problems (member tabs and member focus); after, none. `clusterMemberPending.test.ts` checks the same in the store and Home and the failure case; `clusterMemberCreation.test.ts` checks that all four server paths set the flag. Both failed before the fix.

Docs: `docs/PROTOCOL.md`, `docs/PROTOCOL_CLUSTER.md`, `docs/CLIENT.md` and the CHANGELOG. The manual and help did not change; no control changed.

Remaining: none. Checked live on a real server: no; the fixture suite uses the real client code with messages in the server's order.

## 2026-11-07 -- Extending guide describes the shaders as the code builds them

The "Adding a shader" section of `docs/EXTENDING.md` told contributors to import a new shader in `client/src/themes/shaders/index.ts`, which does not exist, and gave a GLSL 3.00 template with `uAccentPrimary`, `uAccentSecondary` and an `out vec4 fragColor`. A shader written from that template would compile against uniforms nothing sets.

`client/src/components/ThemedBackgroundShaderImpl.tsx` imports every `.frag.glsl` file as a raw string and maps it to its preset in `FRAGMENT_FOR_PRESET`; the preset id lives in `ShaderPreset` in `client/src/themes/types.ts`. Each material gets `uResolution`, `uTime`, `uTension` (0 calm to 1 tense, set by the `arco:tension` event) and `uPrimaryColor`, `uSecondaryColor` and `uBgColor` from the theme's accent and background colors, and the vertex shader passes `vUv`. The shipped shaders, for example `aurora.frag.glsl`, use `precision highp float`, `varying vec2 vUv` and `gl_FragColor`. The section now gives those steps, that template and that uniform list. The constraints list (high precision, bounded hash inputs, low instruction count, no smooth gradients on dark themes) is unchanged.

The neighbouring "Adding a right-panel tab" steps were checked against `client/src/components/RightPanel/index.tsx` and `types.ts` (`TABS`, `GROUPS`, `RightTab`, `STORAGE_KEY`) and match.

Checked: `npm run docs:check` and `node scripts/public-audit.mjs --tree` pass. No shader or product code changed; the template was not compiled.

## 2026-11-06 -- Questions and answers stay in place in the chat; reply rows show their own time

In a Codex turn that asked a question, the question card and the answer appeared below all of the turn's text, and every reply row showed the turn's start time. Text after the question was appended to the open row above the card, and rows took `turnStartedAt` as their time.

Now any row that arrives between two parts of the turn's text splits it. That covers a question card, its answer (a steer), a peer message or a system row. Text that follows such a row starts a new row below it (`hasBoundaryAfter` in `client/src/ws/handleServerMessage.ts`). A closed row is never reopened past one, and queued follow-ups do not split.

The server now sends each event's write time as `eventTs`, on live broadcasts and on every replay (`persistAndStampSeq`, `replayEvents`, `replayTailEvents`). The disk record is unchanged. A reply row takes the time of its first event (without `eventTs`, the turn start as before), and so does a steer that splits a row.

The new browser suite `chat-timeline-order` plays one turn: text, a question, more text, the answer, then more text. It plays it live and as one replay batch. Rows and times must match in both. Before the change it failed: the text merged above the card, and all rows showed the turn start. It passes now. A new `eventLog.test.ts` case checks `eventTs` on the live copy, the full replay and the tail replay, and that it is absent from the disk record. That case failed before and passes now.

Docs: `docs/PROTOCOL.md` and the CHANGELOG; the manual and help did not describe the old placement.

Remaining: browser-saved rows keep their old times until reloaded from the server. Not checked on a live Codex question.
