## 2026-11-06 -- Plain gives a complete final reply; Terse takes the former Plain text

OUTPUT STYLE keeps its three levels, and Plain stays the default. Plain was terser than intended, so it is loosened. It has no word limit per sentence; long sentences are split instead. "No recap" became "do not repeat earlier messages". Progress lines during tool runs stay short, but they say why when the approach changes. The last reply of every turn now has to be complete. It covers what was done, what was checked and how, and what was found, with exact numbers. It also covers the assumptions made, the risks, what is left with the next step, and anything that needs the operator.

Terse is now exactly the former Plain block, with sentences of about 20 words, no recap and one line per tool phase. The old Terse lines that let articles and full sentences go are removed. Saved values are unchanged: a tab saved as `terse` gets the new Terse text, and nothing is migrated. Both blocks are constants in `server/src/providers/outputStyle.ts`, so the prompt prefix stays byte-identical from turn to turn.

`outputStyle.test.ts` checks the following on both blocks: the safety rules (negations, numbers, verbatim code and errors, language, persisted text) and that no articles are dropped. It checks that only Plain has the final-reply rule, that Terse is the shorter block, and that both stay within their word limits. Against the previous text the test fails twice; it passes now.

The CONFIG, creator and member pickers show the new one-line hint. The CONFIG help entry, the manual's Output and thinking section, `docs/PROVIDERS.md` and the CHANGELOG entry describe both levels. The `selected-control-contrast` browser suite reads the new hint.

Remaining: none. Checked on a live model: no.

## 2026-11-06 -- Event numbers stay unique after REVERT TO HERE; older logs read in file order

Chat history now stays complete and in order after REVERT TO HERE. The cause was in `persistAndStampSeq` (`server/src/workflowStore.ts`): the saved line spread the message after the envelope, so a `seq` in the payload replaced the log's own number on disk. Every `checkpoint_created` was saved with its guard number, repeating the previous record's, and `checkpoint_reverted` with the restored checkpoint's old number. After a restart the log continued from that record and reused numbers already in it; the browser keys rows by number, so old and new rows interleaved and some disappeared.

The log now assigns every number and drops a payload `seq`; checkpoint events carry `checkpointSeq` and `restoredSeq`, and HISTORY reads `checkpointSeq`, or the ref for older events. Logs saved before are not rewritten: the readers give each record the number max(own, previous + 1) in file order, read the whole history when numbers drift, and new records continue after the last of them. Sealing no longer treats a legacy segment with repeated numbers as damaged, never drops a live record past the leading copied ones, and starts segments only where numbers rise.

Checked on Linux with a log shaped like the one found on the release candidate (repeats, a backward revert record and reused numbers), a revert followed by more events and a restart, a legacy sealed segment sealed again across a restart, and HISTORY parsing of old and new checkpoint events. The new tests failed before the change. Build, typecheck, unit suite, tree audit and documentation check ran on the final tip.

Remaining: the first use of a conversation after a start scans its whole history once.

## 2026-11-06 -- Output styles: language, code and error rules

Plain and Terse gain three shared rules. Replies stay in the operator's language, or the language asked for, and quoted text never switches it; technical terms stay as written. A code change in a chat reply shows the changed lines with one or two lines of context, and the whole file only when asked, when it is new or when most of it changes. An error is quoted by its shortest decisive line, exactly, instead of the whole log. The rules live in `COMMON_RULES` in `server/src/providers/outputStyle.ts`, so both levels carry them and each block stays a constant, which keeps every provider's prompt prefix byte-stable.

Plain is now 234 words (1,459 bytes) and Terse 277 words. The size check in `outputStyle.test.ts` now allows up to 240 words for Plain. The text test asserts the three new lines on both levels and fails on the previous text. `docs/PROVIDERS.md` lists the rules beside the negation and verbatim rules. No control, setting or default changed, so the help, the manual and `CHANGELOG.md` are unchanged.

Checked on Linux: typecheck, the unit suite, documentation checks and the public tree audit.

Remaining: no measurement against a live model.

## 2026-11-06 -- Output styles: Normal, Plain and Terse on every provider

OUTPUT STYLE now offers NORMAL, PLAIN and TERSE in CONFIG, the New workflow and cluster creator, and the ADD WORKERS and REPLACE member settings. Plain is the default: a tab, member, preset or template without a saved choice uses it, while a saved Normal stays Normal. Lite became Plain, and a saved `lite` reads as `plain` on every read path (settings, presets, templates, `meta.json`, member reconstruction and `update_primary_config`). The default lives in one constant, `DEFAULT_OUTPUT_STYLE`, and is stored as unset.

The addenda were rewritten. Plain asks for the answer first, full sentences of about 20 words at most, one name per thing and no filler. Terse lets articles go where a sentence still reads in one pass. Both keep every negation, number, unit and verbatim string, and leave code, comments, commits, docs, plans, board tasks and agent mail uncompressed. The persona line, the "when in doubt" rule and the output-saving claim are gone.

Claude CLI, Codex exec, Cursor (ACP and print), Ollama, llama.cpp, LM Studio, Strata and DeepSeek now apply the style at the end of their stable prompt prefix or system message. Primitives still force Normal; a guard test lists their spawn sites.

Checked on Linux: build, typecheck, unit suite (4 image tests fail only under a cleared environment), documentation checks, tree audit, and the selected-control-contrast, creator, member-settings and help browser suites. Provider tests use fake Claude and Codex commands, the fake `cursor-agent` and the message builders. 0.1.4 run against a saved `plain`: settings drop the field and keep the rest, and `meta.json` runs Normal.

Remaining: no measurement of either level against a live model.

A one-time capture of `cursor-agent acp` (initialize, `session/new`, model selection and `session/load`, no prompt) showed why: `--model` at spawn is ignored, every new session reports `default[]`, and only the advertised ids, one per model with the CLI's saved settings, are accepted. ## 2026-11-06 -- Read process start times off the server thread

Starting a worker or a terminal on Windows no longer pauses the server. Since 0.1.3 each registration in `worker-pids.json` and `terminal-pids.json` ran PowerShell synchronously, for up to eight seconds, to record the process start time; on a timeout the record kept no identity, so a leftover after a crash was never cleaned up. macOS ran `ps` the same way.

The start time is now read when the process is registered: on Linux from `/proc` at once, on macOS and Windows by an asynchronous query, batched for every process started together, with a 20-second limit and up to three tries. The answer is accepted only while Arco has not yet seen the child exit; until then its pid cannot be reused, because Node holds the Windows process handle and the POSIX child stays unreaped. Both registries share `server/src/workers/durablePidRegistry.ts`. The boot sweep is asynchronous, asks for every recorded pid in one query and is awaited before `listen()`. Shutdown runs no query: held children are killed, and older records wait for the next boot. The file format is unchanged, so records written by 0.1.4 are still cleaned up.

Before the change, the old Windows probe stalled the event loop for its full two seconds against a fake slow `powershell.exe`. New tests measure the event loop while a slow query runs, establish a real child's identity on each system, end a failing query in one logged refusal with no kill, discard answers that arrive after the child exited, and sweep 0.1.4 and legacy files. The PowerShell script and the `ps` query were also run for real.

Checked on Linux: build, typecheck, the unit suite with a cleared environment and a symlinked temporary directory, `smoke:terminal`, the documentation checks and the tree audit.

Remaining: hosted macOS and Windows verification.

## 2026-11-06 -- Keep the AUTO-PICKUP chain-depth limit when mail lands as a turn ends

Rule-based AUTO-PICKUP no longer restarts a loop held at the chain-depth limit when a recipient's turn ends just as mail for it arrives. Before, the member woke at depth 1 and the loop ran on until the per-minute ceiling.

A `peer_send` writes the mail file and then broadcasts `peer_message_sent`; only the broadcast recorded the mail's chain depth. The turn-end re-check in `server/src/server/broadcast.ts` and the periodic resume sweep in `server/src/clusterAutoTurn.ts` read the mailbox and used the recorded depth, 0 for mail found in that gap. Both now use `heldMailDepth()`: the recorded depth, or the depth of the sender's running turn when that is higher, which is the depth the broadcast will stamp.

Hosted Linux CI, Node 26, failed the turn-boundary ring test with four wakes instead of three. Running the test file 48 times in parallel under Node 26.13.0, reproduced it in one of 96 runs: the second member's turn ended between the third turn's mail write and its broadcast, and the resume woke it at depth 1. A new test reproduces that order without timing: the sender stands at depth 3, the mail is written while the recipient is busy, and the recipient's turn ends before any broadcast. It failed before the change (a wake at depth 1) and passes now. After the change, 288 parallel runs of the file under Node 26 passed.

Checked on Linux: build, typecheck, the unit suite with a cleared environment and a symlinked temporary directory, the documentation checks and the tree audit. The changelog and the cluster protocol guide describe the gap; the in-app manual already says held mail does not wake the member, which now holds here too.

Remaining: hosted verification.

## 2026-11-06 -- Wait for background Bash commands on tab close; close the recall index in the auto-handoff test

Closing or handing off a Claude Native tab now also waits for background Bash commands (`run_in_background`) to end, as it already does for background hooks. Before, dispose sent the kill and returned at once; on Windows a command could keep the project folder in use.

In `server/src/providers/claude-native/tools.ts` each shell registration carries an `ended` promise: the process has closed and every kill sent until then has finished (on Windows, after `taskkill` exits). The worker's `dispose` collects these from the shell map before clearing it and waits for them together with the hook processes, for at most 15 seconds. A new hook-parity test starts `sleep 60` as a background command, disposes the worker, and checks that the process is gone. It failed before the change and passes now.

Hosted Windows, Node 24, again failed to remove the auto-handoff test folder with `EPERM`. Listing this process's open files at teardown on Linux showed the cause: the test folder is also the test's Arco home, and `writeMeta` opens the machine-wide recall index there (`recall.db` and its `-wal` and `-shm` files) and keeps it open. The failed fixture handoff leaves nothing open. The test now uses the existing `trackRecallFixture` helper, closes the index after the pending updates, asserts on Linux that no file in the folder is still open, and removes the folder without retries. With the index left open, the new assertion fails and names the three files.

Checked on Linux: build, typecheck, the unit suite with a cleared environment and a symlinked temporary directory, the documentation checks and the tree audit. The PROVIDERS guide, the in-app manual and the changelog cover background commands.

Remaining: hosted Windows verification.

## 2026-11-06 -- End background hooks before a Claude Native tab closes

Closing a Claude Native tab, or handing it off, now ends every background hook (`async`, `asyncRewake` and Notification hooks) that is still running, with the processes it started, and the worker waits until they have ended. An `async` hook has no timeout, so before this change a slow one could outlive the tab; on Windows the tree kill ran in the background and a leftover process kept the project folder in use.

`signalChildTree` in `server/src/workers/processKill.ts` now returns a promise that settles once the kill is done: at once on Linux and macOS, and on Windows after the Git Bash process listing and `taskkill` have finished. `server/src/providers/claude-native/hooks.ts` records every hook process in a set until it has closed and any kill sent to it has finished. The worker's `dispose` aborts the hooks, as before, and then waits on that set, for at most 15 seconds.

The hook parity test helper now awaits `dispose` before deleting its temporary folder, with no retries. Hosted Windows, Node 26, failed there with `EPERM` because the second turn's `async` hook still held the folder. A new test starts an `async` hook that runs `sleep 60`, disposes the worker, and checks that the hook's process is gone when `dispose` returns. It failed before the change ("Missing expected exception: the hook process is gone") and passes now. On Windows the folder deletion is the check.

Checked on Linux: build, typecheck, the unit suite with a cleared environment and a symlinked temporary directory, the documentation checks and the public tree audit. The PROVIDERS guide, the in-app PROVIDERS manual and the Unreleased changelog say when background hooks end. No control changed, so no contextual help entry applies.

Remaining: hosted Windows verification.

## 2026-11-06 -- Hook parity remainders and the Windows tree kill

The first hosted Windows run failed seven tests; five come from the three defects fixed here. A timed-out or cancelled Git Bash command or hook kept running for its full 30 seconds: `taskkill /T` follows Windows parent links, and an MSYS background job loses its parent when the forked copy exits. A hook printing JSON with a Windows path lost its `updatedInput`, because Node quotes arguments for the Microsoft C runtime and Git Bash halves each pair of backslashes inside quotes. A test removed its temporary folder while a file write was still open.

`spawnShellChild()` in `server/src/workers/processKill.ts` now passes Git Bash arguments in MSYS quoting on a verbatim command line and tags the child's environment with `ARCO_PROCESS_TREE`; `signalChildTree()` lists every process holding the tag through Git Bash's `/proc` and ends them with the child. Hooks, both Bash tools and sandbox shell scripts use it.

Claude Native hooks also gained the remaining fields: `if` (one permission rule, `hookIf.ts`), `transcript_path`, absolute file paths, `agent_id` and `agent_type` from the Task tool's `subagent_type`, PreCompact before COMPACT and FOLD, and Notification for `permission_prompt` and `idle_prompt`.

Tests: `hookIf.test.ts` follows the reference tables; six new turns in `hookParity.test.ts` fail on the previous commit; `processKill.msys.test.ts` models how Git Bash reads its command line and runs the sweep on a real tree; the three timeout tests now also check that a background job left no file behind. Checked with build, typecheck, the unit suite under a symlinked `TMPDIR` and a cleared environment, documentation checks, the tree audit and browser suites. The Windows paths are proven only by the next hosted run.

Remaining: SessionStart, SubagentStart, PostToolBatch and the other newer events are not fired.

## 2026-11-06 -- Claude Native hooks follow Claude Code's hooks reference

Arco's Claude Native hooks differed from Claude Code: any non-zero exit blocked PreToolUse and UserPromptSubmit, a timeout blocked, Stop and SubagentStop were informational, `permissionDecision` was parsed and ignored, `updatedInput` was merged, PostToolUse replaced the result on `decision: "block"` and showed exit-0 stderr to the model, and `onFailure`, `async` and `asyncRewake` were not read. On Windows without Git Bash the shell tool was still called Bash.

`evaluateHooks()` in `hooks.ts` now reads every result by the reference's table (exit 0 JSON, exit 2, other exits, a valid JSON object with any exit, failures and `onFailure`, the Windows rule for shell-form PreToolUse hooks), and the worker acts on the verdict per event. Stop and SubagentStop keep the model working with their reason, up to `CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP` times in a row; SubagentStop now fires in the Task child. `ask` parks the call on the review gate card with category `hook-ask`. Background hooks report through the worker; an asyncRewake exit 2 on an idle tab is queued as a follow-up and drained. On Windows without Git Bash the tool is `PowerShell`; `isShellToolName()` in `@arco/shared` replaces every `=== 'Bash'` check, and the hardline floor learned the PowerShell and cmd forms of its hazards.

Tests: `hookParity.test.ts` drives full turns with a fake client (UserPromptSubmit, Stop, SubagentStop, PreToolUse JSON, ask, continue:false, async, asyncRewake), checks the reference table and matcher rules, and runs every safety check against the PowerShell tool; all ten fail on the previous commit. Existing hook tests were updated to the new behavior. Checked with build, typecheck, the unit suite under a symlinked `TMPDIR` and a cleared environment, documentation checks, the tree audit and browser suites.

Remaining: `Notification` and `PreCompact` are accepted but not fired; the `if` field and `transcript_path` are not provided.

## 2026-11-06 -- PowerShell fallback and the hook `shell` and `args` fields

On Windows without Git Bash, Claude Code runs shell commands and hooks in PowerShell, and its hooks accept `"shell"` and the exec form (`"args"`). Arco skipped hooks and failed the Bash tool there instead, and ignored both fields everywhere: an exec-form hook ran its program under `sh` reading the JSON input as code, and a `"shell": "powershell"` hook ran in `/bin/sh` and blocked with exit 127.

`server/src/commandShell.ts` now falls back to PowerShell (`pwsh.exe`, then Windows PowerShell 5.1) and returns a notice that is shown once as information, in the tab and in Connections > Claude. `shellInvocation()` passes PowerShell commands base64-encoded, or through a temporary `.ps1` when too long for a command line, with UTF-8 input and output; a failing program's exit code becomes the command's. `hookLaunch()` in `hooks.ts` starts exec-form hooks with no shell and `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}` filled in, honors `"shell"`, and rewrites the path placeholders to `${env:NAME}` for PowerShell. Both Bash tools name the shell in their description, so the model writes PowerShell when that is what runs. Sandbox shell scripts still need Git Bash.

Tests: faked lookups for every platform; real runs of an exec-form hook, a `"shell": "powershell"` hook with UTF-8 input, and the fallback for hooks and both Bash tools, including a long command and a timeout. The PowerShell runs are skipped where none is installed; locally they ran against a portable pwsh 7.6.9. Checked with build, typecheck, the unit suite under a symlinked `TMPDIR` and a cleared environment, documentation checks, the tree audit and the help and connections browser suites.

Remaining: any non-zero exit still blocks a PreToolUse hook (Claude Code blocks only on 2), and `onFailure` and `async` are not read.

## 2026-11-06 -- Shell commands and hooks run in Git Bash on Windows

On Windows the Claude Native Bash tool, Claude Native hooks and the local-model Bash tool spawned `/bin/sh`, which does not exist there. Every hook failed to start, the spawn error counted as a block, and every tool call a configured hook matched was refused; the Bash tools failed outright. Hosted CI showed it in four `toolChecks.test.ts` tests on the Windows runners. Arco 0.1.4 behaves the same.

`server/src/commandShell.ts` now picks the shell: `/bin/sh` on Linux and macOS as before, Git Bash on Windows as in Claude Code. Its public docs name Git Bash for Bash commands and shell-form hooks, with `CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH` as the override. Arco checks that variable, then the Git install that `git` on `PATH` belongs to, then Program Files, the per-user folder and Scoop. It never uses the WSL `bash.exe` in System32 or WindowsApps. Without Git Bash the Bash tools return the reason and the fix, a hook is marked `notRun` and no longer blocks, the tab gets one `[HOOKS]` notice per reason, and Connections > Claude shows the problem. Arco does not fall back to PowerShell for hooks and does not read the hook `shell` or `args` fields. Timeout, cancel and KillShell end the command tree with `taskkill /T /F` on Windows (`signalChildTree` in `workers/processKill.ts`); the sandbox's shell scripts use the same shell and kill.

Unit tests resolve the shell from faked files and environment on every platform. The hook tests, a Bash tool test with a tree-killing timeout and the local-model Bash tool test now run for real on all three systems; no-shell tests check that hooks do not block, the notice appears once and the Bash tools name what is missing.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, the unit suite under a symlinked `TMPDIR` and a cleared environment, documentation checks, the tree audit and the help and connections browser suites. Windows is checked by the hosted run and the release candidate.

Remaining: the operator's Windows check of the release candidate.

## 2026-11-06 -- Early-exiting Claude Native hooks no longer crash the server

A Claude Native hook that exits before reading its JSON input, such as a PreToolUse hook that only prints a reason and runs `exit 2`, could crash the Arco server. `runHookOnce` in `server/src/providers/claude-native/hooks.ts` writes the payload to the hook's stdin; when the hook has already closed the pipe, Node reports EPIPE as an asynchronous `error` event on stdin. The surrounding try/catch cannot see it, and with no listener it became an uncaught exception. Hosted CI showed it on Linux in `toolChecks.test.ts`, where a blocking hook won the race against the write. Arco 0.1.4 has the same code.

stdin now has an `error` listener that ignores the broken pipe. The hook's real exit code, stdout and stderr still arrive through `close`, so a blocking hook still blocks with its own message. The hooks module is the only Claude Native spawn site that writes to a child's stdin; the search tool leaves stdin unused, and the Bash tool and sandbox ignore it.

A new test in `hooks.test.ts` fires a hook that exits at once against an 8 MB payload, five times, and checks exit code 2, the stderr text and an empty shell registry. Without the listener it fails with `write EPIPE`.

Checked with build, typecheck, the unit suite under a symlinked `TMPDIR` and a cleared environment, documentation checks and the tree audit.

Remaining: other providers write to long-lived child processes without a stdin error listener; listed for a separate review.

## 2026-11-06 -- Settings survives an unexpected browser-list reply

Settings opened blank when `GET /api/app-browsers` answered with something other than a browser report. The App window browser section in Settings > Advanced read `report.browsers.map` without checking it, so the render error took the whole Settings panel down, including the THEME picker and the Arco version and UPDATE AND RESTART controls. The Tester's final gate found it through four fixture browser suites (version-box, update-notice, update-action, phosphor-production): their fixture API answers unknown routes with a generic object, which is the same shape a stray proxy or an unexpected server could return. The suites were right; the product was at fault.

`toReport()` in `client/src/components/settings/appWindowBrowserReport.ts` now checks the reply: no browser list means "Browser list unavailable." in the section, malformed entries are dropped, and the rest of Settings renders as before. A unit test covers a good reply, replies without a list and malformed entries.

The Appearance Layout note in Settings now says the narrow layout still applies under 1100 px with Compact, matching `layoutRange.ts`, the contextual help, the manual and the changelog.

Checked with build, typecheck, the unit suite under a symlinked `TMPDIR`, documentation checks, the tree audit, and the version-box, update-notice, update-action, phosphor-production, app-window-browser, help-docs and help-coverage browser suites.

Remaining: macOS and Windows checks with the release candidate.

The turn visibility browser check now follows the composer status line when a message is queued. The PENDING control opens the message list, where queued and delivered states remain visible. ## 2026-11-06 -- Shared skills stay shared under the project-state check

A skill shared with SHARE WITH REPOSITORY and committed no longer raises the project-state notice ("Git is tracking N file(s) in this project's .arco folder"). Before, its files counted as stray Arco state, and STOP TRACKING or RESTORE IGNORE FILE would have undone the sharing. Now STOP TRACKING untracks every other file in `.arco` but leaves the shared skill's folder in the index, and RESTORE IGNORE FILE writes the default ignore file followed by the share blocks of the skills the old file shared. Any other tracked file in `.arco` still raises the notice as before.

A skill counts as shared only when its exception line (`!skills/<slug>/` or `!skills/<slug>/**`) is in `.arco/.gitignore`; the comment line alone or a force-added skill folder still warns. The share block format now lives in `server/src/projectStateIgnore.ts`, used by both `shareSkill` and `publicProject.ts`, so the writer and the reader cannot drift. The state report gains `sharedSkills`, and an ignore file that is the default plus share blocks reports `default` instead of `edited`. STOP TRACKING uses a `:(exclude,literal)` pathspec per shared skill.

Unit tests cover a shared and committed skill (no warning, no-op actions), a shared skill plus a tracked settings file (only that file warns and is untracked), RESTORE IGNORE FILE on an edited file with share lines, the comment-only case, the routes, and the real `shareSkill` followed by a commit and a second share. With the old check, the new tests fail.

Checked with build, typecheck, the unit suite under a symlinked `TMPDIR`, documentation checks, the tree audit and the help browser suites.

Remaining: 0.1.4 still offers both actions for shared skills; the way-back table says so.

## 2026-11-06 -- Adversarial tests typecheck

`npm run typecheck` passes again with the adversarial checks on the 0.1.5 branch. Chat and plan review still refuse Bash, Read, WebFetch and WebSearch when web access is off, and an armed Chat tab still refuses Bash and Read. Menus still stay inside the window. Nothing the operator sees changes. This commit touches those two test files and this log only.

The break was two TS2345 errors in `server/src/providers/llamacppChatTools.test.ts` and one TS2564 error in `client/src/layout/keepInWindow.dom.test.ts`. The helper that asserts a refusal takes the tool arguments as `Record<string, string>`. The test built those arguments with a conditional, so TypeScript inferred a union of three object shapes, and a union of specific objects is not assignable to a string record. Both call sites, plan review and disarmed Chat, failed for that reason. The object is now annotated `Record<string, string>`. The tool names, commands, paths, fixture URL and assertions are the same. The menu fixture declared `position` as a type and never assigned it. Computed style already treated a missing value as `absolute`, so those tests passed while typecheck did not. The field is now assigned `absolute`.

Checked on Linux with `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `llamacppChatTools.test.ts` and `keepInWindow.dom.test.ts`. Both unit files ran with a symlinked temporary directory and an environment that did not set NODE_ENV. The public tree audit ran as `node scripts/public-audit.mjs --tree`. The first typecheck passed the server and stopped on the unassigned `position` field. After that assignment, typecheck and build completed, and both unit files passed. No product file was edited, and no assertion was relaxed.

Remaining: the release-candidate gate still waits for the operator. macOS and Windows remain unverified.

## 2026-11-05 -- Auto-handoff after N compactions

Auto-handoff (75% warning, 90% trigger) and auto-compaction (near 90%) read the same context percentage and competed. Auto-compaction now owns the context fill; auto-handoff counts compactions. CONFIG > AUTO-HANDOFF has "Hand off after N compactions" (off by default, N = 5, 1 to 20); Settings > Auto-handoff defaults seeds new tabs the same way. The WARN and AUTO sliders and the `[CONTEXT]` warning are gone.

`server/src/compactionCounter.ts` counts per conversation from `compactionReport.ts`: Claude Native `native_compacted`, the Claude CLI's `compact_boundary` (new in `StreamParser`), Codex app-server `contextCompaction` items and `thread/compacted` (once per turn), and Arco's durable Ollama compaction. Fold is not counted. The count is kept in `meta.json` (`autoHandoffCount`) and restarts with a new session. At a turn boundary a due handoff runs before any fold or compaction, in the configured mode; afterwards one chat line, "Handed off after N compactions", links the document. Cursor, Codex exec and providers without compaction show why they cannot count; Claude Native and Ollama say they need auto-compaction.

Saved tabs and defaults with `warnAt` / `autoAt` keep their on or off state with N = 5.

Checked with `autoHandoffCompactions.test.ts` (migration, counting, reset, firing, availability and cluster precedence), `compactionSources.test.ts`, updated engine and budget tests, the new `auto-handoff` browser suite and the form, handoff, help and compaction suites. `arco open` and the Arco shortcut missed common Chromium browsers. Detection now lives in `scripts/packaging/browsers.mjs`, shared by the launcher and the server, with the earlier order kept. Windows adds Brave, Vivaldi and Chromium (App Paths, Program Files and `%LOCALAPPDATA%`); macOS and Linux add Vivaldi; Linux adds Flatpak Chrome, Edge, Brave, Chromium and Vivaldi through their exported launchers, run with `flatpak run --filesystem=<profile>` so the sandbox can use the Arco browser profile.

Settings > Advanced > App window browser picks Automatic, a detected browser, or another program name or path, saved as `appWindowBrowser` and listed by `GET /api/app-browsers`. `arco open --browser <name-or-path>` picks once. A missing choice, or Firefox or Safari, falls back to Automatic with a message. The explorer window uses a found choice first. In a non-Chromium browser the page shows a one-time notice with CHOOSE BROWSER and DISMISS, remembered per browser.

Checked with `browsers.test.mjs` (simulated Windows, macOS and Linux file systems and PATH), `launch.test.mjs`, `appBrowsers.test.ts`, the new `app-window-browser` suite (Chromium, Chromium posing as Firefox, real Firefox), the help suites, and a Linux run in a temporary home where a missing saved choice, `--browser firefox` and a path each opened Brave with the temporary profile. Flatpak, Windows and macOS launches remain for the release candidate.

Provider letters disagreed: C meant Claude on the top-bar dots (`UsageBar.tsx`) and in the terminal sessions list (`TerminalSessions.tsx`), but Codex on worker cards (`providerBadgeChar` in `WorkerCard.tsx`), where Cursor was R because C and X were taken. They are now the same everywhere: A Claude (Anthropic), C Codex, X Cursor, O Ollama, L llama.cpp. Worker cards still show a badge only for providers other than Claude; the function now also maps Claude to A so a later use cannot fall back to the question mark.

Labels name the provider in full instead of the letter. The dots' aria labels read "Claude usage page", "Codex status details; open Providers" and so on, worker badges carry the full name as title and aria label (Claude (Anthropic), Codex (OpenAI), Cursor, Ollama, llama.cpp), and the terminal sessions letter has the provider as its title beside the existing full name.

The workspace manual's dot list, the power-features note on hovering the Claude and Cursor dots, and the Cursor provider page state the letters plainly; the "C and X are taken" wording is gone. The live-status, worker card and terminal sessions help entries list the letters. `docs/CLIENT.md` has a provider letters entry, and `docs/CLIENT.md` and `docs/GOTCHAS.md` call the Cursor dot the X dot. CHANGELOG notes the change.

Checked with the three usage dropdown unit tests and the `help-docs`, `claude-allowance` and `claude-auth-status` browser suites, which now find the dots by the full-name labels and by the new letters, plus a repository search for the old letter wording. ## 2026-11-05 -- Menus and popovers stay inside the window

On small windows and at a larger interface text size, menus near the window edge ran partly off screen: the PROVIDERS panel and the provider usage cards opened right-aligned under dots that sit at the left of a narrow top bar, and BELL, VOICE, SKILLS, NEEDS YOU and others ran past the right or bottom edge. Each panel placed itself; there was no shared rule.

`client/src/layout/keepInWindow.ts` is now that rule, applied to 28 panels through `useKeepInWindow()`: the top-bar menus and cards, the composer SKILLS, model and effort lists and MORE, BRIEF, NEEDS YOU, the member role popover, the help popover, the Home menus, the corner notices, the GitHub branch and clone menus, the theme preview and the file media menu. Each keeps its own CSS position; after drawing, the rule caps the width, flips the panel to the other side of its button when that side has more room, caps the height with scrolling, and shifts it along the edge with an 8 px margin. It places again on resize, interface-scale change, scroll, size changes and a details toggle. The inventory, including what is deliberately left out (modal dialogs, draggable windows, browser tooltips), is in the artifacts as `popover-inventory.md`.

Checked with `keepInWindow.test.ts` and the new `popover-placement` browser suite: 24 panels opened where the layout puts them and with the trigger pinned near each corner, at 1280x800, 900x700 and 480x800, at 100% and 150%, 720 opens. Before the change 342 of 600 measurable opens left the window; after, none. The first suite drafts pinned triggers under the composer's stacking context and left hover cards open between cases; pinned cases now open by focus or a direct click event.

The manual (Window size and layout), `docs/CLIENT.md` and CHANGELOG describe the behavior. No control was added or renamed, so no help entry changes.

## 2026-11-05 -- Private permissions for inline output copies

Saved copies of inline text and code outputs now use owner-only permissions on systems that support POSIX file modes. This matches copies made from output files and protects the saved content from being readable by other local users under a permissive default umask. The original inline output remains available in OUTPUTS after saving a copy.

The OUTPUTS Save a copy action uses `transferOutput` in `server/src/outputs.ts`. Inline content is created exclusively with mode `0600`, subject to a more restrictive umask. Existing destination, protected-folder and unsafe-link checks remain in place. `docs/OUTPUTS.md`, the in-app archive and explorer manual, and the output transfer help describe the permission on systems that support it. The changelog records the fix.

A regression assertion in `server/src/outputs.test.ts` checks the saved inline content and its permissions in a synthetic home folder. With umask `022`, the assertion failed before the change because the copy exposed group and other read access. After the change, the output tests passed, including a run with a symlinked temporary directory. Build, typecheck, public tree audit, documentation checks and the OUTPUTS browser suite also passed on Linux. The complete unit suite was run as part of the local gate.

Remaining: the final release gate and acceptance on macOS and Windows.

## 2026-11-05 -- NUDGE ALL WITH MAIL

The cluster NUDGE picker (composer NUDGE) has NUDGE ALL WITH MAIL (n) beside SEND NUDGE, where n counts the members with waiting mail. One click does what SEND NUDGE does with exactly those members ticked, through the same per-member steer send in `ClusterTab.tsx`: idle members get the wake-up prompt and start a turn, busy members get the active reminder (delivered after the turn on providers without mid-turn steering). Members without mail are never nudged, and a mailbox count left by a removed member is ignored (`membersWithMail` in `client/src/utils/clusterNudge.ts`). With no mail the button is disabled, the reason is its tooltip and shows under the list. The result toast also says how many busy members got the reminder.

The command palette shows "Cluster: nudge all with mail" while a cluster tab is active; it asks that tab through `requestNudgeAll` and does the same, or reports that no member has mail. The NUDGE button itself, the picker's empty default selection and the server path and limits are unchanged; there is no new server message.

Help (`cluster.nudge.all`, and the NUDGE button entry), the clusters manual, `docs/CLIENT.md`, `docs/GLOSSARY.md`, `docs/OPERATOR_ACTIONS.md` and CHANGELOG describe it.

Checked with `clusterNudge.test.ts` and the new `nudge-all` browser suite: four members (two idle with mail, one busy with mail, one idle without). One click sends the wake-up prompt to the two idle ones and the reminder to the busy one, and nothing to the fourth; the disabled state and reason with no mail; the palette request for the active cluster only; and the picker's buttons inside the dialog and window in Compact at 1280 px and in Automatic at 900 and 480 px.

## 2026-11-05 -- Move and copy outputs into a home folder

OUTPUTS can now move a file or save a copy to an ordinary folder in the home directory as well as the previously approved project and media folders. The dialog keeps the result visible until Close. Refusals state that the file was not moved or the copy was not saved; if the connection drops before confirmation, the dialog asks for both locations to be checked. Download is a button beside the other output actions.

`transferOutput` checks both the chosen path and its resolved folder, refusing Arco state, Git state, protected folders and escaping links. `outputGrants.ts` stores a separate permission for each explicitly chosen home destination in the Arco home. Project output metadata alone cannot grant access to another home file, and a later link cannot redirect an approved destination. The original file stays in place if the destination or its metadata cannot be saved; old links still resolve after a successful move. The output guide, manual and contextual help describe the destinations and results.

## 2026-11-05 -- Turn handler split into settings, prompt and after-turn steps

No behavior change. `handleUserMessage` in `server/src/ws/turnEngine.ts` was one function of about 1,600 lines. Three parts that are plain decisions now live in their own modules, each with tests that need no running turn:

- `server/src/ws/turnSettings.ts`, `resolveTurnSettings`: which value wins for each per-turn setting. The CONFIG tab's transport choice beats the settings files, which beat the default. Ultracode forces the Claude CLI. The Native-only switches (server compaction, thinking display, tool filter, fact-force, config protection, sandbox, review gate) are off on other transports. Output style is normal for DOCS and GIT and for transports that do not support it. An isolated member has no north star; Ollama parameters apply only to Ollama.
- `server/src/ws/turnPrompt.ts`, `composeBasePrompts`: the base prompts. Local providers get skills before their system prompt. Chat and Computer chats get their preamble. Claude tabs get knowledge, then CLAUDE.md, then skills, then the preview note and replay. The awaited lookups and the knowledge notice stay in `turnEngine.ts`; the cluster, Genesis and public-project blocks still follow there.
- `server/src/ws/afterTurn.ts`, `runAfterTurn`: Computer reset, calibration, context actions, queued messages, then the goal run. A context action that stops, or a queued turn that starts, ends the list.

The busy claim, the drain loop and the provider call stay in `handleUserMessage`. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` describes the split; help, manual and CHANGELOG are unchanged.

## 2026-11-05 -- Claude Native tool checks as ordered lists

No behavior change. `dispatchTool` in `server/src/providers/claude-native/worker.ts` applied its checks inline over about 500 lines, so their order was only visible by reading it, and no test pinned it. Before the tool run three guards: the hardline command floor, config protection and fact-force. After it come the output filter, the partial-read note and PostToolUse hooks. The order is the safety property. The hardline floor must refuse first. Fact-force records the path it sees, so it must not run after another block. The filter must run before hook text is appended.

The new `server/src/providers/claude-native/toolChecks.ts` holds `PRE_TOOL_GUARDS` with `firstBlockingGuard`, and `postToolSteps` with `runPostToolSteps`. `dispatchTool` runs the guard list after PreToolUse hooks and their input rewrites and before the review gate, then the post list after the tool. The filter's reporting and raw-output storage moved unchanged into a `filterToolOutput` method. The PreToolUse hooks, the tool-call counter, the review gate with standing approvals and tool routing stay where they were. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and `docs/PROVIDERS.md` name the lists. No control changed, so help, manual and CHANGELOG are unchanged.

Checked with `server/src/providers/claude-native/toolChecks.test.ts`. Its first part runs the real `dispatchTool` on synthetic calls in a temporary folder with hook commands as fixtures. It covers each guard's block and its gate event, config protection off, fact-force off by default and the re-issued edit, the call counter, a blocking hook before every guard, a hook rewrite seen by the hardline floor, note and hook text order, a PostToolUse block and the filter event. It passed before and after the change. The second part checks the lists with stand-ins.

## 2026-11-05 -- One tool-call decision for the local-model providers

No behavior change. Ollama, llama.cpp and LM Studio (with Strata and DeepSeek, which use the LM Studio worker) each had their own `dispatchToolCalls` deciding whether a model's tool call runs. The three copies had drifted; the llama.cpp Chat gap fixed earlier today was one result. The decision now lives in `server/src/providers/localToolDispatch.ts`. `decideLocalToolCall` checks bad arguments, Computer (voice) chats, the Chat boundary, SAFE mode, a missing folder, an unknown tool and the loop coach, then runs the tool. Each provider passes a small rules object for what really differs today, and keeps the differences as they were:

- LM Studio refuses tools it did not offer this turn and runs Computer after the boundary.
- llama.cpp has no Computer path; LM Studio and llama.cpp end the turn after ignored coaching.
- The refusal and unknown-tool texts stay per provider.

`parseToolArguments`, `dispatchLocalToolCalls` (stop the batch on cancel) and `truncateLocalToolResult` replace the copies in llama.cpp and LM Studio. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and `docs/PROVIDERS.md` name the module. No control changed, so help, manual and CHANGELOG are unchanged.

Checked with `server/src/providers/localToolDispatch.test.ts`. Its first part runs the real provider dispatchers with synthetic calls in a temporary folder and pins the result text, the order of the checks, the events, trimming and cancel. It passed on the code before the change and after it. The second part tests the seam with stand-in runtimes.

## 2026-11-05 -- Architecture review facts see tests by import

The facts Arco gathers for the built-in Architecture review (composer SKILLS > BUILT-IN > RUN) no longer list a source file as untested when a test with another name covers it. Before, `gatherArchitectureFacts` in `server/src/architectureReview.ts` matched tests by file name only, so `report.ts` counted as tested only next to `report.test.ts`, `test_report.py` or `report_test.go`. On this project that listed `claude-native/worker.ts` as untested, although nine test files in its folder import it. That was the first caveat in the P8.7 report.

Each test file is now also read for its imports. `importedModules` resolves relative JavaScript and TypeScript specifiers (`from './worker.js'`, `import('../x')`, `require('./y')`, a bare `import './z'`) against the test's folder, drops the extension and maps a folder to its `index` file. Python `from app.jobs import run` and `import app.util` match any source path ending in that module, and `from .helpers import x` resolves from the test's folder. Package names and path aliases are not resolved. Test files are read within their own 64 MiB budget. The facts line now reads "Source files that no test file matches by name or imports". On this repository the count drops from 1654 to 1470, and the Native worker, the turn engine, the cluster handlers, the Ollama worker and the client message handler leave the list.

The help for the built-in skill, the manual's Architecture review section, `docs/ARCHITECTURE_REVIEW.md` and CHANGELOG are updated. No control changed.

Checked with two new tests in `server/src/architectureReview.test.ts`: import resolution for JavaScript, TypeScript and Python, and a Git fixture where tests with other names import `panel.tsx`, `lib/index.ts` and `app/jobs.py`.

## 2026-11-05 -- Recover an open page after restart

An open Arco page now offers RELOAD when its running build is older than the server or a session request is still refused after a new cookie and retry. When no unsaved work is detected, the page reloads automatically. An unsent composer draft, attachment, incognito conversation, open dialog or edited field keeps the reload banner visible so that work can be reviewed first. A short per-tab limit prevents repeated automatic reloads.

`wsSession.ts` retries replayable Request objects as well as ordinary same-origin requests. `buildCheck.ts` checks for changed bundles and coordinates the reload guard; `StaleBuildBanner` provides the manual RELOAD action. The session endpoint records refused mint reasons with rate limiting. The update help, manual, operations guide and CHANGELOG describe recovery and the manual action.

Checked with an isolated restart browser suite using separate pages and both localhost address spellings. It covers a new build, retained unsaved text, a second 401 after re-mint, a Request object and an automatic reload of a clean page. The local gate passed build, typecheck, 1,722 unit tests with one skipped, the public tree audit, documentation checks and four browser suites. An early two-page fixture hit duplicate-tab protection; separate browser contexts fixed the fixture.

The original live session refusal was not reproduced, so its cause remains unknown. A page already running an older release receives the recovery code only after it reloads. macOS and Windows live acceptance remains open.

## 2026-11-05 -- SHARE WITH REPOSITORY leaves the ignore file unchanged when it refuses

When SHARE WITH REPOSITORY (Settings > SKILLS, on a project skill) refuses because Git still ignores the skill, `.arco/.gitignore` now stays exactly as it was. Before, `shareSkill` in `server/src/skillManagement.ts` appended the exception lines first and checked with Git afterwards. When a rule elsewhere still ignored the files, it reported the rule but left the lines behind. A second attempt then found most lines already present.

The share now checks the case that cannot work before writing anything. If the `.arco` folder itself is ignored, Git never reads `.arco/.gitignore`. The rule can be a project `.gitignore` line or a global ignore file such as `core.excludesFile`. SHARE refuses at once and names the rule. The check passes `.arco` without a trailing slash, because with one Git also applies the folder's own `*` rule to the folder. In any other refusal or error after the write, the original bytes are written back. An example is a deeper ignore file such as `.arco/skills/.gitignore`. If no `.arco/.gitignore` existed, the new file is removed. Every refusal message now ends with "Nothing was changed."

The SHARE WITH REPOSITORY help, the manual's skills section and `docs/EXTENDING.md` say the ignore file is left unchanged on a refusal. CHANGELOG has a Fixed line.

Checked with a new test in `server/src/skillManagement.test.ts`, run with an isolated home and Git repositories in temporary folders. It covers a project `.gitignore` that ignores `.arco/`, a repository `core.excludesFile` that ignores `.arco`, and a deeper `.arco/skills/.gitignore`. In the first three the file stays byte-identical, including an existing file without a final newline. In the last case, with no ignore file at first, the file is absent afterwards. The test fails on the previous code.

## 2026-11-05 -- llama.cpp refuses tool calls in Chat and plan-review turns

A llama.cpp Chat tab, and a plan-review turn in a llama.cpp agent tab, no longer run a tool call the model writes into its reply. Both kinds of turn offer the model no tools, but the llama.cpp worker turns `<tool_call>` markup in a reply into real calls (the Qwen and GLM XML fallback in `streamOneTurn`). Its `dispatchToolCalls` in `server/src/providers/llamacpp.ts` then checked only arguments, AUTO, the folder, the tool allowlist and the loop coach, so with AUTO on a written `Bash` call ran in the project folder. ## 2026-11-05 -- Updater tests place 0.1.5-rc.1 between 0.1.4 and 0.1.5

The release check, UPDATE AND RESTART, `arco update`, and a packaged npm update now have tests that an install at `0.1.5-rc.1` is offered stable `0.1.5` and is not offered `0.1.4`. The candidate sits between those two releases. The comparisons already followed that order, so no updater behavior changed.

The in-app check is `compareVersions` and `createUpdateChecker` in `server/src/updateCheck.ts`. A current version of `0.1.5-rc.1` and a latest release of `v0.1.5` sets `updateAvailable` and broadcasts one notice. The same current version and a latest tag of `v0.1.4` records the older tag and does not announce. UPDATE AND RESTART is `createUpdateManager` in `server/src/updateManager.ts`: with the running version `0.1.5-rc.1` it starts a separate update for expected release `v0.1.5` and refuses when the check says `v0.1.4` is not an update. `compareVersions` in `scripts/arco-update.mjs` uses the same order. A temporary checkout at package version `0.1.5-rc.1`, with stable tags `v0.1.4` and `v0.1.5` on the remote, reports a check from `0.1.5-rc.1` to `0.1.5` without a downgrade warning, updates to `0.1.5`, and refuses a request for `v0.1.4` unless a downgrade is explicit. The packaged path is `compareVersions` and `updatePackage` in `scripts/packaging/update.mjs`. A global package at `0.1.5-rc.1` installs `arco-workbench` at `0.1.5` when the registry latest is `0.1.5`, and refuses `0.1.4` as older than the installation.

Checked with `node --conditions development --import tsx --test` on `server/src/updateCheck.test.ts`, `server/src/updateManager.test.ts`, `scripts/updater/arco-update.test.mjs`, and `scripts/packaging/update.test.mjs` (57 passed, 0 failed). A temporary prefix install of a local package named `arco-workbench` at `0.1.5-rc.1`, followed by `npm install -g arco-workbench@0.1.4` into that prefix, left version `0.1.4`, removed the candidate-only file, and left a fixture service unit and a fixture home marker unchanged. The public registry lists `0.1.3` and `0.1.4` only, so that candidate package was local.

Remaining: a published candidate still needs the same install command against the real tarball.

## 2026-11-05 -- Evaluator folder given to Codex and Cursor as a real path

The Smart AUTO-PICKUP evaluator runs Codex (app-server) and Cursor in an empty temporary folder. `server/src/smartPickupAdapters.ts` made it with `mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'arco-evaluator-'))`. It started the process there and sent the same string as the thread and session `cwd`. When the temporary directory is reached through a link, the child resolves the link: its process folder is the real path, while the thread was told the link spelling. The Tester found this with a linked TMPDIR, where `smartPickupAdapters.test.ts` failed "the thread runs in the process folder". The official run uses a real directory and passed.

Both adapters now resolve the new folder with `realpath` before using it, so the process folder and the `cwd` in `thread/start`, `turn/start` and `session/new` are the same string. The folder is still removed after the evaluation, and nothing else about the evaluator changes (read-only sandbox, no approvals, no web, no MCP servers).

Nothing a user sees changes, so docs, manual and help are unchanged and there is no CHANGELOG entry.

Tests: `smartPickupAdapters.test.ts` passes with TMPDIR, TMP and TEMP pointing at a link to a real directory (9 of 9; before, 8 of 9 with the reported failure) and with a plain temporary directory (9 of 9). The test keeps its macOS `/private/var` allowance.

When the turn starts, `server/src/architectureReview.ts` measures the project without a model and appends the facts: largest source files, files changed most in 180 days (Git), files without a matching test, files with the most exports, and the attached plan's `decisions` doc. The skill also names the artifacts folder. The agent writes `architecture-review-DATE.md` and a `.json` with the candidates.

In OUTPUTS, **CANDIDATES** on the report opens a dialog (`ArchitectureReviewDialog.tsx`). **ADD TO PLAN** writes a reference doc and one unticked item under `### Architecture review` in the attached plan (the tab's or the cluster's). **RECORD DECISION** appends the reason to the `decisions` doc, which the next review reads. ## 2026-11-05 -- MOVE TO PROJECT: large binaries are reviewed, not refused

MOVE TO PROJECT refused large binary artifacts. A file that was not text (an `.bin`, `.zip`, `.qcow2` or `.pdf`) was decoded leniently and every "line" scanned. Random bytes match the IPv6 part of the ip-address rule (a bare `::` or `:0:4`) often enough that files of roughly 70 MB and more reached the 1,000-findings limit per file. The whole review was then refused, even for a copy into an ignored folder.

`server/src/artifactCopy.ts` now scans such content for runs of four or more printable characters, as `strings` does. The switch happens from the first chunk that is not valid UTF-8 once NUL bytes are dropped, so UTF-16 text with ASCII content still scans as text. A run cut by a read boundary is carried into the next chunk, and line numbers still count newline bytes. In binary content an IPv6 match counts only with at least four hex digits; IPv4 addresses always count. Past 1,000 findings in a file or 2,000 in one copy, the rest is hashed but not scanned. The file is marked `findingsCapped` and listed as not fully scanned, so a public copy outside Git ignore still needs the confirmation. The MOVE TO PROJECT review shows the reason next to the file name.

Help (Copy into project), the manual (Maintenance), `OUTPUTS.md`, `CLIENT.md` and the CHANGELOG describe it.

Tests (`artifactCopy.binary.test.ts`): a 72 MB random `.bin` took about 2 seconds here and produced 8 findings instead of about 190. A credential across the first read boundary and an address near the end were both found, and the copy into an ignored folder succeeded. A zip's stored entry is found and the public copy needs the confirmation. Address noise and UTF-16 text are covered too. The findings-limit test gives 700, 700, 600, 0, 0 across five files. The `artifact-copy` browser suite checks the reason text and the confirmation list. A multi-GB file still takes about 30 seconds per GB to scan.

## 2026-11-05 -- Turn-boundary hooks for real turns; chain-depth cap holds against the resume sweeps

Real turns now drive every turn-boundary hook, not only the alerts. The turn engine reports a turn's start and end through `send()`, while the idle nudger, the chain-depth marker and the auto-pickup re-check were wired only into `broadcastWorkflow`. One function in `server/src/server/broadcast.ts`, `observeTurnBoundary`, now runs from both paths: at the start the idle nudger marks the member busy and resets its per-turn nudge count; at the end it stamps the idle time, the end-of-turn artifact size check runs, the auto-turn chain-depth marker clears, and mail that arrived during the turn is re-checked for Rule-based or Smart AUTO-PICKUP. A start or end seen twice (STOP sends its own end, then the stopped worker sends another) runs the hooks once.

Wiring the turn-end re-check exposed an older gap: the resume sweeps re-entered the detector at chain depth 0, so mail refused by the depth cap woke the member a few seconds later and a loop could continue past the cap until the rate cap stopped it. `server/src/clusterAutoTurn.ts` now remembers the highest chain depth of a member's unread mail, as Smart pickup already did, and both the turn-end re-check and the periodic sweep use it. The value clears when the member's turn starts or an auto-turn fires for it.

Help (Rule-based limits), the manual (AUTO-PICKUP modes and limits), `PROTOCOL_CLUSTER.md` and the CHANGELOG are updated; `OPERATIONS.md` now gives the real chain-depth default, 8.

Tests (`turnBoundary.test.ts`), each through `send()` and `broadcastWorkflow`: idle nudger state, the end-of-turn size check on a short turn, and the depth marker clearing; a duplicate start or end runs once; mail that arrives mid-turn wakes the member once after it ends. A three-member ring of real-shaped turns, with the periodic sweep running, stops at the depth limit and, with a high depth limit, at the rate cap.

## 2026-11-05 -- CI without pull requests; full-history audit on main

Contributors and maintainers see a different trigger list for the **CI** workflow (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`). Pull requests no longer start it. It runs on a push to `main` (documentation-only pushes still skip it), weekly, and by hand from the `workflow_dispatch` form with its `os` input. A release branch is checked with a hand-started run (`os` set to `all`) before `main` moves to it. A push to `main` gets the same three-system verify job as before.

The verify job on a push to `main` now runs `node scripts/public-audit.mjs --history`. That audits the full history of every branch and tag the checkout fetched, so a problem on any branch shows up while the branch can still be deleted. Weekly and hand-started runs keep the narrower `--history-head` scan (the checked-out commit's history and the tags). The checkout steps no longer pick a pull request head commit; every job checks out the commit that started the run.

`CONTRIBUTING.md` now describes the three occasions, the two history scans, the native package jobs that run every time, and that pull requests do not start CI. The earlier paragraph there said a push to `main` ran only the Ubuntu jobs, which the job matrix had not done for some time.

Checked: the workflow parses as YAML with the triggers `push`, `schedule` and `workflow_dispatch` and no `pull_request` reference left. `node scripts/public-audit.mjs --history` passes locally on a fresh clone with every remote branch fetched. Build, typecheck, the unit suite, the tree audit and `npm run docs:check` pass. The workflow itself runs for the first time on the next hand-started run or push to `main`.

## 2026-11-05 -- Turn-finished alerts for real turns

Real turns now produce turn-finished BELL notices and desktop alerts. Before, no operator, auto-pickup or peer-mail turn produced one on any provider, even with "Turn finished" ticked under SHOW IN BELL and desktop permission granted. The cause was in `server/src/server/broadcast.ts`. The turn engine (`runPrimaryTurn`) reports a turn's start and end through `send()`, but only `broadcastWorkflow` passed messages to `server/src/attention.ts`. So `primary_turn_started` was never recorded and no `attention_event` was sent. The earlier checks drove `observeAttention` directly, so they did not catch this.

A new `observeTurnAlerts` now runs from both paths. It feeds the attention alerts and the 60-second long-turn transport notice (`onTurnStarted` / `onTurnEnded`). Plain request errors sent through `send()` are left out, because they are not a failed turn.

In a cluster tab, only the member in view now counts as viewed (the chosen member, or the first one when none is chosen). A finished turn of any other member reaches the BELL. Desktop alerts still follow Settings > Notifications: they are off by default, need at least 15 seconds, and come from the director only unless "every member" is chosen.

Tests: `server/src/ws/turnAlerts.test.ts` runs turns through `handleUserMessage` with a fixture worker for Claude CLI, Claude Native, Codex app-server and Ollama. It also covers an auto-pickup turn without an operator socket, cluster members with both finished sources, and an incognito tab. Six of its seven tests fail without the fix. The `notifications-settings` browser suite now takes its event from a turn sent through `send()` and checks BELL notices for cluster members; both checks fail without the fix. The help, the manual, `docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md` and the CHANGELOG describe the cluster rule.

Remaining: the other turn-boundary hooks in `broadcastWorkflow` (idle nudger, auto-turn depth, idle-resume sweep) still miss real turns; this is on the board.

## 2026-11-05 -- Test cleanup waits for background project writes

Nothing changes for users. Hosted CI failed in 3 of 4 recent runs because `storageWaste.test.ts` "an old log with board copies still replays them" passed its checks and then could not remove its temporary project: `rm` stopped with `ENOTEMPTY` on `.arco`. `writeMeta` starts two index updates (the project session index and the Recall index) without waiting for them, and the event log writes on a 100 ms timer. Either can recreate a folder inside `.arco` while the test is still deleting it.

`server/src/workflowMeta.ts` now keeps track of those index updates, and `settleMetaSideEffects()` waits for them to finish. Callers are unchanged: a meta write still does not wait for its index updates. The new test helper `server/src/testSupport/projectWrites.ts` waits for both kinds of pending write before a project folder is removed (`settleProjectWrites`, `removeProjectDir`).

Finding the affected tests: a temporary local patch delayed every index update and event-log write by 150 ms and reported each one that found its project already deleted. That flagged `storageWaste`, `clusterCoordination`, `workflowStore` and `eventLog.damageFurther`. The two log tests also flush the buffer before dropping it, so no write is left on a timer. The other tests that open the event store or write metadata and then delete the folder now call the same helper: the remaining event-log suites, catch-up routes, checkpoint restore, auto-compaction, Ollama execution, plan review, turn capture, rename and instruction-file tune-up.

Checked: the 16 changed test files passed 50 runs in a row, and once more with the temporary folder behind a symbolic link. Build, typecheck, the unit suite, the tree audit and `npm run docs:check` pass.

LEDGER and HISTORY rows that told the operator where to look now name MEMORY > LEDGER and MEMORY > HISTORY.

## 2026-11-05 -- Release v0.1.4

Release v0.1.4 collects the storage work, the long-chat fix, public-project guardrails and the plans list, plus the 0.1.4 items already on main. Long-running tabs keep user and assistant messages. The ARTIFACTS window gains a STORAGE lens with KEEP, ARCHIVE, RESTORE, DELETE, EMPTY SCRATCH and COMPRESS OLD LOGS, each confirmed; conversation logs stop storing task board copies and are sealed into compressed pieces past 16 MiB, with `arco storage unseal` for a downgrade. OUTPUTS gains MOVE TO PROJECT with Git ignore, visibility, size and content checks. Files: the four `package.json` files, `package-lock.json`, `CHANGELOG.md` and this entry; the tag runs `.github/workflows/release.yml`, which stages the package through npm trusted publishing for approval.

Checked on the release branch: clean install, build, typecheck, the unit suite, the public tree audit, the documentation check, every browser suite one at a time, the storage crash and compatibility scripts, a 300-turn chat and an emptied-tab reload, and a documentation and release-notes read. Hosted CI ran on Linux, macOS and Windows with Node 24 and 26, including native packages and package installs on all three systems.

Failures found and fixed before release: log pieces trusted by file name, scratch and archive actions following links, a sequence number reused after a damaged piece, tail reads stopping at a damaged piece, a NUL byte hiding text from the copy scan, path comparisons failing under linked or short Windows paths, and a Windows refusal to replace a log another reader holds. One hosted job failed in a test cleanup race and passed on re-run.

Remaining: real macOS and Windows hardware checks, UNKEEP, size warnings during a turn, large binary scans, and the cleanup race in `storageWaste.test.ts`.


### Added

- KEEP a whole conversation from the STORAGE view.

- Add a STORAGE lens to the ARTIFACTS window: every project and Arco's own folder, largest first, with COMPRESS OLD LOGS per project and ARCHIVE, RESTORE, DELETE and EMPTY SCRATCH per conversation, each confirmed. Kept items and plans always stay, and an action refuses a linked folder instead of following it. STORAGE SETTINGS holds auto-archive (off by default, never deletes) and a disk budget notice (100 GiB).
- Review artifact copies into project files with Git ignore, repository visibility, size and text checks. KEEP retains protected artifacts in Arco; copying never removes the original, stages or commits it. Large artifact warnings offer KEEP or DISMISS.

- Measure storage in a single-worker or chat tab under MEMORY > USAGE: MEASURE STORAGE (then MEASURE AGAIN) for the project and MEASURE ARCO FOLDER for Arco's own folder, with every byte under one heading (conversation logs, artifacts, worktrees, caches, voice models and more), log bytes by event type, and large files. Read-only. From anywhere, ARTIFACTS > STORAGE offers MEASURE AGAIN.

- Add public-project guardrails: a "This project is public (or will be)" box beside the plan-draft choices (cluster creator, DRAFT A PLAN, PLAN dialog) that makes a drafted plan include a "Privacy and public safety" section, remembered per project folder and suggested from the Git remote (one anonymous GitHub request, cached for a day, only when a plan-draft control is shown); INSERT GUARDRAILS to add the section to an existing plan as an unsaved edit; and a dismissible notice with RESTORE IGNORE FILE and STOP TRACKING when Git tracks or does not ignore a project's `.arco` folder.
- Add optional release labels to cluster board tasks (a RELEASE field in the card drawer, or set by the director), a release filter in the board header, and a ready-to-release line for a chosen release (tasks in, accepted, left). CATCH ME UP lists board changes with their release and a movement summary, and BRIEF on a cluster adds board totals, release progress and the last day's movement. Boards without labels and older saved boards look and load as before.
- Offer LM Studio, llama.cpp, Strata and DeepSeek through Xolotl as utility agents and Smart AUTO-PICKUP evaluators. The selected model must already be loaded; these requests do not load or switch models.
- Add REFINE to the plan draft banner: type a short note and the same agent revises the draft; the new draft replaces the old one, nothing is saved until you attach it, and it can be repeated. In a cluster tab the only save button is ATTACH TO CLUSTER (ATTACH TO WORKER is gone); a single workflow keeps SAVE & ATTACH.
- Show desktop notification permission and a test in Settings, and quiet BELL notices for activity in other tabs with REQUEST PERMISSION, TEST DESKTOP and SHOW IN BELL controls.

### Changed

- Measure storage once a day in the background, read-only, for the disk-budget notice (100 GiB by default). The optional rule that archives conversations closed for a chosen number of days is off by default and never deletes.
- Agents are told to put large intermediate files (builds, disk images, downloads) in the conversation's own scratch folder instead of its artifacts. Nothing in scratch is deleted automatically.
- Seal long conversation logs into compressed pieces once the live log passes 16 MiB. Nothing is deleted, every view still shows the whole history, a damaged piece never hides records the live log still holds, and `arco storage unseal` writes plain logs back before installing an older release.
- Conversation logs no longer store a full copy of the task board on every change, and a cluster plan body is stored once in the plan history instead of in every member's log. Logs grow about half as fast in busy clusters; older logs read as before.
- ARTIFACTS > PLANS groups projects and repeated titles, identifies plans in use and offers MARK DONE for probably finished plans.

- Include recent turns by default in SECOND OPINION when no messages are selected, and show what the models will see.

### Fixed

- Allow ADD TO .GITIGNORE for artifact copies when a project path has a different resolved spelling.

- Allow artifact copies from projects opened through a linked parent folder.

- Scan extensionless artifact copies even when they contain NUL bytes, and ask again before unscanned files are copied into a public repository outside Git ignore.

- Fix long-running tabs losing every user and assistant message: system rows and tool cards now take at most half of the 500-row chat window, and a tab already showing only system rows reloads its recent history from the server once.
- Fix TIDY, CATCH ME UP and BRIEF dialogs being clipped or painted over inside the composer: they open above the whole window on an opaque surface, keep the result or error line and the buttons visible at small window heights, and share one layout with FORWARD and SEND TRANSCRIPT (title in small spaced capitals, close button in the header, actions at the bottom right). The GIT, SPAWN, HANDOFF, REVIEW, PLAN, DOCS, TUNE UP, DEEP RESEARCH and SKILL editor dialogs use the same layout.
- Fix the GIT dialog forgetting its state after VIEW CHANGES (or any close without sending): reopening it in the same tab restores the run-as choice, SHOW OTHER WORK, scroll position, branch, action, commit subject and scope notes, until a request is sent or the tab is closed.
- Apply local public-audit pattern lists in linked worktrees.
- Bound Windows service stop checks and handle a service process that already exited while stopping.
- Read Claude Native utility replies containing thinking blocks, and explain unreadable, non-text and truncated replies separately.
- Show linked instruction-file names on their target in TUNE UP AGENT FILES and explain skipped links.
- Fix UNDO THIS TURN giving no result: after an undo the turn line reads "Undone", UNDO THIS TURN is disabled so it cannot run twice, and the diff opened from it is labelled as the undone change.
- Fix the cluster board hiding finished work: SHOW DONE is now on by default (and remembered once changed), and the task count in the board header matches the tasks drawn, with a note of how many done tasks are hidden when it is off.
- Fix nudges giving no sign they were sent: after clicking a member card's mail mark or sending NUDGE, the mark reads "Nudged" and the composer NUDGE button reads "NUDGED" with a count until the member starts its turn (or two minutes pass). No toast or sound.
- Generate a valid Linux service working directory and provide a guarded `arco service refresh` for existing package installations.
- Fix Home showing every project folder at once: Projects shows two rows of cards, Show all opens the rest in a scrolling area, and search shows every match. The Getting started card now counts any started chat, session or cluster (open or saved) as step 3, and has a HIDE button that is remembered in the browser.

## 2026-11-04 -- Sealing on Windows while a reader holds the live log, and storage under linked folders

The hosted Windows run failed two seal tests with EPERM. When another handle had `events.jsonl` open, sealing could not rename the new live file over it, even after the five-second retry in `renameWithRetry`. Renaming an open file aside works there; replacing an open file does not.

- **Seal (`server/src/eventLog.ts`).** When the live file cannot be replaced, the seal keeps its new segments and the complete live file, removes its temporary file, reports the problem and returns `liveReplaced: false`. Readers skip what is sealed. Seal-on-write retries after a quarter of the threshold more growth; appends never depend on the seal.
- **COMPRESS OLD LOGS.** A log in this state is counted as `deferred`. The STORAGE status line and help say it finishes on the next use.
- **`arco storage unseal`.** It retries the rename on Windows and removes its temporary file on failure. A conversation that cannot be written back keeps its segments; the others are still unsealed, and the command then exits with an error.
- **Linked folders.** On macOS the temporary folder sits behind `/var` -> `/private/var`. Storage actions on a project reached through a linked folder above it already worked; `storageLinks.test.ts` now proves it,; links inside stay refused.
- **Windows test links.** Link tests create folder links through `server/src/testSupport/links.ts` (a junction on Windows) and skip file links where Windows refuses them.

A test hook (`sealTestHooks.beforeLiveReplace`) simulates the refusal on every system. The two gate tests now accept the deferred outcome on Windows, then require a successful seal once the reader closes.

Checked on Linux:
- build, typecheck, the unit suite, documentation checks and the public audit;
- the `storage-view` browser suite and the three storage crash and compatibility scripts.

## 2026-11-04 -- Review unscanned artifact copies

MOVE TO PROJECT now checks extensionless artifacts even when their contents include NUL bytes. The copy review lists files that cannot be fully scanned, including images and recordings. When a destination is included in a public repository, the copy asks for a second confirmation naming those files. Cancelling leaves the project unchanged.

`artifactCopy.ts` scans non-media files with NUL bytes removed and marks undecodable content as not fully scanned. The review token covers the scan result. `POST /api/storage/copy-confirm` requires `confirmUnscanned` for the public case, and `ArtifactMoveDialog` asks for it separately. Git-ignored destinations do not need the extra confirmation. The original remains in the artifact store, and no copy stages or commits files.

Checked with the artifact-copy and storage-warning browser suites and the unit suite on Linux, plus build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit. An adversarial test first showed an extensionless NUL-prefixed credential producing no findings. The revised scan reports it. The manual paragraph check initially failed; splitting the paragraph fixed it. The tests also cover an image listed as unscanned, undecodable content, the server refusal without acknowledgement, and cancelling and accepting the public confirmation.

The manual, contextual help, Outputs guide, client guide, API guide and changelog describe the review. Scanning cannot prove that an artifact contains no private material. Real macOS and Windows hardware remain untested.
## 2026-11-04 -- Damaged log segments: sequence floor, tail reads and a linked archive folder

A second gate round on the storage fixes found three more gaps. Its regression tests (`eventLog.damageFurther.test.ts`, `storageLinks.further.test.ts`) land with this change.

- **Sequence numbers.** Once a damaged segment was renamed `*.damaged-<time>`, its name no longer counted, so the next saved event could reuse a number the damaged segment had named. Sequence recovery in `workflowStore.initWorkflowStore` now uses `highestNamedSeq` in `server/src/eventLog.ts`: the highest number any segment name has claimed, damaged names included. The live tail still counts too.
- **Tail reads.** `readLogTail` and `readLogTailSync` stopped at a damaged middle segment, so CATCH ME UP and the tail views missed older records that the full reader showed. Both now report the damaged segment, skip it and continue with older sound ones.
- **Linked archive folder.** DELETE with an archive removed the archive file even when `.arco/archive` was a link. DELETE now checks the archive folder before it starts and again right before the removal, and answers 409 `unsafe`. RESTORE also checks the folder again before it removes the archive. All other removal calls in `storageActions.ts`, `storageArchive.ts` and `purgeWorkflow.ts` were checked and already go through the link checks.

One gate test computed its plans hash before it planted the file reached through the link, so it could never pass. It now takes the hash after that step.

Checked on Linux:
- build, typecheck, the unit suite, documentation checks and the public audit;
- the `storage-view` browser suite;
- the three storage crash and compatibility scripts.

`PERSISTENCE.md` and `STORAGE_PLAN.md` describe the sequence floor and the tail rule. No control changed, so the manual and help are unchanged.
