## 2026-11-04 -- Sealed logs and storage actions never trust a name or follow a link

The final gate found two ways to lose data in the storage work, and its regression tests (`eventLog.adversarial.test.ts`, `storagePlansGuard.test.ts`, plus two long-chat checks in `appStore.test.ts` and `starvedChatReload.test.ts`) land with this fix.

First, a seal trusted segment file names. A segment named for records 1 to 30 that held only record 1, or a segment that would not decompress, let the next seal drop the live copies of those records. `sealLiveLog` in `server/src/eventLog.ts` now drops a live record only when the segment covering it passes a content check (it decompresses, every line parses, sequence numbers rise, first and last match the name). A failing segment is renamed `*.damaged-<time>`, kept, and its records are sealed again from the live file. New segments are read back before they are renamed into place. Readers skip live records only up to what they actually read, or what a checked segment holds.

Second, EMPTY SCRATCH followed a `scratch` link into `.arco/plans` and deleted the plans. EMPTY SCRATCH, ARCHIVE, RESTORE, DELETE (including `purgeWorkflow`), COMPRESS OLD LOGS and `arco storage unseal` now refuse or skip a linked `.arco`, `.arco/workflows`, `.arco/archive`, conversation folder, `scratch/`, archive file or restore path (409 `unsafe`).

Checked on Linux: `storageLinks.test.ts` links each path to `.arco/plans` and compares a hash of the plans folder; new `eventLog.test.ts` cases cover every reader against a mismatched segment and a damaged middle segment. Build, typecheck, the unit suite, documentation checks, the public audit, the `storage-view` browser suite and the three storage crash and compatibility scripts passed. `HTTP_API.md`, `PERSISTENCE.md`, `STORAGE_PLAN.md` and the CHANGELOG describe the rules; no control changed, so the manual and help are unchanged.

## 2026-11-04 -- Review artifact copies into project files

Artifacts can be kept private in Arco or copied into a project after review. MOVE TO PROJECT shows Git ignore status, public repository visibility, size and line-numbered text findings. Copies over 100 MB into a location included in Git are refused. The original remains in place; Arco never stages or commits the copy. Large artifact warnings offer KEEP or DISMISS.

The controls are available from OUTPUTS. KEEP writes the versioned conversation KEEP list under the same lock used by cleanup. The copy dialog offers CHOOSE ANOTHER FOLDER, ADD TO .GITIGNORE and KEEP INSTEAD. Before publication, the server validates source and destination paths and checks a private snapshot against the reviewed fingerprint. The temporary folder must be outside the project and Git repositories. Failed publication can leave partial files. Images, recordings and other binary data require manual inspection. Credential patterns are shared with `scripts/public-audit.mjs`.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, the unit suite, documentation checks, public audits and the isolated artifact-copy, outputs, storage-warning, help-coverage and help-docs browser suites. A synthetic Git project demonstrated the finding display, confirmed copy, retained original and empty index. Tests also covered the KEEP lock and a destination symlink swap. A viewport test caught an unscaled dialog height; the height was corrected. A visibility regression exposed inherited Git repository overrides; project checks now clear them. A direct fixture inherited Git ignore settings, so it was rerun with an isolated home.

Remaining: Documenter review and real macOS and Windows acceptance. A text scan cannot establish that content is free of private information.

## 2026-11-04 -- KEEP list held through archive, delete and scratch clean-up

Follow-up to the archive fix. A conversation's `keep.json` and `storage-warnings.json` now always stay in its folder: ARCHIVE never packs them, and DELETE and EMPTY SCRATCH never remove them. RESTORE never writes over an existing file, the KEEP list included. Kept items therefore stay protected for every later action.

Archive, delete and EMPTY SCRATCH now hold the conversation's KEEP lock (`keepStore.withKeepLock`) from reading the list through the last removal. The KEEP writer uses the same lock and key (`keepLockKey`), so a KEEP approved while one of these actions runs waits for it and is never undone by it.

`HTTP_API.md` and `PERSISTENCE.md` state both rules. There are no controls in this commit, so the manual and contextual help are unchanged.

Checked on Linux with new `storageArchive.test.ts` cases. A kept file survives ARCHIVE then DELETE (with the archive removed and both state files still in place). A kept scratch file survives ARCHIVE then EMPTY SCRATCH while a newer unkept one is removed. RESTORE leaves an existing KEEP list untouched, both when items were kept and when the list was empty at archive time. A KEEP writer queued while DELETE runs only starts after DELETE has finished. The existing archive, scratch and warning tests still pass, as do the unit suite, build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit.

## 2026-11-04 -- COMPRESS OLD LOGS and the KEEP list during archive

Logs are sealed into compressed segments when they are written to, so closed conversations kept their plain logs. `POST /api/storage/compress-logs` (COMPRESS OLD LOGS, `storageActions.compressOldLogs`) now seals the live logs of one project's closed conversations on the operator's request. Open or working conversations are skipped and listed, and logs under 1 MiB stay plain. The action is recorded in `storage-actions.jsonl`, and nothing is deleted. Each seal goes through the new `workflowStore.sealWorkflowLogNow`, which writes pending events first and runs the seal inside the conversation's write chain, so a conversation reopened mid-seal loses nothing. Builder 1 adds the button to the STORAGE view.

A review of the archive code found that ARCHIVE packed the conversation's `keep.json` with everything else and then removed it. That left kept artifacts in the folder without the list that protects them. The KEEP list now stays beside the kept items; with nothing kept it is archived as before.

`HTTP_API.md`, `PERSISTENCE.md` and `STORAGE_PLAN.md` describe the endpoint and the archive rule. There is no control in this commit, so the manual and contextual help are unchanged.

Checked on Linux with a new `eventLog.test.ts` case: a 1.2 MB closed log sealed, an open one and a tiny one skipped, an event written during the seal kept, and the full history and sequence intact. `storageArchive.test.ts` now asserts the KEEP list survives an archive. The HTTP isolation suite covers the route. Also the unit suite, build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit. The first version of the new test wrote its event before the store was ready, which the store ignores by design; the test now opens the store first.

## 2026-11-04 -- Archive, restore and delete for closed conversations

The server half of P7.5. `storageArchive.ts` archives a closed conversation into `<project>/.arco/archive/<id>.arcoarchive`: one Brotli-compressed file holding each file with its size, mode, time and SHA-256. The archive is read back against every hash before any file is removed; kept items and links stay in the folder. Restore checks the whole archive first, refuses when any file already exists, then writes each file through a temporary file and removes the archive. DELETE (`storageActions.ts`) runs the existing purge, leaving kept items in place, and removes an archive if there is one. All three refuse an open or working conversation, a wholly kept one and an unreadable KEEP list, and each is recorded in `storage-actions.jsonl`. `storageOverview.ts` adds `GET /api/storage-report?scope=all` for the STORAGE view, `GET /api/storage/budget`, `GET`/`POST /api/storage/policy`, and a daily run. The run measures again for the notice (100 GiB) and applies the opt-in auto-archive rule, which is off by default and never deletes. The report gains an "Archived conversations" heading.

The STORAGE view and its buttons come with the client work; the help entry and `CLIENT.md` name the new heading. `HTTP_API.md`, `PERSISTENCE.md`, `STORAGE_PLAN.md` and the CHANGELOG describe the rest.

Checked on Linux with `storageArchive.test.ts` (byte-for-byte restore with times, kept items and a link staying, plans untouched, the archives heading, refusal to overwrite, a truncated archive restoring nothing, unreadable and whole-conversation KEEP, delete with kept items and with an archive, the rule's selection) and the HTTP isolation suite (archive, restore and delete through the routes, policy defaults and validation, the overview, the budget). Also the unit suite, build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit.

## 2026-11-04 -- Scratch folder, KEEP reader and artifact size warnings

The server half of P7.4. The agent guidance appended to worker tasks (`workers/limits.ts`) now names the conversation's `scratch/` folder beside `artifacts/` for large intermediate files such as builds, disk images and downloads, and says the operator empties it with EMPTY SCRATCH; nothing there is deleted automatically. `POST /api/storage/empty-scratch` (`storageActions.ts`) deletes the scratch contents on a confirmed request. It skips kept items, removes links as links, refuses while the conversation works or when its KEEP file cannot be read, and records the action in `.arco/storage-actions.jsonl`. `keepStore.ts` reads the agreed `keep.json` format. When a turn ends, `storageWarnings.ts` checks the conversation's artifacts and sends a logged `storage_warning` once per file of 1 GiB or more, and once per 5 GiB of conversation total; kept items never warn.

The notice, its approve control, the KEEP writer and the EMPTY SCRATCH button come with the STORAGE view, so the manual and contextual help do not change here. `HTTP_API.md`, `PROTOCOL.md`, `PERSISTENCE.md`, `STORAGE_PLAN.md` and the CHANGELOG describe the server part.

Checked on Linux with `storageArtifacts.test.ts` (KEEP path rules and reader, warnings once and again after growth, kept items silent, EMPTY SCRATCH with a kept file inside a folder, a link to a file outside, plans untouched, the action log, an unreadable KEEP file, an invalid id, the guidance text) and the HTTP isolation suite (confirmation, unregistered project, invalid id). ## 2026-11-04 -- Sealed, compressed conversation logs

A conversation's live `events.jsonl` that passes 16 MiB is now sealed into Brotli-compressed segments, `events.<firstSeq>-<lastSeq>.jsonl.br`, beside it, with `log-format.json` marking the format. Nothing is deleted. Sealing runs in the conversation's flush chain: each segment is written to a temporary file, flushed, renamed and read back, then the live file is replaced by one holding only the newest sealed record, so a release that reads only `events.jsonl` still continues the sequence. On real logs Brotli made mid-size logs 14 times smaller where gzip managed 4.

The new `server/src/eventLog.ts` gives every reader one history: segments in order, then the live file, each record once. `workflowStore.ts` (replay, HISTORY pages, tail replay, typed scans, sequence recovery, first sequence) and the direct readers (Native resume, saved-cluster save, CATCH ME UP, resume picker, session titles, Recall, hub link, cluster activity, usage, OUTPUTS) now read through it. OUTPUTS keeps a sequence cursor because sealing replaces the live file. A log marked with a newer format is not written to. `arco storage unseal` (`npm run storage:unseal` in a checkout) writes plain logs back before installing an older release; README, `OPERATIONS.md`, `PERSISTENCE.md`, `GOTCHAS.md`, the manual's command reference and the CHANGELOG describe it. No control changed, so no contextual help entry applies.

Checked on Linux with `eventLog.test.ts` (sealing during writes and every reader across a restart, an interrupted seal, a 1.2 MB final record with concurrent appends, an unreadable segment, the export, a newer format) and a sealed-records case in `outputs.test.ts`, plus the unit suite, build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit. A first test used the wrong usage-event fields and counted zero turns; the fixture was corrected. Remaining: hosted checks on macOS and Windows, and the P7.4 and P7.5 work.

## 2026-11-04 -- Logs stop storing board copies and plan bodies

Conversation logs (`events.jsonl`) no longer receive a full copy of the task board after every change, every board list request and every reconnect. `taskLedgerHandlers.broadcastSnapshot` now sends the copy through the new `sendWorkflowLive` in `server/src/server/broadcast.ts`, which reaches the conversation's open tabs without a sequence number and writes nothing. The per-change `task_ledger_event` records are still written, the board still lives in `project/tasks.json`, and a reconnecting tab still gets a fresh copy. In the measured state these copies were half of all log bytes. Cluster plan changes are still logged in every member's log as an audit record, but the logged copy carries `bodySha256` instead of the body; `planHistory.ts` writes each distinct body once to `plans/.history/<sha256>.md`, inside the permanent plans folder. A cleared plan, or a body that cannot be stored, is logged as before.

Checked on Linux with `storageWaste.test.ts`: three board changes reach an open tab live while the log holds two change records and no copy, the board is rebuilt from the log after its store is removed, an old log with copies still replays them, two identical plan changes write one history file whose content equals the body, both members log the hash, and plan listing ignores the history folder. Also the unit suite, build, typecheck, documentation checks and the public tree audit. The first test draft monkeypatched an ES module export, which does not work; it now creates a real cluster in the sandboxed home.

The UI and its controls are unchanged, so the manual and contextual help needed no change; `TASK_LEDGER.md`, `PROTOCOL.md`, `PROTOCOL_CLUSTER.md`, `PERSISTENCE.md` and `GOTCHAS.md` describe the new behavior. Downgrading to 0.1.3 is compatible: it sends its own live copies and ignores the plan event. Remaining: P7.3 sealed segments.

## 2026-11-04 -- Read-only project storage report

The USAGE panel offers MEASURE STORAGE for a project's saved state and MEASURE ARCO FOLDER for Arco's own folder. Every byte lands in one named bucket: conversation logs, artifacts, handoffs, checkpoints, scratch files, other conversation files, plans, task boards, worktrees and isolated copies, saved clusters, voice and media models, caches and browser profiles, provider account data, server logs, or other. Each conversation shows its log bytes by event type. A file with several hard links is counted once and the extra links are reported; a project state folder nested inside another is skipped and measured on its own. The scan runs in a worker, never follows links and writes nothing.

The report uses `GET /api/storage-report` (`path=` or `scope=home`) and `storageReport.ts`. `docs/STORAGE_PLAN.md` gives the corrected, anonymized measurement, checks each storage claim against the code, and records the recommendations and designs for P7.2 to P7.5, including every log reader by file and function and the compatibility tests. `docs/CLIENT.md`, `docs/HTTP_API.md`, the workspace manual and the contextual help for both buttons describe the report.

The first version of the report counted hard links twice and the chat projects inside the home folder twice; that left about 70 GB of the largest project unexplained, which the buckets now show to be agent-made worktrees with build output, an audit folder and the duplicate links.

## 2026-11-04 -- Public-project checks on the server

The server can now tell whether a project folder is hosted publicly and whether Arco's project state is exposed to Git. `server/src/publicProject.ts` parses `git remote -v` for GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket and Codeberg in https and ssh forms; for GitHub only it makes one unauthenticated repository request (five-second limit, cached for a day) and reports public only on a 200 with `private: false`. `checkProjectState` runs `git ls-files` and a `git check-ignore --no-index` probe under `.arco/workflows/`; `restoreIgnoreFile` writes the default `.arco/.gitignore` after moving an edited one aside; `stopTrackingState` runs `git rm -r --cached -- .arco` and leaves files on disk. Routes are under `/api/public-project/` (`publicProjectRoutes.ts`).

`shared/src/publicGuardrails.ts` holds the one "Privacy and public safety" template, `hasGuardrails` and `insertGuardrails` (before the first phase heading, never twice), and the draft-prompt request text. No screen uses them yet.

## 2026-11-04 -- Reopen 0.1.4 before publication
## 2026-11-04 -- Long chats keep their messages

On a long-running tab the chat showed only system rows and turn markers. `capMessages` in `client/src/stores/memoryTrim.ts` preferred every system row and tool card newest-first until the 500-row window was full; each turn emits at least two system rows, so past about 250 turns nothing else fit. The preferred pass is now capped at half the window (`MAX_PREFERRED_SHARE`), counting only rows not already kept; the hot pass and the newest-first fill are unchanged. `capSessionChat` keeps the newest rows with no preference, so it needed no change.

Tabs already starved in a browser's saved state cannot recover from a reload, because reconnect replay sends only events past `lastSeq`. `isStarvedChat` (100 or more rows, all system or tool cards) is checked in `onRehydrateStorage` after `trimJobMemory`; when true the job's `messages` are emptied and `lastSeq` set to 0. In `rehydrateOneWorkflow` (`server/src/ws/handoffHandlers.ts`) `clientLastSeq === 0` takes the `replayTailEvents` path (last 3 MB), and `appendHistoryEvent` dedupes by seq, so kept history events are not doubled.

Checked: two new tests in `client/src/stores/appStore.test.ts` (300 turns of user, system, assistant, system rows give 500 rows with the newest user and assistant rows and at least 200 non-system rows; `isStarvedChat` true for 120 system rows, false for 20 rows and for 120 system plus one assistant); the existing preserve test passes unchanged. `docs/CLIENT.md` notes the cap beside `MAX_CHAT_MESSAGES`.

Remaining: not tried against a real saved browser store of a starved tab.

## 2026-11-04 -- Bound Windows service stop

Stopping the packaged Windows service now bounds the scheduled-task stop as well as the process inspection, and allows a service process to have already exited when the tree termination runs. A process that survives termination still makes the stop fail, preventing an update from replacing files in use.

`scripts/packaging/services.mjs` uses the asynchronous bounded process runner for `schtasks.exe` and checks the task state if that stop does not finish. The PowerShell tree command suppresses an already-exited taskkill error before checking whether the target survived. The Windows package smoke uses the same bounded runner for its stand-in launcher and process-tree checks, while retaining the unrelated-process and port-release assertions.

Checked on Linux with the packaging service fixture, including a timed-out scheduled-task stop, a ready task, and a reported surviving process. The new deadline assertion failed against the previous implementation and passed after the change. The hosted Windows package smoke previously failed after a long wait in the stop path; its logs do not identify a single cause, so the timeout and race handling are both covered. The full build, typecheck, unit and privacy gate is recorded in the review handoff.

Remaining: hosted Windows package-install verification and real Windows service acceptance.

The documentation review identified the missing update-path instructions after the service fix; this entry records the correction. Remaining: hosted verification and a real-host check of the recovery sequence.

## 2026-11-04 -- Correct Linux package service working directory

New Linux package service installations now generate an unquoted absolute `WorkingDirectory` for systemd. This lets `arco setup` and `arco service install` register a unit that systemd can interpret as a path. The existing `ExecStart` argument quoting remains in place. On Linux, `arco service refresh` updates an unmodified owned unit and its recorded hash and reloads systemd. Future package updates call this refresh before restarting; edited units are refused.

`scripts/packaging/services.mjs` escapes systemd specifiers and backslashes in the working directory, and rejects relative paths and paths containing whitespace, control characters or quotes. `docs/SERVICE_MODE.md` records that restriction. The package-install smoke checks the generated Linux unit with `systemd-analyze --user verify` on hosted runners when the command is available.

Checked on Linux with `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run docs:check`, `node scripts/public-audit.mjs --tree`, `npm run test:unit` before the refresh correction (1534 passed, one skipped), and the packaging tests after it (53 passed). The new regression test first failed on the quoted working directory and passed after the renderer changed. A local `systemd-analyze --user verify` accepted a generated unit. The first test run could not load dependencies in the fresh checkout; `npm ci --include=dev` resolved it.

Remaining: hosted package-install verification and real-host acceptance. The 0.1.3 updater does not call Linux refresh and can roll back after a failed restart; the service guide documents the manual package upgrade and refresh path.

## 2026-11-04 -- GIT dialog reopens where it was

Closing the GIT dialog (VIEW CHANGES in SHOW OTHER WORK calls `onCancel`, as do CANCEL and the backdrop) lost everything typed. New `utils/gitDialogMemory.ts` keeps an in-memory record per workflow id; `GitDialog.tsx` seeds its state from it, saves on every change (run-as selection, action, save-as-default, commit subject, scope notes, branch pick, new-branch name and base, expanded diff, SHOW OTHER WORK through new `open`/`onOpenChange` props on `GitOtherWork.tsx`) and on scroll of the dialog box, and restores scroll position once the status has loaded. When a record exists the initial status load no longer overwrites action and branch with inferred defaults. The record is dropped when CONTINUE sends a request (same tab or RUN AS new tab) and in `removeJob`, so a closed tab never leaks it. Unit test covers remember, merge and forget per tab. The git-dialog browser suite types a subject and notes, opens SHOW OTHER WORK, scrolls, cancels, remounts and expects them back, then sends and expects a fresh dialog; it fails without the change. Test fixture note: `root.render` is asynchronous, so the suite unmounts before remounting. Not checked: a real VIEW CHANGES round trip with several worktrees, and the record does not survive a page reload by design.

## 2026-11-04 -- Clarify Claude utility reply handling

The utility-agent settings guide and security notes now describe how bounded Claude Native requests handle their replies. Thinking and redacted-thinking blocks are ignored when extracting text, while tool use and other unsupported non-text blocks are refused. Replies that exceed the output limit or stop at the token limit are reported instead of being used as completed answers.

`SECURITY.md` records the distinction for utility-agent and Smart AUTO-PICKUP evaluator requests. The Utility agent section in `docs/SETTINGS.md` explains the visible failure behavior for BRIEF, CATCH ME UP, TIDY and TEST. These corrections align the settings and security descriptions with the existing feature guide and the adapter behavior; the request still declares no tools and remains bounded.

Checked with the documentation check, public tree audit and staged whitespace check on Linux. Documentation review found the stale security wording and the missing settings explanation after the adapter change; both are corrected here. Remaining: live-provider confirmation for the newer Claude model and hosted checks.

## 2026-11-04 -- Read Claude utility replies with thinking blocks

BRIEF, CATCH ME UP, TIDY and TEST can now read Claude Native replies that contain thinking blocks alongside text. Thinking and redacted thinking stay out of the answer. Tool use and other non-text blocks are refused, and an empty or truncated reply is reported rather than presented as an answer.

The shared Claude evaluator adapter checks reply block types and the provider stop reason. Its refusal log contains the reason and bounded block types, never the reply text or thinking. `utilityResult.ts` gives separate messages for unreadable replies, unsupported content and oversized or cut-off replies. The shared utility output budget is 2048 tokens, allowing room for a six-member brief and longer tidy proposals while retaining a limit. Settings help, the manual and the feature index describe the failure behavior.

Checked on Linux with fixture Claude responses for thinking plus text, a six-member brief, TIDY, empty output, tool use, oversized output and a token-limit stop. The regression fixture initially failed because the adapter rejected the thinking block; it passed after extracting text. Typecheck, build, documentation checks, public tree audit, four fixture browser suites and the unit suite (1535 passed, one skipped) passed. No paid provider calls or credentials were used.

Remaining: live-provider confirmation for the affected newer Claude model.

## 2026-11-04 -- Refine a plan draft

The plan draft banner (`PlanCandidateBanner.tsx`) gains REFINE. It opens a one-line note field; sending calls `beginPlanRefine` in `terminalInput/planDraft.ts`, which applies the same idle, not-streaming and no-pending-capture guards as a first draft, arms `pendingPlanCapture`, and sends a `plan-review` turn holding the current draft plus the note, asking for the full revised plan inline with the existing no-write wording. The existing capture path in `handleServerMessage.ts` replaces `planCandidate` with the reply; the old draft stays visible, with SAVE, REFINE and DISCARD disabled, until then, so a failed or blocked turn loses nothing. Nothing is saved until attach. In a cluster tab the banner now offers only ATTACH TO CLUSTER (ATTACH TO WORKER removed); a single workflow keeps SAVE & ATTACH. Both hosts (`MemberPane.tsx`, `WorkflowView.tsx`) pass `onSend`. Contextual help gains `composer.plan-refine` and `composer.plan-refine-note`; the manual cluster and plans sections, `docs/CLIENT.md`, `docs/PRIMITIVES.md` and the changelog (plus the banner sentences in `docs/CLIENT.md` and `docs/PROTOCOL.md`) describe the new buttons. The cluster-surface browser suite now refines a draft, checks the prompt carries draft and note, and checks the reply replaces the candidate before ATTACH TO CLUSTER. Not checked on a real provider.

## 2026-11-04 -- Release v0.1.3

Arco 0.1.3 is the first release from the public restart and the first published under the npm name `arco-workbench` (`npm install -g arco-workbench`, then `arco setup`); the `arco` command, the per-user service and the update controls are unchanged. User-visible changes are listed below: notifications on this computer and through ntfy, one status per tab, waiting mail on member cards, cluster controls in the message box (TO, NUDGE, BRIEF), the context meter in the worker header, explained utility agent failures, Codex usage windows in words, new appearance and context defaults, plain-language help for every panel, and a reformatted in-app manual.

The release was cut with `node scripts/release.mjs 0.1.3`, which set the four package manifests and `package-lock.json` to 0.1.3, moved the Unreleased section of `CHANGELOG.md` into this release and added this entry. The release commit and tag `v0.1.3` are pushed together; `.github/workflows/release.yml` builds the native modules, packs `arco-release.tgz` and attaches it to the GitHub Release. Because `arco-workbench` is not yet on the registry, the staging job is skipped and the first npm publication is manual.

Checked before the release on the identical tree: `npm ci`, build, typecheck, the unit suite (1,533 passed, 1 skipped), the tree privacy audit, `npm run docs:check` and all 62 browser suites, run one at a time; hosted CI was green on Linux, macOS and Windows. A dry run of the release script against a temporary local remote showed the expected version, changelog and devlog edits.

Remaining: the first manual npm publication and trusted publishing for later releases, the move of existing package installs to `arco-workbench`, and real-host acceptance on macOS and Windows.


### Added

- Add Settings > Notifications: opt-in alerts when a turn finishes (after 15 seconds), fails or is waiting on you, each switchable for this computer (a system notification, only while Arco is not the focused window, one per event, click opens the tab) and for your phone through ntfy (server, topic with GENERATE, optional access token, TEST; no hub needed, message text off by default; in a cluster "finished" comes from the director unless you choose every member). Everything is off by default, and existing `ARCO_NTFY_*` settings keep working. The window title shows a waiting count such as "(2) ARCO".
- Add a status on every workflow tab, cluster tab and Home Continue card: Working, Needs you, New reply (a turn finished while you were elsewhere; cleared when you open the tab) or Idle. Tabs show a colored status dot (hover or assistive technology for the word; Idle has no dot); Home Continue cards keep the word. A member card's waiting mail is a mail mark with a count (muted while busy, highlighted when idle); clicking it nudges that member, and idle mail counts as Needs you on the tab.
- Add CONTINUE a local Claude Code or Codex terminal session from a project card or the session creator (Home reads history when the project menu opens).
- Add captured file changes beneath completed turns, with diff and checkpoint undo; overlapping managed work offers a diff without undo.
- Add SECOND OPINION (formerly COMPARE): from the composer button or selected chat messages, several models each draft the reply you could send. Optional switches add the tab's last three turns and read-only file access (both off by default). USE THIS REPLY puts a draft in the composer to edit; nothing is sent automatically. Picks per model are tallied in this browser.
- Add FORWARD for selected chat messages to one or more cluster members with an optional note. SEND TRANSCRIPT gains the same note, several recipients, and switches for operator messages, agent replies and mail lines; shortened copies of mail a member sent are left out unless ticked.
- Add ADD WORKER identity fields on SPAWN FROM HERE into a cluster (role, tier, focus, access mode) next to provider, model and effort. The source's role is never copied, a second director is refused, the default name is the role plus a number, and focus starts as the first line of the new direction.
- Add a read-only SHOW OTHER WORK section in the GIT dialog for other worktrees and local branches (tab working there, ahead/behind the default branch, uncommitted files, open pull request when the GitHub CLI is available). VIEW CHANGES opens FILES > CHANGES on that worktree.
- Add OPEN on Outputs entries (and clickable names or image previews) to preview or open in the default app; programs and scripts are refused and stay downloadable.
- Add cluster creation that can request a director's reviewable plan draft, and plan hints that make drafting or attaching a plan easier to find.
- Add Settings > Appearance > Message box size (Small by default, Medium, Large): how tall every message box starts, kept in this browser.
- Add a quiet NEW REPLY mark on the director card in a cluster, and GO TO DIRECTOR on a question toast from another member. Focus never moves by itself.
- Add a NEED YOU counter to the cluster member header and make the Board quicker to clear: ACCEPT replaces DONE on review cards, ACCEPT ALL MET accepts met sign-offs, done tasks hide behind SHOW DONE, review tasks older than 7 days fold into an "N older" row, and an optional Settings > Agents and clusters checkbox marks met tasks done automatically. The Board and Plan tabs now say in one line what each is for, and the manual gains a "Plan or board?" section.
- Add Codex as a utility agent and Smart AUTO-PICKUP evaluator option while it is enabled and signed in. Each request is a one-shot read-only Codex session in an empty temporary folder with no MCP servers and web search off; any command, file change, tool, web item or approval request ends it with no decision. Regular Codex workflows are unchanged.
- Add update notices that follow the current attempt, report the installed version once, and avoid repeated punctuation in errors.
- Add package install port selection that remembers an available local port, moves if it becomes occupied, and keeps shortcuts and `arco open` on the running workbench.
- Add Windows package update checks of the recorded service process and bound process-inspection retries before replacing installed files.

### Changed

- Make the in-app manual easier to read: short paragraphs that lead with what a feature is, lists, tables and subheadings, space between paragraphs and a comfortable line length, without changing what it says.
- Publish the npm package as `arco-workbench` (`npm install -g arco-workbench`); the `arco` command, setup, service and update controls are unchanged. Earlier `@ghreprimand/arco` versions are no longer available.
- Move the CTX and OUT readout to the worker header, keeping its values, colors and calibration action.
- Redesign Home with a personal greeting and status, chat/session/cluster start tiles, waiting-first Continue cards, a filterable project grid and a Getting started checklist. The ARCO wordmark shows on Home as on every other screen.
- Regroup the top bar: provider dots open a Providers panel (versions, usage-page links, FREE VRAM with a connected local runtime, CONNECTIONS); KNOWLEDGE, TOOLS and a VOICE mic toggle with its menu sit beside them; BELL, FOCUS, HELP, SETTINGS, the version box and power follow. HELP and SETTINGS are hidden in focus mode. Move the ? help button into the top bar, right of the version box.
- Move the cluster controls into the message box of a cluster tab: NUDGE is a larger button beside the send area that highlights and counts while any member has waiting mail; SEND TO SEVERAL is replaced by a TO chip (for example "TO: BUILDER", "TO: 3 MEMBERS") that opens the member picker; BRIEF CLUSTER becomes a THIS WORKER or WHOLE CLUSTER choice on the composer's BRIEF. The cluster header keeps only the layout row and the plan line, whose "Members" now opens Workspace > Members. The member card's mail mark is larger. The one-time plan hint beside the composer's PLAN button is gone; its sentence is now the button's hover text.
- Open a cluster with the director focused and listed first (until you reorder cards), and keep the message box on the right as the one place to type. The bottom broadcast composer and SHOW BROADCAST are removed; the empty message box names who it reaches (the director by default).
- Rename the ARCHIVE button and window to ARTIFACTS (a live view of what agents produced across all projects); its content is unchanged.
- Show only what was made for you in Outputs by default (generated images, audio and video, documents at the top of the artifacts folder, reports and exports); code files and files in artifact subfolders sit behind an All files toggle. Image entries use a wide uncropped preview above the title, and long titles clamp to two lines with SHOW FULL TEXT / SHOW LESS.
- Unify the composer into one SKILLS menu for Arco playbooks and supported agent-native skills, with NEW SKILL, apply-once and pin controls.
- Show line and token estimates, read-only previews and deterministic hints on TUNE UP AGENT FILES before running a model; an accepted tune-up can be undone from its backup.
- Default Handoff to a quick, deterministic seed from recent conversation, the action digest and Git state. Written handoffs remain available and default to medium writer effort.
- Default auto-handoff to off. Provider auto-compaction now uses Arco compaction near 90% for Claude Native and Ollama; Claude CLI and Codex retain their own compaction. Existing explicit settings are preserved, and Claude API compaction stays opt-in under CONFIG > ADVANCED.
- Change the shipped appearance defaults: Phosphor themes use Victor Mono, frosted glass opacity defaults to 80%, and text brightness defaults to +25. Saved choices are kept, and the brightness reset returns to these defaults.
- Remove the Cookbook (local-model catalog, recommendations and in-app downloads). The System modal Models tab keeps the hardware profile and fit warnings under "model files".
- Hide cluster cloud and remote-management controls while the management hub is disconnected.
- Theme unstyled buttons (utility agent settings, CATCH ME UP, TIDY, BRIEF and the version box) and let Catch me up summarize from the tab start when no visit is recorded.
- Keep workers available to the operator in agent instructions: no sleep or poll longer than 60 seconds inside a turn; the director hands full verification and landing gates to the Tester (or a Builder when there is no Tester) and CI watching to the change's author; the TESTER preset includes landing-gate verification; Claude Native, Codex, Ollama and llama.cpp prompts carry the same no-wait line.
- Point the GIT dialog push instruction at the branch the commit lands on: after NEW BRANCH it tells the agent to run `git push -u <remote> <branch>` instead of pushing to the previous branch's upstream.
- Paint modal dialogs on an opaque surface under Phosphor glass and Frosted so chat text no longer shows through; side panels keep their glass.
- Bring repository guides, the in-app manual and contextual help up to date for 0.1.3 controls (including Notifications, SECOND OPINION, SKILLS, ARTIFACTS, Outputs, plans, tab status, waiting mail, cluster composer TO/NUDGE/BRIEF, auto-handoff defaults and Claude API compaction), give every durable cluster right-panel control its own plain-language help entry, and note that each opened project gets a `.arco` folder whose own `.gitignore` ignores its contents by default.
- Retake the README Home and cluster screenshots for the regrouped top bar, ARCO wordmark on Home, tab status and the cluster plan hint.

### Fixed

- Show Codex usage windows in words (5-hour, Weekly, N-hour, N-day) without the provider prefix, format reset times in local time, and rename the unlabeled "More" disclosure to "Source and login".
- Explain utility agent failures in BRIEF, CATCH ME UP, TIDY and TEST, and identify the model tried when no answer is returned. Claude Native utility requests resolve model aliases and report timeouts and provider errors.
- Name Codex beside Claude Native, Cursor and Ollama in the utility-agent feature index, Settings map, glossary and getting-started guide.
- Home waits until a project menu opens before scanning its terminal session history.
- Prevent instruction tune-up acceptance from writing through unsafe file links, and preserve separate instruction-file backups when tune-ups share a filename and timestamp.
- Keep cluster plan drafts awaiting review until an operator chooses where to attach them.
- Show the path the server actually read for chat file links, report a missing file by its name, open Windows absolute paths, and link filenames that match agent writes or artifacts.
- Replace em-dashes in in-app help and board UI strings with ASCII punctuation.
- Bound Windows process checks, leave unverified orphan PIDs untouched, and stop only service processes matching the command and home configuration.
- Fix Cursor replies: text on either side of reasoning or a tool call is now a separate message, only the latest message shows the blinking cursor, and the cursor no longer adds or removes a line.
- Attach files through same-origin `/upload` so drop, paste and the attach button work when Arco is not on port 3001, and show a visible error naming the file and the reason when an upload fails.
- Clarify the disabled Recall reference tooltip, local-runtime reconnect labels in Settings Authentication, handoff mode hints for providers without a resumable session, and keyboard focus on the Ollama status details.
- Package installation and native modules support Node.js 24 and 26 without compiling during install.
- Make the Recall "summarize on tab close" checkbox work: closing a workflow tab with a saved session starts the usual Recall summary with a toast and an OPEN RECALL link. Incognito tabs and tabs with no session do nothing, and closing is never delayed.

## 2026-11-04 -- Check local private words before commits

An optional local word list now prevents configured private terms from entering staged changes. The public tree audit checks the same list across the current tree. Matching is case-insensitive and uses whole-word boundaries, so a short term inside an unrelated identifier is not rejected. Findings identify the rule without printing the configured terms.

The list lives in `.git/private-words`, one literal word or phrase per line, and is shared by linked worktrees through their common Git directory. Blank lines and comments are ignored. An absent file has no effect. The existing pre-commit hook invokes the staged audit, which checks added text, added paths and binary additions; history and push audits retain their existing behavior. CONTRIBUTING documents the file and scopes.

Fixture tests cover mixed case, boundaries, literal punctuation, deleted and unchanged content, binary additions, linked worktrees and an actual pre-commit rejection. A binary fixture exposed that removing NUL delimiters hid a whole-word match; checking both raw and compacted text fixed it. Added lines beginning with plus signs are checked inside diff hunks as well. Build, typecheck, unit, public audit and documentation checks are run on Linux. Populating the local list and reviewing a cleaned tree remain for the release integration. No private terms or provider credentials are stored in tracked files.

`scripts/packaging/package-name.json` names the assembled package; the root manifest and lockfile use the same package name. The package updater validates scoped and unscoped names and derives their npm prefixes without treating a linked checkout as a global install. The release workflow checks and stages the unscoped package, with the first publish performed manually before trusted staging is configured. Installation guides, the manual and packaging notes show the new command.

Build, typecheck, unit, privacy and documentation checks run on Linux with isolated homes. The older scoped-only updater validation rejected unscoped names, and its prefix calculation assumed a scope directory; both paths were corrected. The first package smoke used an incorrect npm CLI path; the retry used the installed CLI. Real macOS and Windows package acceptance and the first manual publication remain open. ## 2026-11-04 -- Stop the cluster message pane from widening at narrow widths

The hosted Ubuntu run still failed the 560 px check in `cluster-surface` because the member pane's minimum content width, not the buttons, pushed the composer past the viewport and the overshoot depended on font metrics. Three minimum-width sources are fixed: the composer textarea gets `minWidth: 0` so it shrinks with its row; the member header bar wraps (name, status, context meter, workflow name) instead of holding one nowrap line; and in the composer status row the hint text takes a flexible basis so the cluster controls wrap onto their own line, aligned to the left, rather than sitting at the far edge. The pane edge drops from about 640 px to about 583 px with a wide fallback font; the remaining excess comes from the fixed sidebar and right panel, outside the composer.

`cluster-surface` now forces a wide monospace font with extra letter spacing before its width checks to stand in for CI font differences; the TO, NUDGE and BRIEF right edges stay near 380 to 490 px at 560 px. Also rechecked `help-docs`, `help-coverage`, `brief`, `cluster-mail-visibility`, `tab-status` and `topbar`, plus build, typecheck and unit tests. Hosted CI result is recorded on the board.

## 2026-11-03 -- Codex usage dropdown matches Claude's

The Codex usage dropdown in the status bar showed rows such as "codex · 10080 min" with a raw ISO reset time and an unlabeled "More" disclosure. `server/src/codexUsageRpc.ts` now names each rate-limit window with `codexWindowName` (300 min is "5-hour", 10080 is "Weekly", other whole days or hours become "N-day" or "N-hour"), and drops the bucket prefix when the bucket is the plain codex one; other buckets keep their name. On the client, `parseCodexUsageValue` in `UsageBar.tsx` passes the reset phrase through `formatResetText` in the new `client/src/utils/usageFormat.ts`, which turns an ISO timestamp into the same local label Claude's rows use (`formatResetLabel`, moved there from `UsageBar.tsx`) and leaves other text as is. The disclosure is now "Source and login", since it holds the source select and the legacy browser login.

Checked with unit tests for the window names and the reset formatting, plus build and typecheck. Not checked: against a live Codex account, so real window lengths other than 300 and 10080 minutes are untested.
## 2026-11-03 -- Default context handling to Provider

New workflows, cluster members and chats start with auto-handoff off unless a saved global default explicitly enables it. Provider is the default auto-compaction policy: Claude Native and Ollama use Arco compaction near 90% context, while Claude CLI and Codex retain their own compaction. The ten-percent reserve gives the summarization request room; unknown readings never trigger it.

CONFIG and the cluster panel offer Provider, Off and On with a threshold. A saved worker or cluster choice wins. Legacy Native efficiency values map conservative to On at 80%, aggressive to On at 70%, and off to Provider; the separate tool-count trigger is retired. Fold consumes the boundary before compaction, and the Provider default does not start Native compaction when Claude API compaction is explicitly on. Claude API compaction remains an optional per-tab beta under ADVANCED because it can remove working context mid-turn; saved per-tab settings remain on and new tabs start off.

Focused synthetic tests cover defaults, inherited and explicit choices, migration, stale claims, threshold rearming and Fold coordination. The Linux build, typecheck, unit suite (1,523 passed, one optional skip), privacy and documentation checks passed, as did three browser suites and all seven Native fixture-smoke steps. The first unit run found two outdated Settings section counts after removing the global switch; those were corrected. A fixture also exposed that Ollama manual COMPACT used a separate transient path, so manual compaction now uses the same durable boundary when a policy is active. No provider calls or credentials are used. Hosted release verification remains open.

## 2026-11-03 -- Explain utility agent failures

BRIEF, CATCH ME UP, TIDY and TEST now describe sign-in, timeout, empty and unreadable answers, and provider HTTP errors when available. A failed request identifies the provider and model tried instead of claiming it answered. Claude Native utility requests now resolve model family aliases through the same catalog as ordinary Native turns.

The shared utility runner and Claude and Ollama adapters classify bounded failure reasons without exposing provider response bodies. The four result surfaces share the same explanation and attribution formatter. Settings documentation, the in-app manual and contextual help explain failure reporting.

Focused synthetic tests cover alias dispatch, authentication and provider errors, timeout, cancellation, response bounds and attribution. A fixture first failed because the Claude adapter passed the alias unchanged; resolving it corrected the request. The adapter also converted SDK timeouts and HTTP errors to a generic failure, masking useful diagnostics. The operator's reported concrete model failure cannot be attributed to the alias defect without the provider response; a real retry remains necessary to determine its specific cause. No credentials or provider calls were used. The Linux build, typecheck, unit, privacy and documentation checks passed. Four browser suites passed after updating the cancellation fixture for the new attempted-model line; the first run also exposed changed timeout and success wording, which was restored. Hosted release verification remains open.

## 2026-11-03 -- Sharper README screenshots

`scripts/screenshots/capture.py` now captures the synthetic home and cluster shots at a device scale factor of 2 (3840x2160 from the unchanged 1920x1080 layout) and encodes them with `cwebp -q 85 -sharp_yuv -m 6` instead of Pillow's quality 78 WebP. ## 2026-11-03 -- Stabilize GIT and spawn browser checks

Browser smoke checks for branch creation and cluster member spawning now wait for completed UI work instead of depending on runner speed or text-input caret placement. Product behavior is unchanged.

`scripts/smoke/git-dialog-browser.py` waits for the intercepted branch checkout to dispatch before checking the active branch. The prior fixed 500 ms delay could expire before checkout completed on a loaded runner. The dialog fixture now unmounts after CANCEL and waits for the replacement dialog to appear before clicking the backdrop. Previously it left the old panel mounted, so the second click could hit the panel's propagation-stopping handler instead of the backdrop. `scripts/smoke/spawn-member-browser.py` selects the custom-role field contents with `ControlOrMeta+A` before typing the Director role. The previous sequence appended text at a click-position-dependent caret, sometimes leaving a non-Director prefix and bypassing the warning assertion.

Checked on Linux with `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:unit` (1,459 passed, one skipped), `npm run audit:public -- --tree`, and five consecutive runs each of `npm run smoke:browser -- git-dialog` and `npm run smoke:browser -- spawn-member`. The first browser attempts in a clean worktree failed before module loading because generated `shared/dist` files were absent; building the workspace resolved that setup issue. No provider calls or credentials were used.

Remaining: hosted CI for this test-only change, including the Windows browser job.

## 2026-11-03 -- Scope CI history audits to the checked-out commit

CI history checks now inspect commits reachable from the checked-out `HEAD` and all tags, without scanning unrelated local or remote branches. The new `--history-head` scope is used by the hosted history, identity, and trailer audit. The existing `--history`, `--release-history`, and `--push` scopes remain strict over their existing refs and proposed publication tips.

`scripts/public-audit.mjs` resolves `HEAD` directly and selects tag tips for the new scope; it treats only a missing symbolic branch as unborn. History traversal still checks commit identities, tagger identities, file contents, and content removed by later commits. `scripts/public-audit.test.mjs` covers an unapproved capture on an unrelated branch, the same capture once reachable from `HEAD`, captures reachable only from tags, a packed branch with no tags, and an unborn repository. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` invokes the scoped audit for CI.

Checked on Linux with the targeted regression tests, the full public-audit test file (36 passed, one skipped), `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:unit` (1,423 passed, one skipped), `npm run audit:public -- --tree`, `node scripts/public-audit.mjs --history-head`, and `npm run docs:check` (six passed). The first clean-worktree build lacked installed development dependencies; `npm ci --include=dev` resolved it. The regression tests first failed because the scope did not exist and because packed `HEAD` escaped inspection; both passed after implementation. No provider calls or credentials were used.

Remaining: hosted CI and review.
## 2026-11-03 -- Finish tune-up fixture writes before cleanup

Instruction tune-up acceptance and undo retain their existing behavior. Their automated handler fixture now finishes queued workflow event writes before removing its temporary project, avoiding a Windows cleanup failure after the assertions have passed.

The `mdTuneupSafety.test.ts` handler test flushes its workflow buffer and releases the buffer entry in its cleanup block. This keeps the test's temporary state available until the event store has completed its writes. No runtime control or user setting changes.

The focused tune-up safety suite passed on Linux with ten tests. The integrated stack previously passed the Linux build, typecheck, public audit, 1,471 unit tests and the browser suites covering its changes. Windows browser smoke passed on the integrated stack. Windows Node 24 verification exposed an `ENOTEMPTY` error while removing the temporary workflow directory after all handler assertions passed; flushing the buffer addresses the outstanding write before cleanup. The turn-changes browser locator also matches the ASCII diff heading after integration with the current interface. Hosted verification of these corrections remains open. No provider calls or credentials were used.

## 2026-11-03 -- Preview and undo instruction tune-ups

The TUNE UP AGENT FILES checklist now shows line counts, approximate token counts, and deterministic hints for dated session headings, repeated lines, and unusually long instruction files before any model runs. VIEW expands the current file text through the guarded file-content route. The displayed estimate uses UTF-8 bytes divided by four; preview and full analysis are limited to 2 MiB, with a visible limit notice. The existing compaction prompt and operator review remain unchanged.

After ACCEPT, UNDO restores the original backup. If the target changed since acceptance, a confirmation shows the overwrite consequence and the server verifies the confirmed version again. The replaced text receives its own safety backup, and undo is refused while managed work owns the checkout. The open review holds an in-memory undo receipt while the server runs; backups remain on disk. The client guide describes preview, confirmation, and this lifetime. The separate tune-up safety fixes use unique backup names and guarded regular-file handles, refuse symlink escapes, hard links, invalid UTF-8 and files over 16 MiB, and check for concurrent replacement before writing. Undo verifies the saved backup hash and logs its result.

Checked on Linux with the build, typecheck, unit suite (1,420 passed, one optional scanner skipped), public audit, documentation checks, and three tune-up and contextual-help browser suites. Focused synthetic tests cover preview metrics, unchanged and changed undo, stale confirmation, backup tampering, active work, and path guards. Before the fix, tests demonstrated that a symlink could escape the project and two same-name backups could collide; both fixtures now pass. No provider calls or credentials were used. Remaining: hosted cross-platform CI and documentation review.

## 2026-11-03 -- Preserve colliding instruction tune-up backups

Instruction tune-up ACCEPT now preserves each original when files with the same base name are accepted during the same millisecond. Previously, the second backup reused the first path and overwrote the earlier original. Distinct backups keep both originals available for recovery.

The `backupAndWriteInstructionFile` helper creates each backup exclusively in `.arco/md-backups` and retries with a distinct timestamp when a name is already present. It flushes the selected backup before writing the proposed instruction file. If no unique name can be reserved after the bounded retries, the operation stops without overwriting the target. The TUNE UP AGENT FILES review and ACCEPT controls are unchanged.

A synthetic regression fixture first reproduced the collision using two `AGENTS.md` files and the same timestamp. The corrected helper gives them different backup paths and retains both original contents. The focused test passes on Linux. The Linux build, typecheck, public audit, unit suite (1,422 passed, one optional scanner skipped), and tune-up smoke (9/9) pass. Hosted cross-platform verification remains open. No provider calls or credentials are used. Path confinement for instruction files is handled in a separate fix.

## 2026-11-03 -- Guard instruction tune-up file paths

Instruction-file tune-up acceptance now refuses a target that resolves outside the project, uses a symbolic or hard link, or is not valid UTF-8. The backup directory must also resolve inside the project. This prevents ACCEPT from altering an unrelated file through a redirected path and preserves the original when the input cannot be decoded safely.

The `backupAndWriteInstructionFile` path used by TUNE UP AGENT FILES opens the target without following a final symbolic link, verifies the opened file identity, checks for changes after preparing the backup, and writes through the verified handle. Backup creation exclusively reserves a new path, rejecting existing linked or regular files, and checks the project state directory before creating it. A 16 MiB limit bounds safe reads. These checks apply to the existing operator-approved ACCEPT action; no model run or file write is initiated by this fix.

Synthetic regression tests first demonstrated that a symlink, hard link, redirected state directory, and invalid UTF-8 were accepted. After the fix, the five focused fixtures pass and preserve the outside or original data. The Linux build, typecheck, public audit, unit suite (1,426 passed, one optional scanner skipped), and tune-up smoke (9/9) pass. Hosted cross-platform verification remains open. No provider calls or credentials are used. Backup-name collision handling is a separate fix.

## 2026-11-03 -- Quick handoff and read-only written reflection

Handoff now defaults to QUICK, which assembles a deterministic seed from recent conversation, the prior-turn action digest, and current Git branch, status, and recent commits. The seed is bounded to 20% of the target model context using a conservative byte estimate; an unknown target window uses a 4,096-token fallback. Git facts and older conversation can be truncated, and unavailable Git facts are identified. No writer model runs before the new session is seeded. Automatic handoff also defaults to QUICK while retaining saved global and per-tab written-mode choices.

The HANDOFF dialog offers QUICK, ORIGINAL WORKER, and FRESH SUMMARIZER. Written handoffs default to MEDIUM writer effort unless an explicit or saved effort is selected. Original-worker reflection resumes the session without project tools, returns text, and lets Arco save the handoff artifact. Native dispatch rejects stale tool calls after switching to the tool-free mode. Ordinary Claude CLI hooks are disabled for reflection; administrator-managed hooks can still run. The original-worker template no longer asks for updates to project documents. Settings, CONFIG, contextual help, the manual, and the Handoff and persistence guides describe the choices and budget. The settings and operator guides also identify the Quick default and written handoff file boundary.

Checked on Linux with the build, typecheck, public audit, documentation checks, the full unit suite (1,417 passed, one optional scanner skipped), 21 focused synthetic Git, settings, and provider fixtures, and three browser suites for the dialog and help. The first browser run lacked the isolated Python module path, and the second blocked Vite's local filesystem module; the fixture environment and local module route fixed both. No provider calls or credentials were used. Remaining: hosted three-platform checks and documentation review.

## 2026-11-03 -- Show file changes after each turn

A completed file-changing turn can now show a changed-files line below the reply. The line opens the captured turn diff, and UNDO THIS TURN opens the existing checkpoint restore confirmation. The confirmation explains that later working-tree changes are also restored and that a safety snapshot is taken first. When another managed writer overlapped the folder, the line identifies shared activity and offers VIEW DIFF only. A turn with no captured changes has no line. Isolated scratch members do not use project checkpoint actions.

The turn engine waits at most two seconds for the pre-turn checkpoint, then captures an end checkpoint at settlement before starting a queued follow-up. Failed or late pre-captures and failed end captures produce no summary. Bounded numstat output is persisted as a `turn_changes` event so replay reconstructs the line. Binary files, truncated path lists, and shadow-store files skipped by size caps are indicated. `ChangesPanel` compares the two saved refs, including refs in non-Git shadow stores. `checkoutOwnership` observes overlapping managed writers without preventing normal concurrency. External terminals and other processes are not detected.

Checked on Linux with the build, typecheck, unit suite (1,419 passed and one optional scanner skipped), public audits, documentation checks, turn-changes and contextual-help browser fixtures, `smoke:checkpoints` (12 passed), and `smoke:checkpoint-revert` (16 passed). Synthetic tests cover Git and non-Git diffs, empty and bounded numstat output, a late capture, overlapping managed writers, isolated scratch ownership, queued turns, replay deduplication, the undo confirmation, and a skipped capture. An initial typecheck found an optional busy state at the new chat control; an explicit boolean fixed it. No provider calls or credentials were used.

Hosted macOS verification exposed a timing assumption in the disconnected-observer acceptance test: the provider completion event can precede the end checkpoint and release of the busy claim. The test now reconnects and waits for the workflow to become idle, preserving the check that a turn finishes without an observer. Remaining: hosted cross-platform CI and operator review. Unmanaged writers must be stopped when exact attribution matters.

A project with local Claude Code or Codex history now offers Continue a session from the terminal on its Home card and in the new-session creator. OPEN starts a tab on the matching provider and resumes that session. Claude resumes use the connected Claude CLI for that tab; Codex uses the terminal account even if other profiles exist. Already open sessions are excluded. The list warns to close the terminal first, and the existing Claude concurrent-writer guard continues to apply. No option appears when no matching history is available.

`GET /api/terminal-sessions` reads the configured provider history directories without copying or indexing the transcripts. Discovery matches the project folder, reads bounded head and tail samples, caches parsed results by file modification time, coalesces concurrent history scans, and returns at most 50 sessions per provider. `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `CODEX_HOME` also guide resumed transcript lookup; the CLI watcher uses the same Claude directory. Docker requires the history directories to be mounted. Contextual help, the Home manual and Getting Started explain the controls and the close-terminal warning.

Browser fixtures cover empty lists, already-open sessions, a missing CLI connection, both providers, and explicit OPEN from the project card and creator. No provider calls or real provider history were used.

Remaining: hosted cross-platform CI and review, including real terminal behavior on the operator's Windows and macOS machines.

## 2026-11-03 -- Verify process identity during cleanup

Windows worker and terminal cleanup now limits each process start-time lookup to eight seconds. The lookup makes one non-interactive PowerShell query using the process creation time; a malformed result, missing process, or timeout returns an unknown token. Cleanup now refuses to signal a live process when either its recorded or current token is unknown. An unresolved record, including one whose termination fails, stays in the worker or terminal registry for a later check, while a known mismatch remains untouched. Legacy records without a token cannot establish identity and may require manual cleanup. A newly registered process refreshes a retained PID when its start time can be read, avoiding a stale identity after PID reuse.

`readWindowsStartTime` validates the PID and bounds one query. `pidReuse.test.ts` still requires two independent, equal, non-null start times for a live process, with bounded retries and an explicit test budget for cold Windows runners. The recycled-PID check uses a disposable child instead of the test runner, so an unknown second lookup cannot terminate the test process. Windows service stop also requires the `arco.mjs service-run` command and the exact home configuration argument before ending a matching process tree; a failed termination or unconfirmed stop refuses the update. The System menu and update help, worker safety and maintenance manuals, and service guide describe the behavior.

Checked on Linux with the build, typecheck, unit suite (1,415 passed and one optional scanner skipped), public audits, documentation checks, contextual-help browser suites, `smoke:terminal` (10 passed), and the host-only package install and update smoke. Fixture tests cover one query per call, timeout and malformed output, invalid PIDs, stable live tokens, a recycled live child, retention and reuse of unresolved worker and terminal records, the service command identity and the update refusal path. The Windows package smoke includes an unrelated process with the same configuration argument and requires it to survive. A hosted Windows Node 24 run previously timed out a start-time query and failed the non-null assertion. The first help-browser run in the new worktree started before the shared build finished; rerunning after the build resolved it. An initial package smoke used an obsolete native artifact; collecting the current host artifact resolved it. No provider calls or credentials were used.

Remaining: hosted Windows, macOS, and Linux CI, plus real Windows acceptance with the operator.

## 2026-11-03 -- Defer terminal discovery until a project menu opens

Home project cards now read terminal-session history when the operator opens a card menu. A Home page with many projects no longer starts a history scan for every card at once. The session creator retains its existing discovery behavior, and OPEN still resumes only the selected session after an explicit action.

`ProjectCard` mounts the terminal-session list only while its actions menu is open. Closing the menu cancels an in-flight request through the existing abort handler; reopening refreshes the list. The getting-started guide and contextual help identify the menu and discovery timing. The project card, session creator and provider-history formats are otherwise unchanged.

A browser regression fixture first showed a terminal-history request from a closed card menu. The corrected fixture checks that no request occurs until the menu opens, then resumes a synthetic provider session. The Linux build, typecheck, public audit, docs checks, unit suite (1,438 passed, one optional scanner skipped), and full Home and terminal browser suites pass. Windows CI must confirm whether removing concurrent scans also resolves its Home fixture timeout; that connection is not yet proven. No provider calls or credentials are used.
## 2026-11-03 -- Guard instruction tune-up file paths

Instruction-file tune-up acceptance now refuses a target that resolves outside the project, uses a symbolic or hard link, or is not valid UTF-8. The backup directory must also resolve inside the project. This prevents ACCEPT from altering an unrelated file through a redirected path and preserves the original when the input cannot be decoded safely.

The `backupAndWriteInstructionFile` path used by TUNE UP AGENT FILES opens the target without following a final symbolic link, verifies the opened file identity, checks for changes after preparing the backup, and writes through the verified handle. Backup creation exclusively reserves a new path, rejecting existing linked or regular files, and checks the project state directory before creating it. A 16 MiB limit bounds safe reads. These checks apply to the existing operator-approved ACCEPT action; no model run or file write is initiated by this fix.

Synthetic regression tests first demonstrated that a symlink, hard link, redirected state directory, and invalid UTF-8 were accepted. After the fix, the five focused fixtures pass and preserve the outside or original data. The Linux build, typecheck, public audit, unit suite (1,426 passed, one optional scanner skipped), and tune-up smoke (9/9) pass. Hosted cross-platform verification remains open. No provider calls or credentials are used. Backup-name collision handling is a separate fix.
## 2026-11-03 -- Preserve colliding instruction tune-up backups

Instruction tune-up ACCEPT now preserves each original when files with the same base name are accepted during the same millisecond. Previously, the second backup reused the first path and overwrote the earlier original. Distinct backups keep both originals available for recovery.

The `backupAndWriteInstructionFile` helper creates each backup exclusively in `.arco/md-backups` and retries with a distinct timestamp when a name is already present. It flushes the selected backup before writing the proposed instruction file. If no unique name can be reserved after the bounded retries, the operation stops without overwriting the target. The TUNE UP AGENT FILES review and ACCEPT controls are unchanged.

A synthetic regression fixture first reproduced the collision using two `AGENTS.md` files and the same timestamp. The corrected helper gives them different backup paths and retains both original contents. The focused test passes on Linux. The Linux build, typecheck, public audit, unit suite (1,422 passed, one optional scanner skipped), and tune-up smoke (9/9) pass. Hosted cross-platform verification remains open. No provider calls or credentials are used. Path confinement for instruction files is handled in a separate fix.
## 2026-11-03 -- Keep cluster plan review drafts unsaved

Cluster plan drafts requested for review now remain candidates until the operator chooses whether to attach them to a worker or the shared cluster plan. A plan-shaped reply detected without an armed PLAN action also requires that choice. Previously both paths could attach the shared plan immediately, contrary to the review choice.

The `primary_complete` handler stores the candidate instead of sending `cluster_plan_set` for review and detected drafts. The focused member pane now shows `PlanCandidateBanner` with ATTACH TO WORKER, ATTACH TO CLUSTER and DISCARD. A cluster DRAFT & AUTO-SAVE request also offers this choice, as the composer already promises. The client guide describes the distinction; no plan is attached merely by displaying the banner.

Synthetic WebSocket tests first reproduced both premature attachment paths. The corrected fixtures verify that neither sends a cluster plan update, and a separate case checks that cluster auto-save also remains in review. The focused cases pass on Linux. The Linux build, typecheck, public audit, documentation checks, unit suite (1,424 passed, one optional scanner skipped), and cluster browser smoke pass. Hosted cross-platform checks remain open. No provider calls or credentials are used. This fixes existing review behavior independently of the new-user plan prompts.

## 2026-11-03 -- Waiting mail on member cards

The member card's `NUDGE N` label is now a mail mark with the count of messages waiting for that member. Its hover text says they are delivered at the worker's next turn. While the member is busy the mark is muted text; when the member is idle with mail it is a warning-colored button, styled like the question marker, and clicking it nudges just that member through the same `nudgeMembers` path the cluster NUDGE picker uses, without the toast and without opening the card. Nothing plays a sound or opens a pop-up, and the idle mail alert setting is unchanged.

An idle member with mail also counts toward Needs you: `clusterHasIdleMail` in `utils/tabStatus.ts` feeds the cluster tab chip and the Home Continue card. A busy member with mail does not. New help entry `cluster.member-mail`; the cluster manual, `docs/OPERATOR_ACTIONS.md` and `docs/CLIENT.md` are updated.

Checked with unit tests for idle+mail, busy+mail, no mail and missing depth, and a browser fixture where a busy member shows the muted count, an idle member shows the highlighted button and one click sends one nudge. Not checked: real mailbox delivery after a nudge, and the look of the mark on every theme.

## 2026-11-03 -- One status per tab

Every workflow tab, cluster tab and Home Continue card now shows one status word: Needs you (a worker waits for an answer to a structured question), Working (a turn is running), New reply (a turn finished while another tab or Home was showing; cleared when the tab opens) or Idle. The pulse dot in the tab strip is replaced by the word with a small marker, and the existing question mark on the tab stays.

`client/src/utils/tabStatus.ts` holds the priority (Needs you over Working over New reply over Idle) and the labels. `client/src/stores/tabStatusStore.ts` subscribes to `appStore` once from `App.tsx`: when a job's `busy` flag falls while it is not the open tab, its tab is marked (a cluster member marks its cluster tab), and opening the tab clears the mark. The mark is in memory only, so a page reload starts with none. `TabStatusChip` renders the word in `TabBar` and `HomeView`; help entry `tabbar.status`, the tab-bar manual text and `docs/CLIENT.md` describe it.

Checked with unit tests for the priority and for the store (marked away, not marked on the open tab, cluster membership, cleared on open), plus the build, typecheck and help registry check. Not checked: a failed or interrupted turn also counts as a finished turn and shows New reply; a hidden browser window with the tab open counts as viewed; the label width on very narrow tab strips has not been looked at on a real display.

Disconnecting while the cloud list is open returns it to local snapshots. Cluster remote sharing and remote review gates, along with the corresponding per-workflow controls, also appear only while connected. The header hub indicator remains available for reconnecting a configured hub.

`ClusterRightPanel`, `SavedClustersList`, and `TerminalInput` read the existing hub status store. No connection settings or background connection attempts were added. The cluster and composer help entries and the cluster and remote-management manual sections describe the connection requirement. Existing local snapshot import, export, save, and restore remain available. Previously saved remote permissions and snapshots are not changed by visibility transitions.

Checked on Linux with the build, typecheck, unit suite (1,410 passed, one optional scanner skipped), public tree and staged audits, documentation checks, and the `cluster-surface`, `help-docs`, and `help-coverage` browser suites. The synthetic browser fixture supplies disconnected, connected, and disconnected-again status messages; it checks cloud save, upload, vault key, cluster remote, review gates, and workflow remote controls while preserving the local snapshot list. The first browser run on the new checkout could not resolve the shared package before the build; building the workspace fixed the fixture. No hub, provider service, or credentials were used.

## 2026-11-03 -- File links in chat resolve to the real file

Filename clicks in replies no longer show a guessed path. `resolveChatFilePath` used to join every relative name onto the project folder, so a bare artifact name displayed as `<project>/<name>` although the server served it from the workflow artifacts folder, and a Windows absolute path was joined onto the project folder as well. The helpers now live in `client/src/utils/chatFileLinks.ts`: relative names are sent as written, drive-letter and UNC paths are kept, and the written-files index splits on both separators.

`GET /api/file-content` in `server/src/http/router.ts` adds `resolvedPath` to a successful read and reports a missing file as `File not found: <name as asked>`; `FileViewerModal` shows `resolvedPath` in its header. Linking widened in `ChatMessage.tsx`: a filename alone on a plain line links (the viewer reports a missing file), while a filename inside a sentence links only when it matches the written-files index or the workflow artifacts listing, which `ChatPanel` loads from `/api/outputs` when a turn ends. The worker safety footer in `server/src/workers/limits.ts` now asks for the filename in backticks.

Checked with build, typecheck, a new `chatFileLinks.test.tsx` (relative, Windows, index and prose rules rendered through jsdom) and added assertions in `isolationAcceptance.test.ts` for `resolvedPath` and the not-found message. The Windows path handling was exercised through unit fixtures only, not on a Windows host.

Remaining: browser coverage of the whole-line link in a live chat.

## 2026-11-03 -- GIT dialog push instruction follows the branch being committed on

After NEW BRANCH from main, CONTINUE in the GIT dialog told the agent to push to the upstream of the branch the tab was on when the dialog opened (`origin/main`). `client/src/components/GitDialog.tsx` now derives the upstream from the final branch with `upstreamForFinalBranch` (in `client/src/utils/gitPushTarget.ts`): a new branch has none, the current branch keeps its own, and a switched-to branch uses its own upstream from the branch list. A branch without an upstream gets `git push -u <remote> <branch>`, where the remote is `origin` or the first configured remote; a branch with one pushes to that upstream.

Checked with `gitDialogUpstream.test.ts` (new branch, current branch, switched branch with and without upstream) and the `git-dialog` browser suite, which now asserts that the prompt for a new branch contains `git push -u origin feature/dialog-check` and never the old `origin/main` target. The suite itself covers synthetic-repo ahead/behind and dirty status, protected main, branch creation, same-tab and RUN AS dispatch, and cancel behavior.

The suite's cancel and backdrop assertions now wait for the cancel count instead of reading it immediately, after the Windows browser job read it before the click had been handled.

Not checked: the prompt followed by a real agent, or a repository whose only remote is not named origin beyond the unit logic.
