Thirty modal containers use it: GIT, the skill editor, SPAWN FROM HERE, handoff, review, research, plan composer, plan review picker, transcript send, second-opinion compare, command palette, shortcut sheet, manual, Recall, Outputs viewer, catch me up, BRIEF, auto-pickup, nudge recipients, send-to-several, mailbox, member picker, move target, pending questions, workspace rename, docs update, system modal, tidy composer, computer panel and the doc viewer (TUNE UP AGENT FILES already paints the page color). Side panels, toasts and menus keep `--color-bg-panel`.

Checked with a new browser suite, `dialog-opaque`, which renders a dialog under Matrix with Frosted on, Phosphor, and Phosphor with Frosted and asserts the dialog background alpha is 1 while the panel token stays translucent. Build, typecheck, unit tests, docs check and the audit pass.

Not checked: every dialog by eye under every theme; only one dialog is rendered in the suite and the rest rely on the shared token.
## 2026-11-03 -- Failed-turn alerts for every provider path and a Notifications browser suite

Failed-turn alerts previously fired only for `worker_error`, `primary_refusal` and workflow-scoped `error` messages. `releaseBusy` in `server/src/ws/turnEngine.ts` now also raises the failed alert when a turn is released as failed, so provider failures that never broadcast one of those messages are covered. The per-workflow three-second throttle in `server/src/attention.ts` keeps a worker error followed by the release from alerting twice; voice-operator tabs are skipped and incognito tabs stay silent.

The new browser suite `notifications-settings` mounts Settings > Notifications against fixture endpoints and checks that every switch starts off, that a tick saves exactly that event and place, GENERATE produces an `arco-` topic of 24 hex digits, a bad server address and an empty topic are explained before saving or testing, a saved token is never shown and REMOVE TOKEN clears it, TEST reports both success and a failure message, and the message-text and cluster-source choices save. A unit test in `busyClaim.test.ts` pins one failed alert for a failed release and none for an interrupted one.

Not checked: other provider failures that never release the turn claim, a real browser permission prompt, and a real ntfy server.

## 2026-11-03 -- Desktop and phone alerts

Settings has a Notifications page (`notification-channels` section, `client/src/components/NotificationChannelsSettings.tsx`) with a grid of events (turn finished, turn failed, waiting for you) by place (this computer, phone), all off by default, plus the ntfy server, a topic with GENERATE, an optional access token and TEST, a message-text switch, and the cluster "finished" source (director by default).

`server/src/attention.ts` receives workflow messages from `broadcastWorkflow`: a turn that ran at least 15 seconds, `worker_error`, `primary_refusal`, a pending `operator_question` and a parked `gate_required`. It skips Incognito workflows, allows one alert per workflow and event every 3 seconds, posts to ntfy itself (no hub, no connected client) and broadcasts `attention_event` without message text. `client/src/alerts/desktopAlerts.ts` shows a browser notification only when its switch is on, permission is granted and the window is unfocused or hidden, with the event id as tag so one event shows once; a click opens the tab. `client/src/alerts/waiting.ts` defines a waiting tab (open question or approval) and drives the `(N) ` window title count; the same definition is meant for per-tab status. Preferences are in `~/.arco/notification-channels.json` (mode 0600); the token is never returned by `GET /api/notification-channels`. `ARCO_NTFY_URL` and `ARCO_NTFY_TOKEN` still supply the phone address when none is saved, and the older `turn-finished` transport skips that category while the phone "finished" switch is on.

Checked with build, typecheck and new unit tests for the shared sanitizer, `attention.ts` (switches, replay, text, Incognito, short turns, environment fallback, TEST) and the client waiting and desktop decisions. Not checked: a real browser permission prompt and system notification, a real ntfy server or phone, and whether every provider path emits `worker_error` or a refusal for a failed primary turn, so some failures may not alert. The Settings navigation test and the section count in `docs/SETTINGS.md` were raised to include the new section.

The Notifications page was exercised only by the existing settings and help suites (a fixture that returned an unexpected body once crashed the panel, so the store now validates the response). Remaining: a dedicated browser suite for the page; per-tab status (separate item).
## 2026-11-03 -- Outputs lists what was made for you by default

`isPrimaryOutput` in `server/src/outputs.ts` classifies each output and `listOutputs` returns it as `primary` (`WorkflowOutput` in `shared/src/outputs.ts`). Primary means a generated image, audio or video, a non-code document or image at the top level of the workflow artifacts folder, an inline text artifact, or an explicit export (`editor export`, `media export`, `saved copy`). Code files, unknown binaries and anything in an artifact subfolder are working files. `OutputsPanel.tsx` filters on `primary` unless the new All files checkbox is ticked, and a note under the filters counts the hidden entries; an entry without the field (an older server) is treated as primary.

Checked with build, typecheck, `outputs.test.ts` (a table of primary and working cases plus the listing) and the `outputs` and `outputs-open` browser suites. The `outputs` suite marks one fixture as a working file and checks the hidden count and the All files toggle. Classification is by extension and location, so a code-like file with a document extension (for example JSON) stays primary.

Remaining: none known.

## 2026-11-03 -- Spawn into a cluster as a described member

SPAWN FROM HERE with "Spawn into cluster" checked now shows the ADD WORKER identity fields beside provider, model and effort: ROLE (presets or custom text), TIER (none, light, standard, heavy), FOCUS and ACCESS (Standard, Blind, Isolated). They start empty, so the source's role is never copied, and DIRECTOR is disabled when the cluster already has one; a custom director-style role is refused with the at-most-one-director message. The default name is the role label plus the next free number (`Worker #1` with no role), never `<source> (spawn)`; FOCUS starts as the first line of NEW DIRECTION until it is edited. Isolated is offered for Claude only and switches the new tab to the native transport. A spawn outside a cluster keeps its fields and its `<name> (spawn)` default.

The fields travel as `memberIdentity` on `spawn_from_here_request` (`shared/src/protocol/handoffs.ts`) through TerminalInput, MemberPane, ClusterTab, ClusterPlanTab and App. `handleSpawnFromHere` in `server/src/ws/handoffHandlers.ts` validates them with the join before anything is created (`spawnIdentityProblem`: multiple directors, invalid tier or access mode, Isolated needs Claude native) and applies them after `addMember` through the existing `setMemberAccessMode`, `setMemberRole`, `setMemberTier` and `setMemberFocus`, including the role in `cluster_member_added`.

Checked with unit tests for the name helper and the server validation, and the new `spawn-member` browser suite (outside-cluster spawn unchanged, fields, director fence, name and focus defaults, what is sent on confirm, Isolated). The ADD WORKER section itself is untouched.

Not checked: a live cluster spawn end to end with a real provider, and Isolated members created through spawn (the seed handoff carries the source's context into an isolated member).
## 2026-11-03 -- SECOND OPINION replaces COMPARE as a way to answer a worker

The COMPARE button, dialog title, overlay, help, manual and docs now read SECOND OPINION; internal ids, routes and the `compare_*` messages keep their names. The dialog asks for a question (optional when messages are selected), shows the selected excerpt, offers two switches that start off (the tab's last three turns, capped near 12 KB, and read-only project file access) and a corrected RUN AS hint: the old "dispatch to the tab's primary" and "spawn a new tab" lines never applied here. Entry points are the composer button and a new SECOND OPINION action in the selection bar from the FORWARD change.

`compare_request` gains `purpose: 'reply-draft'`, `context` and `fileAccess`. `server/src/ws/compareHandlers.ts` clamps the context to 24 KB and builds each leg's task with `buildReplyDraftTask` (draft only the reply text, no commentary); `runCompare` uses the empty tool list unless `fileAccess` is on, then `readOnlyFileTools` (Read, Grep, Glob; no Write, shell, web or MCP). `compare_started` echoes `purpose`, so the overlay says drafts, offers PICK THIS DRAFT and, on the picked and synthesized panes, USE THIS REPLY, which writes the text into the source tab's composer through `applyTidy` (UNDO restores what was there) and sends nothing. Each pick is counted per provider and model in local storage and listed in the dialog with a RESET.

Checked with unit tests for the task builder, tool lists and tally, and the new `second-opinion` browser suite (selection entry, typed-question entry, switches default off, request contents, hint text, blind labels, pick and tally, USE THIS REPLY with undo, nothing sent).

Not checked: real providers drafting replies, how Codex and the local runtimes behave with file access on, and the synthesize leg for reply drafts against a live model.

## 2026-11-03 -- FORWARD selected messages and a fuller SEND TRANSCRIPT

Every chat message now has a small select box (operator, agent and mail rows). While anything is ticked a bar above the composer shows the count with FORWARD, offered on cluster members that have a peer, and CLEAR. FORWARD opens `ForwardDialog` in `client/src/components/ForwardMessages.tsx`: several recipients, an optional covering note placed above the text, and a 32 KB preview of the selected rows in chat order; SEND delivers the same text once per recipient through the existing `operator_peer_send` path, so it stays operator-initiated and outside the model message guard. The row formatter moved to `client/src/utils/transcriptFormat.ts` with the include options; FORWARD uses it with everything on because the selection is explicit.

Checked with unit tests for the include switches and the selection store, and the new `forward-messages` browser suite (select two messages, FORWARD to two members with a note and check the delivered text, SEND TRANSCRIPT recipients, note, switches and the echo default).

## 2026-11-03 -- Other work in the GIT dialog

The GIT dialog has a collapsed SHOW OTHER WORK section. Opening it reads the new `GET /api/git/other-work` (`server/src/gitOtherWork.ts`, routed in `server/src/http/gitWorktreeRoutes.ts`), which lists the repository's linked worktrees and the local branches that are not checked out, leaving out the tab's own tree and the default branch. Each row carries commits ahead of and behind the default branch (`origin/HEAD`, else main or master), the `git status --porcelain` count for checked-out trees, and the open pull request from one `gh pr list` call with an 8 second limit. A missing or signed-out `gh`, or no network, sets `prStatus: 'unavailable'` and the section says only that pull requests are not shown. Rows are capped at 60.

The client matches each worktree to the tabs working there by workflow directory or by an assigned task's worktree. VIEW CHANGES (only on rows with a checkout) calls `requestWorktreeView` in `client/src/utils/worktreeNavigation.ts`: the dialog closes, the right panel opens FILES on CHANGES, and `useWorktreeSelection` selects that worktree in the existing picker once the picker's list contains it. There are no merge, delete or switch actions anywhere in the section.

Checked with `gitOtherWork.test.ts` on a temporary repository (a linked worktree with a dirty file, an ahead and behind branch, a branch only behind, no remote so pull requests are absent, and the survey from inside the linked worktree), the tab-match and hand-off through the new `git-other-work` browser suite (rows, counts, PR link, no-gh note, failure note, read-only buttons, selection hand-off), and the usual gate.
 The worktree path assertion compares resolved real paths so it holds on Windows short and long temporary-folder names.
Not checked: pull requests from a real GitHub remote, very large repositories, and the full dialog to FILES hand-off in a live app (the suite drives the pieces separately). The Tester's git-dialog suite is on another branch and was not extended here.

## 2026-11-02 -- Recover package startup from occupied ports

Package installations now start when the preferred local port is occupied. Setup starts at 3001, searches the remaining range through 3099, and falls back to an operating-system-assigned port. A later start retains the chosen address or moves again if it is occupied. The workbench reports a move, service logs record both ports, and `arco open` and shortcuts use the saved selection; `arco doctor` reports its status and whether an explicit port was set. An already running server for the same home is recognized without starting a second one. Explicit port settings can also move.

The package launcher binds before publishing its selected port, keeps a per-home startup lock, and preserves the workspace key across port changes. The service configuration is updated only after binding, while `healthz` identifies the home by a digest. The notification is local to the workbench and is consumed once. `GETTING_STARTED.md`, `OPERATIONS.md`, `SERVICE_MODE.md`, and the in-app manual describe the package behavior. Source-checkout startup is unchanged.

Checked on Linux with `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:unit` (1,381 passed and one optional scanner skipped), `npm run docs:check`, the tree privacy audit, 23 focused port and service tests, the help-docs browser smoke, and the host-only package install, startup, update, and service smoke. A network-isolated package-mode run checked eleven occupied default ports, same-home duplicate startup, a later port move, and restart persistence. The hosted package smoke covers eleven occupied ports, relocation, launcher routing, and restart persistence on Linux, macOS, and Windows. The rebase retained the Node 24/26 package layout and the existing same-origin upload fix. The first hosted Linux package smoke failed during fixture cleanup because its occupied-port sockets remained open after aborted probes. The fixture now closes accepted sockets, and a local reproduction of that cleanup passes. An Outputs browser check measured a lazy image before it decoded; the check now waits for decoding and still fails if the image request fails.

Remaining: hosted cross-platform results and real macOS and Windows acceptance.
## 2026-11-02 -- Bound Windows service stop verification

Windows package updates now wait for the recorded service process to exit before replacing installed files. A live process still blocks the update and an inspection failure still stops it safely. Older service installations without a process record use a bounded process inspection with one retry. This change takes effect after the release containing it is installed.

The packaged `service-run` writes an Arco-owned process record in its service state, and cleanup removes it only when it still belongs to that process. `updatePackage` captures the record before stopping the service, uses `process.kill(pid, 0)` to check for exit, and bounds the wait. The Windows service stop and fallback CIM checks use bounded PowerShell execution, close their output pipes on timeout, and retry a timeout once. No command line or local path is included in the inspection result.

Checked with the local build, typecheck, `npm run test:unit` (1,381 passed, one optional scanner skipped), privacy audits, and package smoke on Linux. Focused fixture tests cover a delayed process exit, a persistently live process, a missing record, retry and failure of the fallback inspection, replacement of the process record, and a deliberately stalled subprocess. The rebase retains Node 24/26 package support. The hosted Windows package smoke exercises the recorded PID with a synthetic service. A prior combined update-result and stop-wait change failed Windows process inspection because a PowerShell probe timed out; this separate implementation avoids that probe when a service process record is available.

Remaining: hosted three-platform CI, real Windows verification with the operator, and confirmation that older installed packages are updated before relying on the new record.

## 2026-11-03 -- Outputs click to open

Output names and image previews are now clickable. A previewable entry opens the existing viewer; PDF, `.docx`, `.odt` and other types without a preview open in the computer's default app. File-backed entries also get an OPEN button next to Download.

`server/src/http/outputOpen.ts` holds the refused-type list, the per-OS opener command (`xdg-open`, `open`, `rundll32.exe url.dll,FileProtocolHandler`; the path is always one argument, never a command string), the desktop check (hidden in Docker or without DISPLAY/WAYLAND_DISPLAY on Linux) and the launcher. `POST /api/outputs/open` in `outputRoutes.ts` resolves the registered output, requires a loopback peer, a regular file and a non-refused type, logs the open and starts the opener detached. `GET /api/outputs` now also returns `desktopOpen` and `refusedExtensions` so the panel can hide or explain. `OutputsPanel.tsx`, the help entries, `docs/OUTPUTS.md` and the SECURITY note follow. PDF uses the default app because the Outputs file route serves PDF only as an attachment under a sandbox policy.

Checked with unit tests for the opener arguments per OS, the refused list and the route (opener stubbed through `ARCO_OUTPUT_OPEN_STUB`), plus the `outputs-open` browser suite. No real application was launched, and the Windows and macOS openers were not run on real hosts.
## 2026-11-03 -- Stay available: no in-turn waits over 60 seconds

Users directing a cluster or a single workflow now get workers that stay
reachable: agents must not sleep or poll for more than 60 seconds at a time
for builds, test suites or CI. They report what is running and end the turn
so peer mail and the next operator message can arrive.

The director contract in `server/src/clusterDirector.ts` adds stay-available
handoffs (full verification and landing gates to the Tester or a Builder,
CI watching to the change author) and the never-wait-over-60-s rule.
`buildClusterCoordination` adds the same no-wait line for every efficiency
level and role. The TESTER preset in `shared/src/clusterRolePresets.ts`
includes landing-gate verification (≤120 chars) with the prior body mapped
in `normalizeClusterRoleText`. Claude Native turn discipline, Codex
developer instructions (cluster and single), and the Ollama / llama.cpp
primary prompts in `server/src/server/prompts.ts` carry the finishing-is-not-
waiting sentence. Docs and the clusters manual describe the Tester and
director handoff; Unreleased records the change.

Checked with unit tests for director doctrine, coordination no-wait text,
Tester preset normalization and Codex non-cluster instructions, plus
`npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:unit` on the touched suites,
`npm run docs:check` and `npm run audit:public -- --tree`.

## 2026-11-02 -- Keep update messages tied to their attempt

Old update failures no longer appear beside a later update. The update result identifies its attempt, source version, and target, reports a successful target after reconnect, and expires after it has been shown. Dismissed results stay dismissed. Error messages no longer gain repeated punctuation.

`server/src/updateManager.ts`, `scripts/arco-update.mjs`, `scripts/packaging/arco.mjs`, and `scripts/packaging/update.mjs` carry and check attempt metadata so a delayed updater cannot replace a newer result. `UpdateAction` and `UpdateResultNotice` clear status that the server no longer reports. Successful release updates state `Updated to` followed by the target version while retaining the restart and revision details. Existing Windows process inspection and service stopping remain unchanged.

Checked with build, typecheck, `npm run test:unit` (1,375 passed, one optional scanner skipped), documentation and public audits, the update-action and version-box browser smokes, and the package install and update smoke on Linux. Fixtures cover stale failures, new attempts, success after restart, dismissal, and late result writes. Rebasing onto the Node 26 package changes retained both supported-version checks and result tracking. Earlier work that combined this behavior with a Windows process-wait change failed the hosted Windows process inspection; that process-wait work is separate. No provider calls or credentials were used.

Remaining: hosted cross-platform verification of this result-only change and the separate Windows stop investigation. Updates started by an older installed updater retain the old updater behavior.
## 2026-11-02 -- Cursor reply segments and the streaming cursor

Cursor replies no longer run together or leave blinking cursors behind.
Reproduced first with a replayed event sequence (text, reasoning, text, tool
start, text, tool end, text): the second chunk was appended to the first
("fix.While") because reasoning was not a segment boundary, and the reuse
test in the `primary_text` handler re-opened the earlier segment, which is how
an older message regained a cursor.

Changes: `primary_thinking` for Cursor jobs closes the open segment and the
thinking activity row records its event sequence, which the reuse test now
counts as a boundary for Cursor; `appStore.finalizeStreamingExcept` keeps at
most one streaming assistant message per job; the server Cursor worker joins
chunks that follow reasoning or a tool update with a paragraph break
(`providers/cursor/acpSegments.ts`) so the combined turn text stays readable.
The cursor is now a zero-width `::after` on the last text block of an
`.arco-streaming` message. Before, it was an inline block after the markdown,
which sat on a line of its own and added about one line (23.8 px at the
default size) while streaming, the jump the operator saw at the end of a reply.

Checked: client replay test (three cases, including other providers keeping
reasoning inside the segment), server joiner unit test, new `cursor-segments`
browser suite (separate segments, one cursor, pseudo-element drawn, message
height identical with and without the cursor at 27 widths). Not checked: a
live Cursor session; tool blocks ending a reply (last block a code fence or
table) show no cursor until the next text.

## 2026-11-02 -- Same-origin attachment uploads

Users can now drop, paste or attach a file on any Arco port: the composer
posts only to same-origin `/upload`, so a foreign process on port 3001 never
receives the session cookie or the file. When an upload fails, Agent and Chat
composers show a dismissible alert that names the file and the reason (HTTP
status, empty body, size limit or network error) instead of dropping the file
silently.

The behavior is controlled through `client/src/utils/uploadAttachment.ts` and
`uploadFile` in `client/src/hooks/useWebSocket.ts`. `TerminalInput` and
`ChatModeView` surface the failure. Vite continues to proxy `/upload` to
`ARCO_PORT` in development. The `upload-attach` browser suite exercises attach,
paste and drop on port 5227 and a failing upload alert.

Checked with `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:unit` (uploadAttachment cases),
`npm run audit:public`, and `node scripts/smoke/run-browser-suites.mjs
upload-attach`. During implementation, the old client tried `:3001` first and
treated a 401 there as a silent miss; routing every POST through `/upload`
resolved it. Hosted CI must pass on Linux, macOS and Windows.

Remaining: Outputs image layout and contextual-help accuracy land separately.

## 2026-11-02 -- Install native package on Node 24 and 26

A plain npm package install and `arco setup` work with the supported Node.js 24 and 26 majors without compiling native dependencies during installation. `arco setup` and `arco doctor` explain when the Node major is unsupported. Source checkouts still need the normal build tools where terminal bindings must be compiled.

The package uses `better-sqlite3` 13.0.3 and the Node-API `node-pty` binding. `scripts/packaging/collect-native.mjs`, `assemble.mjs`, `native-repair.mjs`, `probe-native.mjs` and `server/src/nativeSqlite.ts` use one platform-and-architecture artifact across both Node majors. Assembly bundles the terminal runtime without installation hooks. Repair uses a verified SQLite binding and the rebuilt terminal binding. CI probes each collected artifact on both Node versions and tests package installation on Linux, macOS and Windows.

Checked locally on Linux with Node 24 and 26: each unit run passed 1,361 tests with one optional scanner skipped. The same SQLite and terminal artifact loaded on both, and plain npm 11 install, `arco setup` and package smoke passed with compiler commands disabled and native-build output rejected. Docker fixture smoke passed. Node 22 setup and doctor reported supported versions. A repair verification initially looked for a packaged manifest in a source checkout; checking repaired files directly confirmed both bindings load. One Node 26 full run timed out waiting for its isolated server; all 15 cases and a complete rerun passed. An initial manual reproduction inherited ambient connection settings and was stopped; subsequent checks used a clean environment. The public audit has a separately pinned Windows SQLite artifact exception for an existing reviewed instruction-byte false positive; it requires Director review. No provider calls or credentials were used.

A hosted Windows Node 24 package smoke failed while running the plain npm 11 install; its original output omitted the failure cause. The smoke now allows five minutes and reports only a bounded process status and recognized error codes. The Node 26 Windows package smoke passed.

Remaining: the corrected hosted three-platform run and the operator's real macOS install check.

## 2026-11-03 -- Recall "summarize on tab close" is wired

The footer checkbox in the Recall view (`recallStore.summarizeOnClose`, default off) saved its value but the tab close handler never read it. `TabBar` now closes through one `closeWorkflow` helper, used by the tab's x and by closing every member of a cluster. When the box is checked it asks `closeSummaryPlan` (`client/src/recall/closeSummary.ts`) whether to summarize: not for Incognito tabs, not for a tab with neither an operator message nor a resumed session. If yes, the tab is removed first and `recall_summary_request` follows, so the close is never delayed and the server uses its transient manager for the already-closed workflow. The summarizer is the tab's own primary provider, model and effort, now shared with the Recall row action through `recallSummarizerFor`.

Toasts: "Summarizing <tab> in Recall" with an OPEN RECALL link (a new optional `action` on notifications), then, from the `recall_summary_ready` handler, a ready toast with the link or an error toast; a closed socket also gives an error toast and changes nothing else. Unchecked, nothing is sent. The RECALL guide, the contextual help and the Recall manual section describe the new behavior and drop the "deferred" notes.

Checked: unit tests for the decision (off, Incognito, no session, resumed session, title fallback, summarizer parity) and the new `recall-close` browser fixture (unchecked close sends nothing; checked close sends exactly one request with the tab's summarizer and shows the toast and link; Incognito and empty tabs send nothing; a failed send only toasts), plus the build, typecheck, unit suite and docs check. Not checked: a real summary after a real close on a running server; the server-side index must already hold the closed session.

## 2026-11-03 -- Codex as utility agent and Smart AUTO-PICKUP evaluator

Codex joins Claude Native, Cursor and Ollama in the shared evaluator adapter list (`server/src/smartPickupAdapters.ts`), so it is offered both under Settings > Agents and clusters > Utility agent and in the Smart AUTO-PICKUP dialog. `UTILITY_AGENT_PROVIDERS` gains `codex`; the suggested model hint is any model id containing `luna`.

The adapter starts a separate `codex -c mcp_servers={} app-server` per request in a fresh temporary folder, removed afterwards. `thread/start` is ephemeral with `sandbox: read-only`, `approvalPolicy: never` and `web_search: disabled`; `turn/start` repeats the read-only policy. Only the last final-answer agent message is returned. Items other than the prompt echo, reasoning and agent messages, and every server request (approval, question, dynamic tool), call `turn/interrupt`, kill the process tree and give no decision. Start-up time, answer time, abort and output cap follow the Cursor adapter. Capability is true only when Codex is enabled in Settings and `codex login status` reports a sign-in.

The workflow worker is untouched except that its `thread/start` object moved into an exported pure function, `buildThreadStartParams`, pinned by a new test so the one-shot lockdown cannot leak into it.

Checked: fake app-server tests for the request shape, the final-answer choice, approval, command, file change, MCP and web items, the output cap, a failed turn, timeout and abort; the unit suite and the settings and help docs. The adapter test compares the process folder with the folder sent to Codex after dropping the macOS `/private` prefix. Not checked: a real `codex` binary (no paid calls) and the exact residual read access of Codex's read-only sandbox, which SECURITY.md states as read access on the machine.

bullet records the documentation update.

## 2026-11-02 -- Release v0.1.2

Third release. It is the in-app update target for 0.1.1 package installs (UPDATE AND RESTART, the version box or `arco update`), and on Windows it is the first update to run through the 0.1.1 service stop that ends the whole launcher and Node process tree. User-visible additions: the utility agent setting with its shared no-tools job runner, BRIEF and BRIEF CLUSTER, CATCH ME UP, TIDY with refinements and revisions, the always-present version box next to the power button, and per-user application shortcuts with `arco open` for an app window. The UPDATING state in the version box now survives other update controls mounting.

Release controls: `scripts/release.mjs patch` bumps the four manifests and the lockfile to 0.1.2, moves Unreleased into the `v0.1.2` section and creates this commit and the annotated tag. `.github/workflows/release.yml` builds the package, attaches `arco-release.tgz` to the GitHub Release and stages the npm publication in the `npm` environment for approval.

Checked: on the release base, `npm ci`, build, typecheck, 1,359 unit tests (one skipped) including the public-audit fixtures, the tree audit with both local pattern files, `docs:check` and all 42 browser suites passed locally. Hosted CI on the feature commits passed verify, browser, Docker, native package and package install jobs on Linux, macOS and Windows; one macOS package-install job on the base was not started by GitHub because of macOS arm64 runner capacity, and every other job passed. The browser jobs now allow 40 minutes, print per-suite durations, and retry a failed suite once on CI, reporting a pass on retry as FLAKY.

Remaining: on real hardware, the Windows Start menu entry and taskbar icon, the macOS Launchpad and Dock entry, and the in-app update from 0.1.1. The utility agent, Smart AUTO-PICKUP evaluator and the jobs built on them accept Claude with Native sign-in, Cursor, Codex and Ollama.

### Added

- Add a utility agent: choose one provider and model for short side jobs in Settings > Agents and clusters (or on the last Welcome screen). It is never chosen for you, TEST checks it, and USE UTILITY AGENT fills it into the Smart AUTO-PICKUP evaluator, Research, Cookbook recommendation, document edit and handoff summarizer choices without changing saved ones. BRIEF, CATCH ME UP and TIDY need a utility agent.
- Add BRIEF on each member card and in the composer toolbar, and BRIEF CLUSTER above the member cards: a short synopsis from the utility agent using facts Arco can see (turn state, current step with the same elapsed time and long-running mark as the working indicator, Queued or Delivered for the last operator message from the same state the transcript shows, recent events, pending question, peer mail, board task, uncommitted files), with the facts listed. It sends nothing to any worker.
- Add CATCH ME UP summaries of workflow and cluster activity since the last visit, written by the utility agent. For a cluster, a member that is not loaded or is Incognito is skipped with a note that its activity is not available, instead of failing the whole request.
- Add TIDY in workflow and Chat composers: the utility agent proposes a clearer draft, with refinements, revision history and undo. ACCEPT only replaces the composer text; TIDY never sends. KEEP ORIGINAL and Escape leave the draft unchanged.
- Add an always-present version box in the top bar, right of the power button: it shows the running version, turns into an accent box with an up arrow and the new version when an update is available (and stays that way after the notice is dismissed), opens the update dialog on click, and shows UPDATING while an update runs.
- Add per-user application shortcuts and `arco open` for a dedicated app window.

### Contributors

- Allow local tree-only privacy audit patterns without applying them to published history.

## 2026-11-02 -- Keep browser navigation assertions platform-aware

The browser smoke runner's navigation preflight now validates the timeout selected for its host. Windows checks the 60 second navigation setting used by its suites; Linux and macOS check 30 seconds. This allows the hosted Windows runner to proceed to its browser acceptance suites.

`scripts/smoke/browser_navigation.test.py` previously expected 30 seconds in a test that runs on every operating system. The Windows job failed before launching browser suites because `navigation_timeout_ms()` correctly returned 60,000. Harness checks run before per-suite retry handling, so this failure did not trigger the CI retry. The assertion now reads the selected timeout from the helper; the separate platform test still explicitly verifies both Windows and POSIX values.

Checked after the correction with `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:unit` (1,324 passed), `npm run audit:public -- --tree`, `npm run docs:check` (6 passed), the Python navigation tests (3 passed), the Node retry tests (4 passed), and the Linux browser runner (42 passed, 0 skipped; 416.2 seconds). The Windows hosted result exposed the platform assumption; the assertion now follows the same function that configures each suite.

Remaining: the updated PR checks must confirm the Windows preflight and browser suites, plus Linux and macOS verification.

---

## 2026-11-02 -- Make browser smoke navigation resilient on Windows CI

Browser acceptance suites no longer wait for the browser's full `load` event before continuing. Navigation uses `domcontentloaded`; each suite keeps its existing app-ready checks. Windows navigation gets a 60 second timeout, while Linux and macOS retain 30 seconds. On hosted CI, the smoke runner retries one failed suite once, labels a recovery `FLAKY`, includes the first failure's output tail, and still fails when the retry fails. Local runs do not retry.

`scripts/smoke/browser_navigation.py` supplies the shared Playwright defaults to browser suites. `scripts/smoke/browser-suite-retry.mjs` owns retry decisions and bounded diagnostic tails; the runner reports flaky suite names in its summary. Dedicated tests cover both helpers.

Checked on the rebased tree with `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:unit` (1,324 passed), `npm run docs:check` (6 passed), `npm run audit:public -- --tree`, the Python navigation tests (3 passed), the Node retry tests (4 passed), and the Linux browser runner (42 passed, 0 skipped; 415.0 seconds). An initial helper draft assumed every fixture page had `#root`; fixture-specific mount points disproved that, so readiness remains with each suite. Earlier aggregate runs met ports held by concurrent local suites; the final complete run passed after those processes exited.

Checked after the following CI timing update landed: the hosted Linux browser job passed 42/42 suites in 804.2 seconds; Windows passed 37/37 with five Linux-only skips in 788.1 seconds. Verify passed on all three operating systems. The macOS browser job was skipped by its existing best-effort pull-request trigger.

Remaining: a target-host macOS and Windows acceptance still requires the operator's machines.

## 2026-11-02 -- Run public-audit fixtures in the unit gate

The standard unit gate now exercises the public-safety audit fixtures whenever contributors run the unit suite. The hosted verify jobs use the same command on Linux, macOS and Windows, so regressions in the tree, staged, package and history scan boundaries are checked before a change is accepted. This does not alter audit rules or the publication scopes.

`scripts/test-unit.mjs` explicitly includes `scripts/public-audit.test.mjs` alongside the source and packaging test files. The existing runner continues to isolate the home directory, temporary storage, Git configuration and provider commands. The audit suite creates temporary Git repositories to check local pattern handling, redacted findings, commit identity, hooks and historical content. Its optional independent scanner case remains skipped unless explicitly enabled; it does not download or run an external scanner during routine verification.

The Linux local gate passed build, typecheck, the full unit suite (1,358 passed and one optional scanner case skipped), documentation checks and the tree and staged public audits. The test runner output identifies the audit cases and reports their results with the rest of the suite. Before this change, a direct invocation passed but `npm run test:unit` did not discover the audit file; including the file in the runner corrects the coverage gap. No paid provider calls or credentials are used. Remaining: hosted verification on the three platforms and Director review.

## 2026-11-02 -- Browser CI reports suite durations

The browser smoke jobs can now run for up to 40 minutes on Ubuntu, macOS, and Windows. Each completed browser suite prints its elapsed seconds beside its pass, fail, or skip status, and the runner prints the total browser-suite time after the summary. This makes a slow suite visible in the Actions log before the job reaches its limit.

The time limits are set on the three browser jobs in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `release.yml` has no browser-suite job. `scripts/smoke/run-browser-suites.mjs` measures each suite from before its startup checks through its server cleanup, including isolated-server suites and occupied-port failures. Skipped Linux-only suites on other platforms also show their short duration. The existing suite selection and pass/fail exit behavior are unchanged.

Checked with `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:unit` (1,323 tests), `npm run audit:public -- --tree`, and the complete Linux browser runner (42 passed, 0 skipped). The runner output included a duration for every suite and a final total of 420.1 seconds. An earlier local run could not start four suites while port 5203 was held by the preceding runner; after it exited, the complete run passed. The originating Ubuntu job exceeded its 20-minute limit while running help coverage; the 40-minute limit addresses that observed failure while duration reporting identifies future slowdowns. No provider calls were used.

Remaining: hosted Ubuntu, macOS, and Windows browser jobs must confirm the longer limits and output format. macOS remains best effort under the current workflow policy.

## 2026-11-02 -- Current-content privacy audit patterns

The public-safety audit accepts an optional second local pattern file for current content. Terms in this file can prevent a recurrence in the working tree, staged changes and packages without making historical commits fail the publication audit. Existing private-pattern matching remains active across all of its existing scopes, and no local pattern values are stored in the repository.

`scripts/public-audit.mjs` reads `.git/private-patterns-tree` using the same case-insensitive, trimmed one-term-per-line format and comment handling as `.git/private-patterns`. An absent file is a no-op. Tree, staged and package content and filenames are checked; history, release history, pre-push and commit messages do not use the second file. The contributor audit guidance identifies the scopes. Git resolves the file for the current worktree, and the existing environment override for the original file remains unchanged.

The audit unit fixture checks a missing file, case and whitespace handling, redacted findings, all three current-content scopes and exclusion from history, release history, push and commit messages. The new test first failed because tree-only patterns were not read; the implementation resolved that failure. A staged assertion initially had no indexed change, and was corrected to stage a distinct fixture. The Linux build, typecheck, unit suite (1,323 passed), documentation checks and tree audit passed. No provider calls or credentials were used. Remaining: hosted Linux, macOS and Windows verification and Director review.

## 2026-11-02 -- BRIEF uses the turn visibility state; cluster Catch me up skips unavailable members

The earlier BRIEF entry said turn visibility was not on its base; it is now on
main, so BRIEF uses it. In `client/src/brief/briefFacts.ts` the "Last operator
message" fact takes Queued or Delivered from the per-turn message state
(`turnMessages`) that the transcript and the queue indicator show, and no
longer derives it from the queue contents; with no held messages it falls back
to the transcript (queued marker, answered, delivered with no reply). "Current
step" now comes from `turnStep` in `client/src/utils/turnStep.ts`, so the step
name, elapsed time and long-running mark (over 60 seconds, replacing BRIEF's
own two-minute threshold) match the working indicator. A cluster Catch me up
(`server/src/http/utilityAgentRoutes.ts`) used to answer 409 for the whole
request when one member was not loaded or was incognito; it now skips that
member and adds a fact "<name>: activity not available" (an incognito member
is listed by its short id, not its name). A workflow-scope request, or a
cluster where every member is unavailable, still answers 409. Unit tests cover
both changes (`briefFacts.test.ts`, `catchUp.test.ts`). Checked on Linux with
the gate and browser suites; Windows and macOS come from the pull request run.

## 2026-11-02 -- Tidy proposals for composer drafts

TIDY offers a clearer proposal for a draft in the workflow and Chat composers. The original remains untouched while the dialog compares it with the editable proposal. ACCEPT replaces the draft; UNDO TIDY restores the original until the draft changes. KEEP ORIGINAL and Escape leave the draft alone. A changed draft is never overwritten by a stale proposal.

The dialog can REFINE a proposal with an additional direction, repeat that action, and use BACK to revisit earlier revisions. Each request includes the original, the current proposal and all refinement directions, including those from a timed-out or cancelled run. Long originals scroll; columns stack on narrow screens. The dialog identifies the chosen utility agent, offers CANCEL while a job is running, shows its returned facts and model, and traps keyboard focus. A draft whose complete refinement material would exceed the input bound is refused rather than silently cut. The no-tools `POST /api/utility-agent/tidy` route records the result as a workflow notice and does not send a message to the worker. The existing runner honors cancellation during provider discovery and ignores late responses.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, 1,307 unit tests, privacy tree audit, documentation checks, help browser coverage, a fixture-provider route test and a browser fixture covering unset agent, two refinements, BACK, editing, acceptance, undo, Escape, timeout, cancel and a long narrow-layout draft. An initial browser check found that Escape did not close the dialog when focus remained outside; modal focus and keyboard handling fixed it. No provider calls were made. Remaining: hosted checks on Linux, macOS and Windows and operator review of the proposal workflow.

## 2026-11-02 -- Catch me up for workflows and clusters

CATCH ME UP summarizes workflow or cluster activity since the previous visit to that tab. The dialog shows the requested time range, the answering utility provider and model, every fact supplied, and a warning if older or lengthy activity was omitted. A new tab without an earlier browser-local visit marker explains that a range will become available after leaving and returning. Nothing runs until SUMMARIZE is selected.

The browser stores separate last-viewed markers for workflow and cluster tabs and records a visit when the tab is left, hidden, or closed. The server reads bounded tails of member workflow event logs and selects transcript, peer mail and board changes for the requested interval. `POST /api/utility-agent/catch-up` uses the existing no-tools runner, writes a result notice to the workflow event log, and exposes CANCEL while work is running. Settings remains the place to choose the utility agent. Incognito workflows are not offered; unavailable cluster member activity is reported instead of guessed. The runner now honors cancellation during provider discovery and discards late answers after cancellation.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, 1,307 unit tests, public tree audit, documentation checks, help browser coverage a real HTTP route test with an in-process provider fixture, and a browser fixture covering unset agent, workflow and cluster markers, success, timeout, cancel and narrow layout. The first unit pass found an unscaled viewport height; the dialog now accounts for interface scale. A browser run overlapped the build and failed to initialize its fixture; repeating it after the build passed. No provider calls were made. Remaining: hosted three-platform checks and operator review of the summary experience.

## 2026-11-02 -- Version box UPDATING state survives other update controls mounting

`UpdateAction` published its local `pending` flag to the shared `updating` flag
in `appUpdateStore.ts` from an effect that also ran on mount, so opening
Settings (which mounts another instance whose `pending` starts false) cleared
the UPDATING state shown by the top bar version box. The effect now publishes only real changes of its own flag, so neither the
first run nor a StrictMode re-run publishes the initial false. This is a bug fix found by review.
The `version-box` browser suite sets updating, mounts the Settings update row
and requires the box to still read UPDATING. Checked on Linux with the build,
typecheck, unit suite and browser suites; Windows and macOS come from the pull
request run.

## 2026-11-02 -- BRIEF and BRIEF CLUSTER

BRIEF sits on each cluster member card and in the composer toolbar (`BriefControl.tsx`); BRIEF CLUSTER sits at the right end of the layout row above the member cards. A click collects facts from client state when pressed (`client/src/brief/briefFacts.ts`, read from the stores in `briefFromStore.ts`): turn state and duration, current step (tool name, first line of its command or path, elapsed time, marked long-running from two minutes), the thinking-with-no-actions case with its duration, the last operator message and whether it is queued, delivered with no reply, or answered, the last six activity events, the last reply, pending question, unread peer mail from the mailbox depth, open board tasks assigned to the worker, and the uncommitted file count when a live changes snapshot is held. A value the client does not hold is reported as "not visible" instead of being inferred. The turn-visibility display work is not on this base, so message state is derived from the queue and transcript only.

`POST /api/utility-agent/brief` takes `{jobId, scope, facts}`, validates and bounds the facts (80 facts, 80 character labels, 600 character values, newlines flattened, 64 KB body) and runs the shared utility job with fixed instructions (`server/src/utilityBrief.ts`): use only the facts, say "not visible" rather than guess, no advice, plain text. The panel shows the answer, the answering provider and model, the elapsed time, a still-working note after four seconds with CANCEL, and the facts under a collapsible list. With no usable utility agent it explains that one must be chosen and links to the Settings section. Nothing is sent to a worker and nothing is stored. Escape and CLOSE dismiss it.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, unit tests for fact collection (step, long-running, thinking, message states, bounds, not-visible, cluster roll-up) and for fact validation, instructions and prompt order, and the new `brief` fixture browser suite (no agent set, success with facts shown, cluster facts, timeout, cancel, 360 px layout). No provider calls were made. Remaining: facts from the turn-visibility change once it lands, and macOS and Windows acceptance.

## 2026-11-02 -- Utility agent setting and shared job runner

Settings > Agents and clusters has a new Utility agent section (`UtilityAgentPicker.tsx`), and the last Welcome screen has the same picker in a collapsed optional block. Only providers that are enabled, connected and able to answer without tools are offered: Claude, Cursor and Ollama; Codex is excluded because it cannot be stopped from using tools. The picker never preselects anything. A suggestion line offers a Claude model containing "haiku" or the Cursor `auto` model through a CHOOSE IT button; SAVE writes `settings.utilityAgent` (`{provider, model}`, validated and bounded in `server/src/settings.ts`, `null` clears, an invalid patch keeps the earlier value). TEST sends one fixed request, shows a still-working note after four seconds, and has CANCEL.

`server/src/utilityJob.ts` is the shared runner for utility jobs, built on the Smart AUTO-PICKUP adapters (`freeText` option added so the Ollama request carries no JSON schema). A job gets a cap of 24,000 input characters (facts first, each cut to 400, material trimmed from its start), a 60 second answer timeout with the adapters' existing start-up allowance, a cancel registry keyed by job id, and returns status (`ok`, `not_set`, `timeout`, `cancelled`, `error`), text, the answering provider and model, the fact list and elapsed time. Provider error text is never passed on. Routes: `GET /api/utility-agent/providers`, `POST /api/utility-agent/test` and `/cancel`.

USE UTILITY AGENT appears, only while a utility agent is saved and its provider is connected, in the shared RUN AS picker used by Research, Cookbook recommendation, document edit and the Settings handoff summarizer, and in the Smart AUTO-PICKUP evaluator. It fills the choice in and changes nothing saved by itself. Not yet wired from the consumer inventory: Recall summaries, TUNE MD, knowledge distill, Genesis assist, reviewer, the Rotate Session dialog, Ollama compaction and session titles. Those have no agent picker today and stay on their current behavior.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, 1,295 unit tests including runner coverage (prompt bounds, not set, success, timeout, cancel, unavailable, empty answer) and the settings key, help coverage, and the `utility-agent`, first-run, Connections and help-coverage browser suites, including the utility fixture (unset by default, suggestion not applied, save, success, timeout, slow start with cancel, unavailable provider note, 340 px layout, clear, fill-in). The rebase onto the current release required updating the Settings help-entry count; the first browser run overlapped the build and was repeated after it finished. The hosted history scan included the concurrently published launcher branch; the exact reviewed hashes for its SVG-derived icons are included in the audit. A local all-ref history scan still reports pre-existing identity findings in unrelated local refs; the hosted scan checks the published refs. No provider calls were made. Remaining: the unwired consumers above, and macOS and Windows acceptance.

## 2026-11-02 -- Application shortcuts and app windows

Packaged installations can create an Arco application shortcut during `arco setup` and open the workbench in a dedicated Chromium app window. The shortcut is per user and uses a separate browser profile under the Arco home, keeping microphone permission in that profile. If a supported browser is unavailable, the launcher reports its fallback to a normal browser tab.

`arco open` starts a stopped packaged service and waits for its health endpoint; `arco open --tab` uses the default browser. `arco shortcuts add` and `arco shortcuts remove` manage the Start menu entry, Applications bundle, or Linux desktop entry. `arco service uninstall` also removes an unchanged owned shortcut. Modified shortcuts are retained for review. The web manifest and icon files derive from the existing Arco mark; a browser or desktop can still show the browser icon on the taskbar or Dock. Package uninstall instructions, service documentation, and the in-app manual describe the new commands.

Checked on Linux with the build, typecheck, 1,294 unit tests, launcher fixtures, documentation checks, public tree audit and the host-only package install smoke. The package smoke creates and removes a shortcut in a temporary location and verifies a session cookie against the app-window localhost origin. The first package smoke used npm 11 and stopped at its npm 12 prerequisite; rerunning with npm 12 passed. Hosted Windows verification found two fixtures assumed Unix path separators; the fixture paths now follow the host and the app-window test uses an Edge fixture on Windows. Raster icon audit exceptions are restricted to the exact generated files and hashes.

Remaining: hosted package and browser checks on macOS and Windows, and operator checks of Start menu, Launchpad, taskbar and Dock presentation on real hardware.

## 2026-11-02 -- Version box in the top bar

After DISMISS on the update notice nothing in the interface showed that an update
was still waiting. A new `HeaderVersionBox` (`client/src/components/HeaderVersionBox.tsx`)
sits right of the power button in `SystemHeader.tsx` and is always present,
using the shared header button style. Up to date it is muted and shows the
running version (`v0.1.1`), with a tooltip giving the last-checked time. When
the release check or the live notice reports a newer release it turns into an
accent box with an up arrow, the new version and a dot, and stays that way
until Arco is on the latest release; DISMISS only hides the large notice, which
now says the update stays available from the box. While an update runs it reads
UPDATING (a new `updating` flag in `appUpdateStore.ts`, set by `UpdateAction`).
With release checks off, by setting or `ARCO_UPDATE_CHECK=0`, it shows the
muted version and says so. Clicking with an update available opens the existing
Confirm update dialog through `UpdateAction` (new `autoConfirm` prop), or the
copyable command for installs that cannot update themselves; otherwise a popover
shows the running and latest known version, last check, CHECK NOW, a release
notes link and a link to Settings > Advanced > CLI and runtime versions. The
box has an aria-label, a `header.version` help entry and a manual paragraph.
New browser suite `version-box` covers up to date, update available, still
visible after DISMISS and reload, the confirm dialog, the popover and checks
off. Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, unit, docs and the browser suites;
Windows and macOS come from the pull request run. Not checked: very narrow
widths of the top bar.

## 2026-11-02 -- Release v0.1.1

Second release and the first through the full release pipeline. It is the update target for 0.1.0 package installs (`arco update` or UPDATE AND RESTART) and a fresh-install target on Windows. User-visible changes: messages sent mid-turn show Queued or Delivered and the working indicator names the current step with its elapsed time; cluster coordination treats silence as approval of a stated next step, send results show the recipient's activity, and status-only director messages are held once for confirmation; new homes enable only signed-in providers and detected local runtimes; Claude status follows the selected transport and the Claude sign-ins are grouped in Settings; project locations that are Git repositories list themselves and their child folders; the usage dropdowns are reduced to the provider name and usage bars; `llama-server` is found on PATH; a package uninstall path is documented. On Windows the background service runs without a console window through `wscript.exe` and the `service/launch.js` launcher in the Arco home, its output goes to log files shown by `arco service logs`, and stop, restart and uninstall end the whole service process tree.

Release controls: `scripts/release.mjs patch` bumps the four manifests and the lockfile to 0.1.1, moves Unreleased into the `v0.1.1` section and creates this commit and the annotated tag. `.github/workflows/release.yml` builds the native archives, attaches `arco-release.tgz` to the GitHub Release, and the npm job stages the package through trusted publishing in the `npm` environment for approval on the registry.

Checked: on the release base, `npm ci`, build, typecheck, 1289 unit tests, the tree audit, `docs:check` and all 37 browser suites passed locally; hosted CI passed verify, package install and browser suites on Linux and Windows, verify and package install on macOS, all six native package builds and the Docker smoke. Two Windows defects were found by hosted CI before the tag: `conhost.exe --headless` started no child process, and ending the task left Node running; both were fixed and proven on the Windows runner.

Remaining: on a real Windows desktop, confirm that no window appears, and exercise restart and uninstall through Task Scheduler; the first trusted-publisher run and the registry approval.

### Added

- Document a complete package uninstall path (service removal, npm uninstall, optional permanent data and checkpoint cleanup) in Operations, with a README link and in-app manual entry.
- Show mid-turn message delivery and the current timed working step in workflows and cluster cards.

### Changed

- Cluster coordination treats silence as approval of a stated next step and asks continuing reports to name that step.
- Peer send results and mailbox inspection show recipient activity, current task and latest reply to the sender.
- Director status-only peer messages ask for confirmation before delivery; reports and actionable messages continue normally.
- New homes enable detected provider sign-ins and local runtimes; Strata and DeepSeek through Xolotl start off. Existing provider choices remain intact.
- The usage dropdowns on the provider dots are minimal: the provider name, then the usage bars with percent and reset time (Claude Session and Weekly, Codex its usage lines, Cursor what its source provides), with one small refresh button. Sign-in and account detail stays in Connections and Settings. "Allowance" wording is now "usage limits".

### Fixed

- Project locations that are Git repositories now show the repository and its child folders; workflow names use the selected project display name.
- Windows: the background service no longer leaves a console window open on the desktop. The task now starts Node through a hidden script host launcher, raw output is kept in log files under the Arco home, `arco service logs` shows them, and `arco update` and UPDATE AND RESTART move an older install to the new task definition. Stopping or restarting the Windows service now also ends the Node process, so the port is freed.
- Claude: Welcome and Connections now report Claude as connected only when the selected transport can be used. With Native (the default) selected and no Native credentials, Claude reads "Native: sign-in needed" with SIGN IN FOR NATIVE in place and "Use Claude CLI instead" when the CLI is signed in, and Continue waits for a usable sign-in.
- Settings > Providers and models groups the Claude sign-ins together (Native first, then CLI) ahead of the other accounts and local endpoints, and the link from Connections lands on that group and highlights it.
- Repeated ADD FRESH batches with the same name continue numbering after existing cluster members.
- Fresh service installation on Windows and macOS no longer prints the expected missing-service probe as an error.
- llama.cpp: `llama-server` is now found on PATH on every platform (`llama-server.exe` on Windows) instead of a fixed Linux path, `ARCO_LLAMACPP_BIN` still overrides it, and the not-found message says where to look.

## 2026-11-02 -- Windows service stop now ends the Node process, not only the launcher

With the script host launcher, the scheduled task's process is `wscript.exe`
and Node is its child. A probe on the hosted Windows runner showed that
`taskkill /PID <wscript> /F` leaves Node running, and that registering the real
task definition, running it and ending it with `schtasks /End` also left Node
alive. Left alone, `arco service stop` would not stop Arco, a restart would hit
the held port, and `arco update` would refuse because Arco processes were still
running. `serviceAction('stop')` in `scripts/packaging/services.mjs`, which
`restart` and `uninstall` also use, now ends the task and then runs a short
PowerShell step that selects `wscript.exe` and `node.exe` processes whose
command line contains this home's `service/config.json` and ends each with
`taskkill /T /F`. The marker is the per-home configuration path, so another
home's Arco and a source-checkout service are not matched. The update guard is
unchanged and stays the final check. A unit test covers the order and the
failure path. `scripts/packaging/pack-smoke.mjs` starts a stand-in service
through the written launcher, kills only the launcher as `/End` does, runs the
real stop code, and requires both processes gone and the port free. Checked on
Linux with the unit suite and gate; the Windows result comes from the hosted
pull request run. Not verified: a real Windows desktop.

---

## 2026-11-02 -- Windowless Windows service moved from conhost to a script host launcher

Hosted CI on the windowless Windows service change failed two Windows jobs, and
the second showed the launcher itself was wrong. The package update unit test
(`scripts/packaging/update.test.mjs`) expected npm calls `view` and `install`;
on win32 the update also runs `arco service refresh` in a fresh process, so the
expectation is now platform-aware and asserts that third `service refresh`
call. The pack-install smoke (`scripts/packaging/pack-smoke.mjs`) reported that
`conhost.exe --headless node` wrote no marker. A throwaway probe workflow on the
Windows runner then tried seven variants (piped and ignored stdio, quoted and
verbatim command lines, `cmd /c`, no `--headless`, a bare `node`): conhost
exited with status 0 and started no child in every one, even after waiting.
The same probe showed `wscript.exe //B //Nologo launch.js <node> <script>` and
PowerShell `-WindowStyle Hidden` both run node, including from a path with
spaces. The task now runs `wscript.exe` (a GUI-subsystem program, so no console
window) with a JScript launcher, `service/launch.js` in the Arco home, that
runs node hidden and waits. `services.mjs` writes it at install and at
`arco service refresh`, and removes it on uninstall; JScript is used because
VBScript is being retired. The smoke now runs the written launcher against a
spaced path and requires the marker. Checked on Linux: build, typecheck, unit
and audits. Not verified: that no window appears on a real desktop, and task
stop kills the whole tree; both need a real Windows host.

## 2026-11-02 -- Package uninstall procedure in Operations

Users can now follow a complete removal path for a packaged native install on
Linux, macOS, and Windows. The steps cover stopping the per-user service,
removing the npm package, optionally deleting Arco data with an explicit
permanent-loss warning, and stating what remains installed.

The procedure lives in `docs/OPERATIONS.md` under Uninstall and is linked from
the README Install section. Commands are `arco service uninstall` and
`npm uninstall -g @ghreprimand/arco`. Optional deletion covers the Arco home
(`~/.arco`, `%USERPROFILE%\.arco`, or `ARCO_HOME`), a separate `ARCO_VOICE_HOME`
when set outside that home, per-project `.arco/` folders, and Git refs matching
`refs/arco/*` via `git for-each-ref` and `git update-ref -d`, with PowerShell
forms for Windows. Service paths match `scripts/packaging/services.mjs`
(systemd `arco.service`, LaunchAgent `arco-workbench.plist`, Task Scheduler
task and `service/task.xml`). Checkpoint ref cleanup matches
`server/src/workflows/purgeWorkflow.ts`. The in-app manual entry
`arco-uninstall` points at the same guide. Node.js and provider CLIs are left
installed.

Checked with `npm run docs:check`, `npm run test:docs`, `npm run audit:public
-- --tree`, and the help-docs and help-coverage browser suites. No provider
calls were made. Remaining: physical-host confirmation that the documented
paths match a real package install on macOS and Windows.

The Claude dot's dropdown (`UsageBar.tsx`) listed Native and CLI sign-in text, allowance meter, source, account and freshness lines, a checked time, a disclaimer and a CHECK ALLOWANCE NOW button above the Session and Weekly bars. It now shows a Claude title row with one small refresh button (`UsageTitle`, aria-label "Refresh Claude usage limits", aria-disabled while checking so focus is kept), then the Session and Weekly bars, or the meter's own message when there is no data. A stale snapshot with bars still shows its bars; no wording about it. The Cursor dropdown shows a Cursor title, the included gauge and the token total with the overage note; the polled-not-live header, the in/out breakdown and the dashboard link are gone (the dot still opens the usage page). Tooltips use border-box with a width capped to the viewport so they fit at 360 px.

User-facing "Allowance" became "usage limits" in the dot title, header help, the Connections help, `docs/OPERATIONS.md` (section now "Claude usage limits"), `docs/PROVIDERS.md` and `docs/HTTP_API.md`; internal names (`claudeAllowance.ts`, the suite name) are unchanged. No data was removed from `/api/usage`.

Checked with updated `claudeAllowance`/`claudeAuthStatus` unit tests, a new `usageDropdowns.test.tsx` (Codex and Cursor titles, one refresh button, no removed text), docs check, and the `claude-allowance` and `claude-auth-status` browser suites (title and bars, one button, fit at 1280 and 360 px, refresh fencing unchanged). ## 2026-11-02 -- Windows service runs without a console window

The Windows logon task `arco-workbench` ran `node.exe` directly (`<Exec><Command>` in `scripts/packaging/services.mjs`), so a console window stayed open on the desktop streaming the server log and git warnings. `renderService('win32')` now registers `C:\Windows\System32\conhost.exe --headless <node> <arco.mjs> service-run <config>` (path from `SystemRoot`, every argument quoted). Options weighed: `conhost.exe --headless` gives the process a console with no window and no flash and has no dependency on other components; a wscript plus generated .vbs launcher depends on Windows Script Host, which VBScript's retirement from Windows 11 puts at risk, and adds a file to keep in sync; PowerShell `-WindowStyle Hidden` flashes a window; an S4U or "run whether logged on or not" principal changes the profile and desktop the providers see. Ending the task still ends the process tree, so stop and restart behave as before.

With no console, `service-run` on Windows now redirects `process.stdout` and `process.stderr` into `service.stdout.log` and `service.stderr.log` in the log directory (`ARCO_LOG_DIR` or `<home>/logs`), 5 MiB each with one rotated `.1` copy (`service-logs.mjs`), next to the structured `arco.log` the server already writes. `arco service logs` prints `arco.log` and, on Windows, any non-empty raw file under headers.

Existing installs: `refreshServiceDefinition()` (`arco service refresh`) rewrites an Arco-owned task only when the stored hash still matches the file, registers the new XML from a staged copy first and changes the file and hash only after `schtasks /Create /F` succeeds; a modified definition is never touched. `arco update` and UPDATE AND RESTART run it in a fresh process (the new code) between install and start, tolerating failure with a note; `service-run` also calls it at start so a 0.1.0 install updated by the old updater is moved too, effective at the next start (the first start after that update can still show the window once).

Checked with unit tests for the generated XML, refresh (rewrite, current, modified, failed registration, no service), stdout/stderr capture with rotation and the logs report, and the update ordering (stop, refresh, start; Linux unchanged); `npm run smoke:package` gained a Windows step that runs `conhost.exe --headless node` and checks the definition. Not run on a real Windows host: that no window appears, and end-to-end task start, are unverified.

## 2026-11-02 -- Repository locations show their child folders

Project locations that are Git repositories now show the repository itself and its non-hidden child folders in Home and the creator. A location can therefore contain other projects without hiding them merely because the parent has a Git marker. The default Docker single-repository mount remains a single project, keeping its source directories out of the project list. The workflow name defaults to the selected project's display name, including on Windows paths, and an explicitly edited name survives later folder selections.

`scanFolderProjects` in `server/src/projects.ts` includes the repository and safe immediate children, and treats Git directory and file markers alike. The container mount exception applies only to the Docker project root. `ProjectPicker` supplies its existing project list to the creator, where `projectNameForDirectory` resolves display names and recognizes Windows and POSIX separators. The Home and creator help and `docs/GETTING_STARTED.md` explain the behavior.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, unit (1,271 passed) and documentation suites, public audit, the full browser runner and the project-roots browser fixture. The fixture creates a synthetic repository location and checks Home, creator choices, its default name and a retained edit. The focused scan test initially failed by returning only the parent, then passed with child discovery; unit cases also cover the isolated Docker root and Windows path naming. Occupied browser ports required alternate-port reruns; the interrupt fixture also needed its preview URL set explicitly. Real Windows and macOS acceptance remains open.

## 2026-11-02 -- Detect providers for a new home

New Arco homes now enable Claude, Codex and Cursor when their existing sign-in is detected, and Ollama, LM Studio and llama.cpp when their endpoint or executable is detected. Strata and DeepSeek through Xolotl start disabled. An installed runtime may still require a running server or a model. Existing settings files, saved provider switches and homes with saved projects or chats keep the previous availability behavior.

At startup `initializeProviderSettings` writes the complete initial `enabledProviders` map only when no prior settings or saved-work markers exist. `initialProviders.ts` reuses connection-status probes and checks local executables on PATH. Connections permits a disabled provider to be checked or signed in; a successful explicit check enables it, while a failed check leaves it disabled. A completed CLI login triggers that check, and a verified Native login from Settings or Connections does the same; choosing an already signed-in CLI enables Claude as well. Routine status refresh and workflow recovery do not change provider switches. Settings help, Connections help, the manual and `docs/SETTINGS.md` describe the behavior.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, the isolated unit suite (1,273 passed), documentation checks, the public tree audit, the complete browser runner before rebasing, and the affected first-run and Connections fixtures after rebasing. The provider fixture checks a Claude-only home, local detection, old settings without a provider map, explicit saved values and unsuccessful checks. An initial build used an unavailable status field and was corrected; a recovery fixture needed the new explicit check route, while workflow-bound recovery was kept unchanged. A later rebase required the in-place Native sign-in to remain usable while Claude is disabled. The default browser port was occupied for eight suites, which were rerun on an isolated alternate port. Real macOS and Windows acceptance remains open.

## 2026-11-01 -- Confirm status-only director messages

A director sending a status-only message to another cluster member now receives a confirmation note before delivery. The note asks for the same message to be sent again with `confirm: true`; the confirmed body is delivered unchanged. Peer reports and operator-originated messages do not use this check. Questions, assigned task IDs, the documented instruction verbs and attached artifacts bypass it, keeping actionable messages flowing.

The shared `peerSend` operation applies the deterministic word check before deduplication and budget accounting, and logs a held attempt as `status_confirmation`. The held attempt consumes no message budget. A confirmed status resend can proceed even when the normal per-turn limit is full; a successful delivery is counted. Native and MCP `peer_send` expose the optional `confirm` argument through their respective schemas and the cluster HTTP route. No existing message body or recipient is rewritten. The Unreleased changelog records the behavior.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, the unit suite (1260 passed), docs checks, the public tree audit and the cluster-coordination browser smoke. The focused coordination tests cover the held status example, unchanged confirmed delivery, full budgets, questions, task IDs, artifacts, actionable rebase instructions, peer reports and operator sends. An overlapping unit run was terminated; rerunning the complete suite alone passed. No provider calls or credential copies were used. Review, landing and hosted cross-platform verification remain open.

## 2026-11-01 -- Recipient context on peer sends

A peer send now returns the recipient's current turn state, active board task and most recent message to the sender, including the reply's first line and age. The MAILBOX inspection dialog shows the same context for each pending message. The context is informational: it never prevents a send or alters delivery, and unavailable live state is reported as unknown.

The shared peer-send operation builds the context from the live workflow registry, the cluster's selected task board, the sender's pending mailbox and its received-message events. Both the Native tool and MCP route return the result. The mailbox peek route enriches each pending row, and the mailbox modal displays the facts beside the existing message and cancellation controls. Restricted members do not gain board access. The mailbox help entry and the Unreleased changelog describe the added information.

Checked on Linux with build, typecheck, the unit suite (1259 passed), docs checks, the public tree audit and the cluster-mail-visibility browser fixture, including the new mailbox context row. A targeted 14-test run additionally exercised the shared context builder and send result. The first browser invocation found its usual port occupied; an isolated Vite instance on another port passed the same fixture. No provider calls or credentials were used. Review, landing and hosted cross-platform verification remain open.

## 2026-11-01 -- Stated next steps need no approval message

Cluster coordination now tells members that silence approves a next step already stated in a report. A reply is needed to change that step, answer a question, or assign other work. Reports that continue without a reply end by naming the next step. This reduces status-only follow-ups while retaining explicit communication for changes and questions.

The guidance is added once in `buildClusterCoordination`, beside the existing send check, and is included for Director and Implementer roles at Thorough, Balanced and Lean levels. It does not change peer delivery, message guards, task-board status, or the operator's controls. Existing coordination settings and saved roles remain in place. `CHANGELOG.md` records the changed guidance under Unreleased.

Checked on Linux with `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:unit` (1258 passed), `npm run docs:check`, and `npm run audit:public -- --tree`. The cluster coordination test checks the exact text appears once for both roles at all three levels and follows the existing send check. No implementation or verification failures were encountered. The change is held for the planned release sequence; review, landing, and hosted cross-platform checks remain open.

A message sent during an active turn now shows Queued until a supported delivery boundary is confirmed, then Delivered until the next agent text or the turn ends. The working indicator and cluster member card show the current tool, the first line of a shell command, elapsed time, and a long-running mark after 60 seconds. Queued follow-ups retain their existing delivery order and cancellation controls.

The inventory found that `QueuedMessagesIndicator` and `TerminalInput` already showed queued steer and follow-up chips for every provider, while `ChatMessage` hid queued bubbles in the normal transcript projection. `WorkflowView` and `MemberPane` shared that composer. Activity panes already listed tool names and inputs, but `PrimaryStatus` discarded the tool label after six seconds and `MemberCard` lacked a timer. Claude Native, Ollama, llama.cpp and LM Studio drain at their input boundaries; Codex app-server can requeue after a failed steer, so its delivery signal now follows the successful RPC. Other transports do not claim mid-turn delivery. No queue or provider scheduling behavior changed.

A reconnect test passed an array where the snapshot expects a worker map; typecheck caught it and the fixture was corrected.
